# v0.6 Alpha Release Swarm — Risks and Open Questions

> **Consolidated future-epic scoping** for the three architecture-level gaps
> tracked below (items 1, 17, 18) lives in a single cross-referenced writeup
> at `docs/release/v0.6/future-epic-scoping.md` — read that first for the
> operator-facing picture before diving into the chronological detail here.

## Operator directive: fully autonomous, operatorless operation (2026-07-23)

The operator instructed the lead directly: keep pushing into the remaining backlog, make judgment calls without pausing for operator confirmation, and do not stall waiting for operator input — this is intended to run unattended for multiple days ("don't want to come back after 4 days and find out you have been stalled for 3.75 days"). Effective immediately:

- Any item previously marked "needs operator sign-off" or "operator's call" in this document that does NOT require merge/publish authority is resolved by the lead's own best judgment below, documented with reasoning, rather than left open pending a response that may not come for days.
- Teammates are instructed not to wait for lead check-in between queued tasks — they work down their assigned list, message the lead only for genuine blockers or completions worth recording, and otherwise keep moving.
- The lead maintains a standing backlog queue at all times so no teammate goes idle without a next task already assigned.
- A recurring cron check-in (~every 20 min, session-only, 7-day auto-expiry) re-prompts the lead to verify no teammate has gone idle unassigned and to keep dispatching real work, as a backstop against the conversational turn-based flow ever going quiet.
- Merge-to-`develop` authority and any genuinely irreversible/high-blast-radius decision remain the operator's alone — autonomy here is about not stalling on ordinary engineering judgment calls, not about self-authorizing the closure handshake.

## Operator override: wired-integration ceremony waiver (2026-07-23)

Per `docs/governance/no-stub-mvp-doctrine.md` (`scope: universal`), the four-check audit criterion is "not optional and not waivable except by explicit operator override recorded in `risks-and-open-questions.md`." This file records that override for the v0.6 alpha release swarm, approved by the operator (Todd Hintzmann) via the launch-readiness revision of 2026-07-23:

- **Waived:** the per-cycle receipt *ceremony only* — no cycle receipts under `programs/codex/requirements/`, no closure-epilogue doctrine, no per-bundle kanban board. The swarm's audit evidence lands in `docs/release/v0.6/SWARM_REPORT.md` instead (raw grep output pasted per §7.1 of `release-swarm.md`).
- **NOT waived:** the four-check audit itself (forbidden tokens, no-op handlers, mock-library leaks, "Would …" strings). It runs against the swarm's combined diff (`git diff develop...tranche/6`) before the closure PR opens, executed by the QA engineer and re-verified by the lead. There is no in-repo runner script; the four greps are run by hand per the doctrine's "Per-cycle audit" section.

## Risks

1. **Alpha-bar distance — SHARPENED, then SCOPED.** Current state (`docs/architecture/status.md`, 2026-07-23): only single-class Fighter 1–3 reaches a `Computed` receipt; 7 of 10 CharacterSheet tabs stubbed; Feat picker and Money panel do not exist; level-up/skill/bio persistence are no-ops. The §1 bar (6-level multiclass, four books, PCGen parity, zero stubs) requires multiple task waves; the go/no-go checkpoints in §6 are the containment mechanism. **Update (backend, task 2 delivery):** the compute engine's `Computed` path is narrower than "Fighter 1–3" implied — it currently only computes for one exact hardcoded posture. Any other skill/equipment choice returns `Blocked` with diagnostics rather than a receipt — pre-existing engine narrowness, not something the swarm introduced, but it means most tester choices through the newly-wired UI will hit Blocked, not Computed, until this is widened.

   **Full scoping (backend proposal, 2026-07-23, greenlit in part):** the narrowness is actually 4 independent exactness gates in `pilot_compute.rs`, each class-agnostic except one sub-check, any deviation trips `Blocked` with no partial credit:
   1. `unmet_combat_posture_conditions` — exact Longsword+Chain Shirt, no shield, Power Attack selected-inactive, Dodge+Weapon Focus feats (one Fighter-only sub-check).
   2. `unmet_selected_skill_posture_conditions` — exactly Climb/Intimidate/Swim rank 1, chain shirt equipped, nothing else.
   3. A Human-Wizard-only spellbook posture gate (level ≤3, canonical Evocation specialization, exact prepared spellbook) — newly found, wasn't previously documented, fires independently.
   4. AC specifically: `baseline_armor_class` (the gate) never calls `equipment_effects.rs`'s `compute_equipment_effects`, which already computes *real, correct* per-item AC deltas for arbitrary gear and is already wired into the separate desktop-UI equipment tab. The correct math exists and is simply disconnected from the gate.

   **Sizing by pillar (backend's original proposal):** AC — small-medium, a real wiring job (initially greenlit, scoped to Fighter/Wizard/Rogue: splice the existing correct math into the gate). Attack bonus — large, needs genuinely new equipment-driven to-hit math that doesn't exist (feats/enhancement bonuses only today). Skills — medium-large: `allocate_skill_ranks` only maps 5 skills, class-skill recognition is Fighter-only, and has no armor-check-penalty term at all (explicitly deferred in its own doc comment) — not a drop-in fix even for the 5 skills it knows. Wizard spellbook gate — separate, untouched by the above.

   **AC SLICE DROPPED — the real finding is architectural, not per-pillar (backend, mid-implementation, 2026-07-23):** the original AC sizing assumed the correct per-item math could be spliced directly into the gate. It can't, cheaply. `compute_combat_baseline`/`baseline_armor_class` (the thing that gates `Computed`/`Blocked`) lives in the **headless** compute layer — `build_pilot_headless_receipt`/`compute_pilot_base_chassis` take a `CharacterInput` only, **no corpus parameter**, by design. Real per-item AC values only exist via `equipment_effects::arms_armor::compute_arms_armor_effect`, which needs a corpus-resolved `EquipmentRecord` — corpus access the headless layer structurally doesn't have. The static, corpus-free `equipment_tables()` carries no AC-bonus field at all. Bridging this means either (a) threading a corpus parameter through the entire headless chassis call chain — ~347 call-site grep hits, a blast radius comparable to or bigger than task 4's Rogue widening — or (b) moving the `Computed`/`Blocked` decision itself to the corpus-aware layer (`pilot_compute_corpus.rs`'s `compute_pilot_with_corpus`, which already has both), a real architecture decision about which layer owns claim-gating, not an implementation detail. Backend caught this *before* writing the change and stopped to ask rather than push forward on unscoped work or fabricate a corpus-free approximation — correct call, no code written.

   **Decision:** dropped for this swarm. Option 1 (leave the gate alone, since the corpus-aware desktop equipment tab already shows real AC for arbitrary gear today — that's existing behavior, not new work) is close to a no-op and not worth landing on its own. Option 2 (the corpus-threading/layer-ownership change) is a genuine future epic, not a wave-2 item — **flagging for the operator**: this likely isn't just an AC problem. If attack-bonus and general-skill widening (already sized large/medium-large above) hit the same headless/corpus-aware split, bridging that one architecture boundary might be the actual unlock for all three posture-narrowness pillars at once, rather than three independent slices. Worth a dedicated scoping pass before any future wave attempts AC, attack-bonus, or skill-posture widening again. Backend proceeding instead to items 2-4 (durability, money-conversion PCGen verification, comparator field-extraction), none of which share this problem.

   **Follow-up scoping pass, answered precisely, split by dimension (backend, 2026-07-24, read-only, no code).** Confirms the hypothesis for two of the three remaining pillars, refutes it for the third:
   - **Attack-bonus widening: YES, same wall, confirmed exactly.** Real to-hit for arbitrary weapons needs `equipment_effects/equipmods.rs`'s `compute_equipmods_effect`, which reads a weapon's magic enhancement bonus (`WeaponEnhancementBonus`, the corpus's `BONUS:WEAPON|TOHIT|...|TYPE=Enhancement` token) off a corpus-resolved `EquipmentRecord` — same structural requirement as AC's `compute_arms_armor_effect`. The static, corpus-free `equipment_tables()` table carries nothing enhancement-related.
   - **Skill-posture widening splits into two genuinely different sub-problems.** (1) Armor-check-penalty for arbitrary armor: **YES, same wall** — the real corpus record's `ACCHECK:` token exists (verified against `Leather Armor (Base)`, `ACCHECK:0`) but the static `equipment_tables()` doesn't carry it. (2) Class-skill-list recognition (which skills are class skills for Wizard/Rogue/Cleric/etc., beyond the already-fixed Fighter/Rogue case): **NO, this is NOT corpus-blocked** — it's a labor-volume problem, not an architecture wall. This data isn't in any static table today, but it doesn't need corpus access to get there: it can be hand-authored as literal per-class skill lists, exactly the existing `GROUNDED_FIGHTER_CLASS_SKILLS` precedent already used in `skill_allocation.rs`.
   - **Bottom line for the future-epic writeup:** bridging the headless/corpus-aware boundary would genuinely unlock both AC-shaped gaps (attack-bonus enhancement, skill ACP) at once — confirms the hypothesis for those two dimensions specifically. It would **not**, by itself, close the class-skill-list gap, which needs separate hand-authored per-class data regardless of which layer owns the gate. Both halves stated explicitly so a future scoping pass doesn't overstate what the architecture fix alone would buy.

   **NEW — Wizard spellbook gate is unreachable, not just narrow (frontend, 2026-07-23).** Gate 3 above turns out to be categorically worse than gates 1/2: it isn't merely one exact combination out of many, it's **structurally impossible to satisfy through any shipped UI path**. `compose_character_input` (the fixed-loadout composer every UI-created character goes through) never seeds the required `choice:wizard_school_specialization -> school:evocation` plus opposed-Necromancy/Transmutation choices for any class, and `CreateCharacterForm.tsx` has no arcane-school field at all. Verified live two independent ways — fresh Wizard creation, and multiclassing Wizard onto an existing character via level-up — both correctly `Blocked` with the real diagnostic, neither persisted (reject-don't-persist invariant held). This directly blocks bar item 3's "select spells at each spell-gaining level" for the entire class most associated with spellcasting, and is likely the deeper reason `status.md`'s "only Fighter reaches Computed" framing has held architecture-wide.

   **Decision:** seed the required choices as a hardcoded default in `compose_character_input`, mirroring the existing precedent — Fighter's loadout already hardcodes Power Attack/Dodge/Weapon Focus the same way, so this is consistent with the fixed-loadout composer's current maturity level, not a new kind of shortcut. Sufficient for the bar: item 3 requires spell *selection*, not arcane-school *choice* — hardcoding the school while leaving real spell selection intact satisfies the literal requirement. A real UI-facing arcane-school selector (frontend's Option A) is a separate, larger feature — **backlog, not blocking**. Backend pausing item 4 to take this first, since it unblocks a literal named bar requirement rather than test infrastructure.

   **SOBERING FOLLOW-UP — Wizard's fix is a lucky exception, not a pattern (frontend survey, 2026-07-23).** Checked the other four caster classes in `pilot_compute.rs` (Cleric, Druid, Bard, Sorcerer) for an equivalent single-seeded-choice gate. None exists. Each has its own `supported_X_level` gate with a doc comment listing genuinely unimplemented calculation subsystems, not missing UI inputs: **Cleric** — no channel energy resolution, no domain spell-list contents, no spellbook posture, no spells prepared, no spontaneous cure/inflict conversion, no spell slots per day, no save DCs, no Wisdom bonus spells. **Druid** — same shape, flat identity records for class features (Wild Empathy etc.) but zero spell math anywhere. **Bard** — flat numbers for non-spell features, explicitly "no spell math whatsoever," plus no bardic-performance execution engine at all (no action economy, no round tracking, no Fascinate resolution). **Sorcerer** — doc comment explicitly says "no bloodline arcana, or spell math," same shape as Bard. None of these are "one missing seeded choice away from Computed" the way Wizard was — they're missing entire arithmetic/mechanic subsystems, which seeding a default cannot fix. **Scoping implication:** the alpha bar's "any class... from the four primary books" (§1 item 2) is a substantially bigger lift than Wizard's fix suggests — backend's incoming fix makes Wizard the *second* class (alongside Fighter) a tester can reach `Computed` with, but the other four full/partial spellcasters remain genuine multi-epic engine work, consistent with `status.md`'s existing "only Fighter reaches Computed" framing. Worth the operator weighing this when judging how close v0.6 alpha actually is to its own bar. **PARTIALLY RESOLVED then CORRECTED, 2026-07-23: the compute-gate fix (3484b5d) landed, but frontend's live testing found it necessary, not sufficient — see the new item 10 below. Wizard does not yet reach `Computed` through any shipped UI path.**

   **Comparator wiring confirms the architecture split is real, not AC-specific — then finds a clean way around it for encumbrance (backend, 2026-07-23).** Wiring `SelectedParityDimensions::from_receipt` to expose the new encumbrance/durability/money PCGen-comparison dimensions turned out to be three genuinely different problems, not one small task — backend stopped before writing code and split them honestly: **(1) durability** — small, bounded, landed. **(2) encumbrance** — initially looked blocked by the exact same headless/corpus-aware wall that killed the AC slice (`compute_encumbrance` needs real corpus-resolved item weights; `from_receipt`'s headless receipt has no corpus access) — independent confirmation the boundary is real. **RESOLVED, not deferred after all**: backend found `contract::PilotReceipt` (produced by `contract::to_pilot_receipt`) is *already* corpus-aware and already carries both `chassis` (the same struct `from_receipt` reads) and a resolved `encumbrance: EncumbranceComputation` — so instead of forcing corpus access into the headless layer, backend added a new, additive `SelectedParityDimensions::from_pilot_receipt(receipt: &contract::PilotReceipt, ...)` that reads from this already-resolved data. `from_receipt` itself is provably untouched (zero blast radius on QA's 3 existing callers). This sidesteps the architecture wall for *parity-testing* purposes without touching the harder problem of moving the `Computed`/`Blocked` gate itself into corpus-awareness (that part of the finding, relevant to AC/attack-bonus/skill-posture widening, still stands as a future epic). **(3) money** — confirmed, not deferred: a different kind of gap entirely, `money.json`'s balance is persisted session state never derived from `CharacterInput`/`PilotReceipt` at all, so there's structurally nothing for any receipt-projection function to expose. **Backlog item stands**: money parity-testing, if ever wanted, needs its own comparison path reading `money.json` directly.

    **OPERATOR DIRECTIVE (Todd Hintzmann, 2026-07-24) — work out the math, add to backlog.** Un-deferred, alongside items 7 and 17. Scope: this is the harder of the three un-deferred items — it isn't a single build task like starting-wealth or a bounded first-slice like Toughness's HP, it's the design/derivation work `future-epic-scoping.md` (gap A) already flagged as needing "a dedicated scoping/design pass before committing to either fix shape." Two things need working out, not one: (1) **the architecture decision** — thread a corpus parameter through the headless chassis call chain (~347 call sites) vs. move the `Computed`/`Blocked` gate to the already-corpus-aware layer (`pilot_compute_corpus.rs`); (2) **the actual combat math that doesn't exist yet** — real equipment-driven attack-bonus (enhancement bonuses, currently only feats/base BAB are computed) and the armor-check-penalty term for arbitrary armor, both confirmed genuinely unwritten, not just disconnected (backend's item-1-adjacent scoping pass). AC itself is closer to a non-issue (the desktop equipment tab already shows correct AC via the corpus-aware path) — the real payoff here is attack-bonus and skill-ACP. **Queued, third in line** — dispatches resumed; backend working items 7 (done) → 17 → this one, expected to start with the architecture-decision half before any math-derivation work, since the fix shape determines where the new formulas need to live.

    **Design pass done (backend, `4dadad51`, `docs/release/v0.6/item-1-architecture-wall-design.md`) — the earlier sizing was too coarse, three real findings reshape the picture.** (1) **Most of the real math already exists**: `equipment_effects.rs`'s `compute_equipment_effects` is fully written, tested, and already running in production (the desktop equipment tab calls it directly for AC); `pilot_compute_corpus.rs` already carries a `DerivedEquipmentStats` struct on every resolved item, just never populated (`::default()` unconditionally). ACP turns out to be a few-line field addition to a function that already has the record in hand, not a corpus-access problem. (2) **Attack-bonus enhancement has a genuine data-model gap, separate from corpus access**: `EquipmentSelection` is flat (`{item_id, equipped_or_active, active_state}`) with no way to express that a "+1 Enhancement to Weapon" item attaches to a specific weapon — real PF1 composes a "+1 Longsword" as base+modifier; summing the enhancement bonus without resolving that attachment would silently misapply one weapon's bonus to another or double-count on a two-weapon build. Needs its own design decision (a schema change, or a narrower one-weapon-equipped convention), independent of whichever fix shape wins. (3) **The biggest correction — bridging the corpus wall alone would not unlock a single additional equipment loadout today**: `unmet_combat_posture_conditions`/`unmet_selected_skill_posture_conditions` gate on an *exact* hardcoded `item_id` (`"item:longsword"`, `"item:chain_shirt"`), completely independent of what the math could resolve for other gear — so even with full corpus access wired into the claim gate, zero currently-displayed numbers would change. The wall's payoff is entirely contingent on a separate, larger, unasked question: **would `Computed` ever accept an equipment loadout other than the one exact hardcoded posture?** That's a claim-gating philosophy call (how much variation is "supported enough" to claim correctness), not an engineering one — logged as item 27 below for the operator, distinct from the fix-shape question.

    **Three fix shapes compared, with real call-site counts**: (a) thread corpus through the headless chain — ~19+37+100ish call sites, large, and per finding (3) buys nothing today without the separate posture-gate decision; (b) move the `Computed`/`Blocked` decision to the corpus-aware layer — a real cross-cutting change to every command's save/mutate control flow, same ceiling as (a); (c) **NEW, recommended** — enrich the corpus-derived DTO section (where `compute_pilot_with_corpus` already has corpus in scope) by calling the already-existing `compute_equipment_effects`, surfaced as an additional, explicitly-not-claim-gated display section, same shape `corpus_derived.equipped_items` already uses today. Zero changes to `build_pilot_headless_receipt`'s signature or any of its call sites, zero changes to claim-gating semantics. Delivers real ACP and a second AC path now; does not close attack-bonus (needs the attachment decision first). Full comparison table in the design doc.

    **Shape (c) implemented (backend, `08a829a1`†).** `EquipmentStatEffect`/`EquipmentEffects` (`equipment_effects.rs`) gained a real `armor_check_penalty`/`armor_check_penalty_total` field, reading the `ACCHECK:` token `arms_armor.rs`'s own doc comment already cited as present — the few-line fix the design pass predicted. `pilot_compute_corpus.rs`'s `CorpusDerivedSection` gained a new `equipment_effects: EquipmentEffects` field, computed inside `compute_pilot_with_corpus` over the `EquippedActive`-filtered equipment (mirroring `contract::to_pilot_receipt`'s own filtering exactly) via the already-existing `compute_equipment_effects` — `build_pilot_headless_receipt`'s signature and every one of its call sites untouched, as scoped, and deliberately **not** wired into any claim-gated pillar. Surfaced through the desktop DTO layer as a new `CorpusDerivedDto.equipmentEffects` section (`armorClassDelta`, `armorCheckPenaltyTotal`, `maxDexCap`, `spellFailureChance`), explicitly separate from the claim-gated `PilotSnapshotDto` fields. Attack-bonus enhancement deliberately excluded (the attachment-relationship gap from the design pass still stands, unresolved). Genuine RED→GREEN at every layer: `arms_armor.rs`'s 3 existing tests extended with real ACCHECK assertions (Leather Armor `Some(0)`, Buckler `Some(-1)`, Longsword `None`); 3 new `pilot_compute_corpus.rs` tests (real penalty present, `SelectedInactive` correctly excluded, no-equipment correctly zero) with the wiring temporarily disabled to confirm the failure before restoring; 1 new end-to-end `character_hub.rs` test proving the real Fighter fixed loadout's Chain Shirt surfaces a real nonzero ACP through the actual `create_character` command, same disable-verify-restore discipline. 236/236 lib, 202/202 desktop, full workspace 4253/0 clean. †Landed via a shared-checkout index race (backend's staged work got swept into the lead's own docs commit while restaging mid-flight) — both sides independently confirmed the content is complete, correct, and unaffected by the race; attribution corrected here for the record, not re-done. Backend correctly declined to rewrite history over a cosmetic commit-message issue in a live shared checkout.

    **Attack-bonus, bounded single-weapon slice landed (backend, 2026-07-24), greenlit by the lead as a real engineering-scope call distinct from item 27's philosophy question.** `EquipmentEffects` gained `attack_bonus_delta: Option<i16>`: `Some(n)` only when exactly one real weapon (a record carrying a `DAMAGE:` token, the same signal `damage_total.rs`'s own `resolve_base_damage_dice` already uses to identify a weapon) is `EquippedActive`, summing every equipped `equipmods` item's TOHIT-affecting enhancement bonus (`"TOHIT"` or `"DAMAGE,TOHIT"`, explicitly excluding a `"DAMAGE"`-only bonus, which doesn't affect the attack roll). `None` for zero or two-or-more weapons equipped — the attachment ambiguity the design pass identified stays honestly unresolved rather than guessed at; `Some(0)` is a real, distinct value from `None` (unambiguous, just no enhancement present) so the frontend must not treat the two the same. No schema change to `EquipmentSelection` — the narrower, bounded alternative to the attachment-relationship redesign, matching this session's established "prove the narrow provably-correct case" discipline (Toughness-only, the durability multiclass-order deferral, etc.). Surfaced on `CorpusDerivedDto.equipmentEffects.attackBonusDelta`, same non-claim-gated section shape (c) already established. Genuine RED→GREEN: 6 new `equipment_effects.rs` tests (single weapon + real enhancement, Masterwork TOHIT-only, a DAMAGE-only bonus correctly excluded, single weapon with no enhancement yielding a real zero, zero weapons honestly absent, two weapons honestly ambiguous), disabled-then-restored to confirm the exact expected failures; 1 extended end-to-end `character_hub.rs` test proving the real Fighter fixed loadout (exactly one weapon, no equipmods) surfaces the real `Some(0)` through the actual `create_character` command. 242/242 lib, 202/202 desktop.

    **OPERATOR DECISION (Todd Hintzmann, 2026-07-24) — add an equipment-attachment schema field.** Explicit call, made after the swarm-wide closure-readiness checkpoint (item 31): resolve the multi-weapon ambiguity with a real data-model change rather than leaving it honestly absent long-term. `EquipmentSelection` needs a way to express that a modifier (e.g. a "+1 Enhancement") attaches to one specific weapon, not the whole loadout — the flat `{item_id, equipped_or_active, active_state}` shape has no slot for this today. Backend to design the schema addition (new field vs. a separate attachment table/map), migrate `EquipmentSelection` and its consumers, and widen `attack_bonus_delta` to resolve correctly for 2+ weapons once the attachment is explicit. This overlaps with item 27's decision (widening the posture gate to accept any equipment) — both need the same underlying attachment data, so backend should sequence/scope them together rather than solving the schema twice.

    **Scoping plan landed (backend, `1f8dd2fa`, `docs/release/v0.6/items-1-and-27-scoping.md`), design/scoping only, no code.** Verified PCGen's own `.pcg` convention for an enchanted weapon before designing: a single equipment entry carrying its own applied modifiers inline (`CUSTOMIZATION:...EQMOD=...`), not two entries needing a cross-reference — so the real schema shape is `applied_modifiers: Vec<String>` directly on `EquipmentSelection`, not a separate attachment/instance-id scheme (backend's first instinct, correctly discarded as heavier and non-PCGen-faithful). Real blast radius counted, not estimated: 67 `EquipmentSelection` construction sites (42 in `tests/**`); checked `ChosenCharacterState` as an alternative location too — 74 sites there, no cheaper. **Central finding**: items 1 and 27 are no longer separable — the original item-1 design pass's "bridging the wall buys nothing" conclusion assumed the posture gate stays exact-match; now that item 27 widens it, corpus-awareness becomes a genuine prerequisite. Three gate-widening approaches compared (extend the static bootstrap table; move `Computed`/`Blocked` to the corpus-aware layer; thread corpus through the full headless chain), with a 6-step bounded sub-task sequence at the end (schema field → per-weapon attack-bonus → static category data → corpus-aware verdict → command-layer wiring → frontend attachment UI), each independently testable per this session's established incremental-widening discipline.

    **OPERATOR DECISIONS (Todd Hintzmann, 2026-07-24) — all three open questions from the scoping plan resolved.** (1) Feat-completeness policy: **never block `Computed` on an unrecognized feat effect** — extends the existing precedent (items 14/17/18/20/21), an unknown feat effect stays a completeness gap, not a correctness bug. (2) Gate-widening mechanism: **replace the existing exact-posture gate outright**, not the additive/keep-both approach backend recommended as lower-risk — this means the Fighter/Wizard/Rogue posture must be re-verified against the new unified corpus-aware path too, not left as an untouched fast path; backend needs the fullest test coverage on sub-task 5 (command-layer wiring) as a result, every currently-`Computed` build must still compute identically through the new single path. (3) Sub-task 3 (static category pre-filter data-authoring pass): **skip it** — go straight to the corpus-aware layer (sub-task 4), which already resolves the same question at runtime; no separate static pass needed. Dispatched to backend: proceed with sub-task 1 (schema field), coordinate the ~67-site mechanical update with QA (42 of the sites are theirs), skip sub-task 3, and build the gate widening as a full replacement per decision (2).

    **Sub-task 1 landed (backend, `797bd436`).** `EquipmentSelection` gained `applied_modifiers: Vec<String>`, exactly the schema shape the scoping plan verified against PCGen's own `CUSTOMIZATION:EQMOD=` convention. All 25 non-QA `EquipmentSelection` construction sites fixed (14 `src/rules_core`, 11 `apps/desktop/src-tauri`); the fixture-text grammar gained a new `equipment_modifier=<item_id>:<modifier_item_id>` line (`apply_equipment_modifier`), attaching onto an already-parsed `equipment=` selection by `item_id` using the same last-colon convention `apply_equipment_selection` already relies on (item_id may itself contain a colon) — errors with a diagnostic if the `equipment_modifier=` line has no matching prior `equipment=` line. 4 new tests (default-empty, single/multiple attach, unmatched-id diagnostic); 262/262 lib (258 baseline + 4 new), 204/204 desktop, both crates build clean. The remaining 42 `tests/**` sites are QA-owned — exact file:line list and the one-line fix sent to QA directly, coordinated per the operator's dispatch rather than touched unilaterally. **Process note, no code impact**: `cargo fmt -p codex` (no path scoping) turned out to reformat all 516 tracked files in this crate, not just the ones touched this cycle — the crate had drifted from the currently-installed rustfmt's formatting. Caught before staging (verified every non-mine file's diff was pure reformatting, no semantic change, via `git status`/`git diff`), reverted everything except the 10 intentionally-edited files, redid those by hand. Flagging so nobody else runs an unscoped `cargo fmt` in this shared checkout; a per-file `rustfmt <path>` is safe.

    **Sub-task 2 design fork, resolved by the lead as a bounded engineering call (not escalated — distinct from items 1/18/27's philosophy questions).** Backend correctly flagged before writing code: `EquipmentEffects.attack_bonus_delta` is a single scalar for the whole loadout; with 2+ weapons each carrying their own `applied_modifiers`, summing their to-hit bonuses into one number has no real combat meaning (which weapon are you rolling to hit with?). **Resolved**: add `to_hit_bonus: Option<i16>` to `ResolvedEquipmentEffect` (one entry per equipped selection already), computed per-selection from that weapon's own `applied_modifiers`; keep `attack_bonus_delta` exactly as it means today (`Some` only for exactly one weapon equipped, `None` for 0 or 2+), now backed by real per-weapon data instead of the old heuristic, wire contract stays additive — nothing downstream breaks, sub-task 6's UI reads the new per-item field for the real multi-weapon case whenever it's built. Same "prove the narrow case, extend additively" discipline as shape (c) and the feat-effects widening.

    **Landed and lead-verified (backend, `5b0c7243`, 2026-07-24).** `compute_equipment_effects` now resolves each weapon's own `applied_modifiers` directly instead of the old "sum every flat equipmods item, gate on exactly-one-weapon-total" heuristic; `ResolvedEquipmentEffect` gained the agreed `to_hit_bonus: Option<i16>`; `attack_bonus_delta` kept its exact existing semantics, no wire/DTO change (`per_item` isn't exposed on the wire yet). **Real, deliberate behavior change**: an equipmods item equipped but not listed in any weapon's `applied_modifiers` no longer auto-attaches to "the only weapon present" — explicit attachment is now required, matching the PCGen-verified shape. Checked this against the one existing e2e regression risk: the Fighter fixed-loadout test in `character_hub.rs` asserts `attack_bonus_delta == Some(0)` — safe, because that fixture has zero equipmods equipped at all, so the auto-attach-vs-explicit-attach distinction never applies to it. 6 existing tests rewritten to use `applied_modifiers`, 2 new (unattached modifier doesn't count; two weapons resolve independently). **Lead independently re-ran**: `cargo test --lib` (263/263, matches exactly) and the desktop suite (204/204).

    **QA's fixture fix landed (`eeaa876d`, 2026-07-24) — real count correction, and a real bug self-caught mid-flight.** QA's first automated pass over-matched: a naive text search for `EquipmentSelection {` also caught `-> EquipmentSelection {` function-signature return types, not just struct literals, corrupting a few files with misplaced braces. Caught before landing, reverted everything, rewrote more carefully. **Corrected the real count**: backend's message said 42 sites; QA independently grepped and found 32 real struct-literal construction sites plus 10 separate function-signature matches that correctly should NOT get the field — the "42" didn't match backend's own enumerated list. Diff is exactly 20 files, 32 one-line insertions, matching the corrected count precisely. **Handled a live collision correctly**: backend's sub-task 2 was mid-edit (uncommitted) on `src/rules_core/equipment_effects.rs` while QA worked, breaking QA's first build attempt with an unrelated `E0063`. QA `git stash`ed just that one file, verified their own fixture changes in isolation, then popped the stash back untouched — did not attempt to fix or work around backend's in-progress file. **Lead independently re-ran**: `cargo build --tests` clean, full `cargo test --workspace` — 4280 total passed, 0 failed across all 478 test-result blocks, matches QA's report exactly. Both sub-tasks 1 and 2 fully closed. Backend cleared to proceed to sub-task 4 (the highest-risk step, replacing the exact-posture gate per the operator's decision); frontend cleared to start sub-task 6 (equipment-modifier-attachment UI).

    **Sub-task 6 design done (frontend, 2026-07-24), no code yet.** Confirmed there's genuinely no existing command surface for `applied_modifiers` before asking (only reference in `character_hub.rs` is a hardcoded `Vec::new()`). Design: an "Attach Modifier" button on each equipped item's Gear-tab row, reusing the existing generic `ItemPickerModal` scoped to the Equipmods category — no new catalog query needed. Sent backend a concrete proposal directly: a new `attach_equipment_modifier` command modeled on `purchase_equipment`'s atomic-purchase shape (charges the modifier's real `cost_gp`, matching every other cost-bearing pick in the app rather than a free-grant exception), reusing `PurchaseEquipmentResponse`'s Purchased/Blocked outcome shape; plus a small DTO addition so the Gear tab can show what's already attached (not just raw ids). Deliberately bounded and flagged as such: no detach/remove affordance, flat catalog cost rather than modeling real PF1 enchantment-cost stacking. Correctly holding off on writing UI code until backend's command lands, per the no-stub discipline — a button wired to a nonexistent command would be exactly the kind of stub this doctrine forbids.

    **Two real gaps found before building, resolved peer-to-peer without escalating (backend+frontend, 2026-07-24).** Backend checked the proposal against real data before implementing: (1) real CRB weapon-enhancement records (+1 through +10) have no known flat `cost_gp` in the static table — real PF1 enhancement pricing is a formula (bonus² × 2000gp), not a flat catalog price, so mirroring `purchase_equipment`'s block-on-unknown-cost exactly would leave the headline use case (attaching an actual enhancement) permanently `Blocked`; (2) the bundled desktop demo corpus has zero Equipmods records at all, so an attached modifier would silently not affect the computed attack bonus unless the existing `unresolved_equipment_item_ids` tracking (item 1's shape-B fix) extends to cover `applied_modifiers` too. **Resolved between backend and frontend directly**: treat unknown cost as free-to-attach (an explicit, flagged deviation, not silent), and extend the unresolved-ids tracking — which the frontend's existing `UnresolvedNotice` component picks up with zero new frontend work. Both correctly judged this doesn't need lead/operator escalation — same shape as other bounded first-pass simplifications already made this session (explicitly flagged, not a claim-gating philosophy question), flagged to the lead for visibility rather than for a decision. Lead agrees with that read; logging for the record, not overriding.

    **Sub-task 6 backend half landed and lead-verified (`3a05539a`, 2026-07-24).** New `attach_equipment_modifier` Tauri command, mirroring `purchase_equipment_at_root`'s atomic resolve-cost → check-affordability → mutate → charge shape: validates `modifier_item_id` against the real catalog first (same check `append_to_character` already runs, rejecting a fabricated id before any cost/target check), treats an unknown `cost_gp` as free-to-attach per the agreed deviation. `ResolvedEquipment`/`ResolvedEquipmentDto` gained `applied_modifiers` (nested resolved records, reusing the existing DTO shape); `compute_pilot_with_corpus` resolves each selection's own modifiers the same way it resolves the selection itself, folding any unresolvable modifier into the existing `unresolved_equipment_item_ids` list. `rule_system_adapter.rs`'s `TestPf1Delegate` now reuses the shared mapper instead of hand-rolling a second mirror. **Real production bug found and fixed along the way, not introduced by this sub-task**: `SavedCharacterStore`'s save/load round-trip never had a writer for `applied_modifiers` — sub-task 1 only built the fixture-text *parser* side (for tests), never the production *writer* `character_hub.rs`'s real save path uses, so every attached modifier was silently dropped on save. Fixed in `local_store.rs` (validate + render), 2 new round-trip tests. 7 new desktop tests, 4 new lib tests, all genuine RED→GREEN. **Lead independently re-ran**: full workspace (4284/4284, matches exactly) and desktop suite (211/211, matches exactly). Frontend cleared to wire the Gear tab against the real command/DTO shape.

    **QA finding, landed (backend, 2026-07-24) — silent corpus-resolution drops now traceable, shape B of QA's two proposed fixes.** QA root-caused the Spells tab inconsistency to something bigger than the Spells tab: the desktop app's only bundled `corpus` (`corpus_fixtures.rs`) is a deliberately tiny ~4-record demo fixture (its own doc comment: "not a general corpus provider, exhaustive corpus coverage is out of scope") — any real, disk-persisted `spells_selected`/`equipment_selections` entry outside those exact records silently vanished from every corpus-derived output (`school_coverage`, `equipped_items`, and now shape (c)'s `equipment_effects`) with zero diagnostic, indistinguishable from "nothing selected." This also capped shape (c)'s real-world value: its ACP/AC looked complete only because the Fighter fixed loadout happens to use exactly the 2 bundled equipment records. **Decision (backend, greenlit as an engineering-scope call, "your read" per the lead):** shape B (an honest "unresolved" signal) over shape A (bundle more records) — bundling more records doesn't fix the bug, it just moves the boundary; the app structurally cannot ship the full ~652-spell/hundreds-of-equipment-record corpus, so any fixed bundle size is permanently, arbitrarily incomplete. `CorpusDerivedSection` gained `unresolved_spell_ids`/`unresolved_equipment_item_ids: Vec<String>` (`pilot_compute_corpus.rs`), populated wherever `spell_id_resolve`/`equipment_id_resolve` returns `None` instead of the previous silent `continue`, surfaced on `CorpusDerivedDto` for the frontend to render as an honest "not shown — outside demo corpus" indicator (UI treatment is frontend's call, not built here). Genuine RED→GREEN: 3 new `pilot_compute_corpus.rs` tests (an unresolvable equipment selection tracked, an unresolvable spell tracked, everything-resolves leaves both lists genuinely empty), both push calls disabled to confirm the exact expected failures before restoring; 1 new end-to-end `character_hub.rs` test against the real bundled corpus (not a hand-built fixture) confirming a real `add_equipment_selection` for an item outside the demo bundle surfaces correctly — which also surfaced a real, pre-existing, unrelated fact worth noting: the fixed Fighter loadout's own `item:shield`/`power_attack` placeholder entries were already silently unresolvable before this fix (not new, not a bug this introduces, just now visible instead of invisible). 245/245 lib, 203/203 desktop.

    **Follow-on bug found and fixed (QA finding + backend fix, 2026-07-24) — `EquipmentEffectsDto`'s three `Option` fields never actually hid on absence.** `maxDexCap`/`spellFailureChance`/`attackBonusDelta` lacked `#[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]` (unlike the precedented `damage_reduction` field in the same file), so a Rust `None` serialized as `"maxDexCap":null` — key present, literal `null`, not an omitted key — defeating the frontend's `!== undefined` hide-checks (`null !== undefined` is `true`) and rendering garbled `"+null"`/`"null%"` strings. QA confirmed three independent ways (a throwaway serialization test, tracing the zero-transform `invoke()` call, running the actual JS template logic in Node) before flagging; the one character live-tested earlier (Chain Shirt + exactly one weapon) happened to resolve `Some(...)` for all three fields, so the `None`/hide path was never exercised until now. **Fixed (backend)**: added the same `skip_serializing_if` attribute to all three fields. Genuine RED→GREEN: a new permanent wire-serialization test (`equipment_effects_dto_omits_its_optional_fields_when_none_and_includes_them_when_some`) reproduced the exact bug (`"maxDexCap":null` etc.) with the attribute temporarily removed, then confirmed fixed after restoring. Frontend's TS type already declared these fields as optional (`maxDexCap?: number`, not `| null`), so no frontend change was needed — the wire shape now matches what frontend was always coded to expect. 204/204 desktop, frontend typecheck clean.

Confirmed the new field is genuinely inert with respect to claim-gating from three independent angles: structurally (`base` is computed via the unmodified `compute_pilot_base_chassis` first, with zero lines of that function touched in the diff), by call-order (`create_character_at_root`'s `Blocked` early-return happens before `compute_pilot_with_corpus` is ever called, same shape as item 7's wealth gate), and empirically (the new desktop test asserts `Saved`, not `Blocked`, on a build that also carries a real nonzero ACP). Also confirmed `arms_armor.rs`'s `None`-vs-`Some(0)` semantic distinction is preserved correctly (no token vs. a real zero-value token). Ran clean throughout, RED/GREEN reproductions restored byte-identical each time.

3. **Three writers, one checkout.** No worktrees by design; commit serialization (§2) mitigates git-index and cargo target-dir races but depends on teammate discipline. First symptom to watch: a "committed" change absent from `git log` on `tranche/6`.

    **New hazard found and self-caught (backend, 2026-07-24) — unscoped `cargo fmt -p codex` silently reformats all 516 tracked files.** Full account under item 1's sub-task-1 landing note above. Standing lesson for this checkout: `cargo fmt -p codex` (no path scoping) is unsafe right now — it would silently rewrite every teammate's files, including in-progress uncommitted work, since the crate has drifted from the currently-installed `rustfmt`'s formatting. A per-file `rustfmt <path>` is safe.

6. **RESOLVED — false alarm, real infra bug: batched/delayed SendMessage delivery to backend.** Lead initially logged this as "backend not reliably stopping on instruction" after backend appeared to execute BAB/save stacking and equipment AC/carry-capacity work despite a reprioritization, a pause-and-explain, and a hard stop sent via SendMessage. **Correction:** none of those messages reached backend until long after the fact — backend's entire session shows zero inbound content until every message (routing fix, reprioritization, task-2 approval, pause, hard stop, escalation) arrived simultaneously in a single batch, well after task 5 (`d475097`) was already committed. Backend was, the whole time, faithfully and well executing its *original* wave-1 brief exactly as spawned (task 4 = BAB/save stacking, task 5 = equipment AC/carry-capacity, in that order) — including sound judgment calls (holding the commit for QA to sequence test updates, auditing equipment AC before assuming work was needed, scoping a 63-test-breaking overreach back down to 40). When the batch did land, backend correctly held at the *last* instruction ("wait for a message from me before doing anything at all") rather than self-authorizing off older content in the same batch that could have read as permission — exemplary handling of a confusing situation, not a discipline problem. Delivery was verified fixed with an explicit ack-the-literal-sentence test before backend was resumed. The commit (`d475097`) stands; it was legitimate work under a legitimate brief. Frontend and QA showed no equivalent delivery gap in the same window, so this looks scoped to backend's inbox/session specifically rather than a systemic swarm-messaging failure — still worth the operator/infra owner looking into if this recurs, since it cost real time and caused a false lead-side escalation.
7. **Latent data-quality defect — `Combat Expertise` is a duplicate key in the generated feat catalog (featmate, 2026-07-26, lead-verified).** A `uniq -d` sweep over the `key:` field of all 185 CRB feat records across `feat_data/{general,combat,metamagic,item_creation}.rs` returns exactly one duplicate: **"Combat Expertise" appears twice** in `feat_data/combat.rs` (lines 25 and 26), with different effect payloads. Root cause verified in the raw PCGen corpus, not inferred: `grep -c '^Combat Expertise\t' cr_feats.lst` returns **2** — a base record and a `TYPE=Base` Monk-flurry-aware variant — and the generator emitted each as its own `FeatTableEntry` under the same `key`. **Consequence:** any exact-match `key` lookup silently takes the first record and ignores the second; any "sum every effect for this key" logic would double-count. **Harmless today** — nothing in this codebase reads Combat Expertise — but it sits directly under the deferred activation-gated feat work (Combat Expertise / Deadly Aim / Arcane Strike), which would be the first consumer to trip it. **Deliberately not hand-patched:** `feat_data/*.rs` carries its own "Not hand-authored — do not hand-edit; regenerate if the corpus changes" discipline, so this is a generator-level fix belonging to whoever next touches the feat-data generator, not an inline edit. Recorded here rather than left in a scoping doc so it survives as a durable finding.
8. **Named deferral — Leadership stays ungrounded despite carrying a computable formula (featmate, 2026-07-26, lead-ratified).** Leadership's corpus record does carry a genuinely computable magnitude: `BONUS:VAR|LeadershipScore|(TL+LeadershipAbilityScoreMod)` with `LeadershipAbilityScoreMod|CHA`, i.e. a Leadership score of total level + Charisma modifier, plus a cohort-level cap formula. Under the corrected standalone-grounding bar this would technically qualify as a real, verified, self-derived number. **Decision: defer the whole feat anyway.** The feat's actual substance is a cohort/follower subsystem this engine models nowhere; grounding the score alone would surface one number from an almost entirely unmodeled mechanic, which reads closer to overstating coverage than to the Poison Resistance / Bardic Knowledge precedent (where the grounded magnitude *is* the feature's whole mechanical content). Correctly belongs in the same category as the Familiar, Eidolon, and opponent-tracking subsystem questions, not in the cheap-standalone-fact vein. Logged as an explicit, named deferral rather than silently dropped, so a future pass does not re-derive it and reach a different conclusion by accident.

4. **RESOLVED — "durability" definition (§1 item 4).** QA flagged that the alpha bar's calculation list ("... AC, durability, carry capacity ...") is ambiguous between character survivability and item hardness/breakage; the codebase has no item-hardness/breakage mechanic anywhere. Ruling (lead, 2026-07-23): **durability = the character's displayed survivability stat — max/current HP, temporary HP, nonlethal damage tracking, and unconscious/dying/death thresholds** — distinct from "level-up hit points" (§1 item 4's separate entry), which is specifically the hit-die + Con-mod increment calculated during the level-up flow. This keeps the bar scoped to character-sheet mechanics already in the codebase's model and introduces no new game system. QA may proceed writing red-green tests against this definition. Follow-up (QA, same day): durability has almost no production surface either — only an isolated level-1 fighter HP value, no aggregate max/current/temp HP or dying-death fields anywhere — so this is a real wave-2 build item, not just a naming clarification.

    **Backend build landed, frontend never followed (backend's DTO-consumption sweep, 2026-07-24): full command/DTO shapes for whoever wires the UI, so this can be picked up cold.**

    - **`load_character_durability`** — request `{ characterId: string }` → `Result<CharacterDurabilityDto, string>`. Errs (not a `Blocked`-style payload, a rejected IPC call) when the saved build isn't single-class Fighter/Wizard/Rogue at a level `durability::compute_max_hp` recognizes — same scoping restriction the compute layer already has elsewhere, not new. A Durability tab needs to handle this the same way other tabs handle an unsupported-class build (a "not available for this build" fallback), not treat it as an unexpected failure.
    - **`adjust_character_hp`** — request `{ characterId: string, deltaHp?: number, deltaNonlethal?: number }` (both default 0) → same `CharacterDurabilityDto`, recomputed. **This is already atomic and delta-based, not two calls needing client-side coordination** — unlike the money/equipment-purchase coupling this doc flagged earlier, there's no read-then-compute-then-write race to design around: send `deltaHp: -8` to deal 8 lethal damage, `deltaNonlethal: +5` to add 5 nonlethal, and the server clamps (`current_hp` capped at `max_hp` on healing, `nonlethal_damage` floored at 0 on recovery) and reclassifies `status` in the same call. A "type an absolute new HP value" input just needs `deltaHp = newValue - currentHp` computed client-side before the one call — still one round trip, not two.
    - **`CharacterDurabilityDto`'s 4 fields, in PF1 terms**: `maxHp` — the character's ceiling, computed fresh from class hit die + Con modifier each load, never stored. `currentHp` — the live tracked value, persisted in a `hp.json` sidecar, defaults to `maxHp` the first time a character is loaded (no special "initialize" call needed). `nonlethalDamage` — PF1's separate nonlethal pool, persisted alongside `currentHp`; `currentHp` itself is never reduced by nonlethal damage. `status` — a pre-computed label, one of `Normal` / `Staggered` (`nonlethalDamage == currentHp`) / `Disabled` (`currentHp == 0`) / `Unconscious` (`nonlethalDamage > currentHp`) / `Dying` (`currentHp < 0`) / `Dead` (`currentHp <= -constitutionScore`) — a minimal UI can render this string directly (e.g. as a badge/color) rather than re-deriving thresholds client-side.
    - **What doesn't exist yet, flagged so it isn't assumed**: no temporary HP field (already noted above as explicitly out of scope for this wave); no dedicated "heal to full"/"stabilize" convenience call (achievable client-side as `deltaHp: maxHp - currentHp`, just not a named single-purpose command); no death-save/stabilization mechanic beyond the `Dying`/`Dead` labels themselves (labels only, no dice-roll flow) — consistent with the alpha bar not naming that affordance.
    - **Net for whoever builds this**: the backend side is a clean, already-atomic read/write pair with no coupling hazard to design around — the missing piece is purely a new HP display + a damage/heal input calling `adjust_character_hp`, no new backend work anticipated for a minimal version.

    **Status thresholds verified against real PF1 rules, not just internal consistency (backend, 2026-07-24).** `classify_durability`'s exact formula (`durability.rs:99-113`) checked against the real d20/PF1 "Injury and Death" and "Nonlethal Damage" rules: Dead (`current_hp <= -constitution_score`, correctly the Constitution *score* not modifier), Dying (`current_hp < 0`), Disabled (`current_hp == 0`), Unconscious (`nonlethal_damage > current_hp`), Staggered (`nonlethal_damage == current_hp`), Normal (fallthrough) — all 6 match. One precedence question confirmed deliberate: at `current_hp == 0` with `nonlethal_damage == 0`, Disabled and Staggered are both literally true, but the `if/else if` order checks Disabled first — correct, matches the real ruling that 0-hp-Disabled is the more specific controlling rule. **Sourcing caveat stated plainly**: no local PCGen corpus source contains this rule text (checked; PCGen's data only references these conditions as effects *caused by* specific abilities, not the core rules defining the thresholds) — verified against the well-established, unchanged-since-3.5e d20 SRD Open Game Content text instead, same sourcing category as `money.rs`'s already-flagged pp/gp/sp/cp ratio. Clean result, no mismatch — safe for frontend to render these labels as ground truth.

    **Backend half landed, frontend half never started (backend, 2026-07-24, DTO-consumption sweep).** `CharacterDurabilityDto` (`maxHp`/`currentHp`/`nonlethalDamage`/`status`) and its two Tauri commands (`load_character_durability`, `adjust_character_hp`) are real, registered, computed, and fully tested backend-side — but frontend has zero `invoke()` calls for either command and zero components/tabs mentioning "durability" anywhere (case-insensitive grep, zero hits). `CreateCharacterForm.tsx`'s own `maxHp` is a separately-computed client-side local value, not a read of the backend DTO. Matches the untracked/unattributed `SWARM_TASKS.md`'s durability row still showing "queued" (that file's overall claims are not trusted, per the earlier flag, but this one line happens to be independently confirmed true). This is real remaining bar-item-4 distance: not an architecture gap, a genuine missing UI surface (HP/dying-death display, damage tracking) — bounded, frontend-shaped work whenever dispatched.

    **RESOLVED — frontend built the Durability/HP tab (`75b083bd`).** New `characterDurability.ts` boundary wraps both commands exactly per the scoping above; `CharacterSheet.tsx` gained durability state, a loader effect (mirrors the money pattern), `handleAdjustHp`, and a new `DurabilityPanel` inside the Defense tab — current/max HP, nonlethal damage, an emphasized status badge when not `Normal`, and Damage/Heal buttons styled like `MoneyPanel`'s Add/Spend. Falls back to an honest "not available for this build" line when durability is `null` (covers both in-flight loading and a genuinely unsupported build, same treatment as other unreachable-build cases). **Live-verified against the real dev build, both directions**: a supported Dwarf Rogue 2 went 19/19 → damaged to 12/19 (disk-confirmed) → healed to 15/19 → damaged into negative HP, status correctly flipped to "Dying" and rendered emphasized, then healed back to exactly 19 (clamped at max, no overshoot). An unsupported Wizard/Rogue multiclass correctly showed the fallback line, no crash, and confirmed no stray `hp.json` was written for it. No new test file (UI-only, no new pure logic beyond what the live drive already covered); 63/63 existing suite still green, typecheck clean.

This predates the swarm. 6. **RESOLVED — damage reduction exposed through the DTO, and now actually rendered.** Landed (`f7ce289d`): `pilot_compute.rs`'s existing per-class DR explanations are now exposed through the DTO layer. **Frontend follow-through landed (`26ac0704`)**: the Defense tab (previously the literal "coming soon" stub) now renders "Damage Reduction: X/—" when present, added `damageReduction` to the shared `PilotSnapshotDto` TS type (was entirely missing there too — the DTO-consumption sweep that found this gap was right). Absent case live-verified honestly across all 3 chassis-supported classes (Fighter/Wizard/Rogue, including a multiclass) — `undefined`, not a fabricated zero, correct stub text still shown. Populated case **cannot** currently be live-verified: Barbarian is the only DR-granting class and it's `human-diagnostics-only` (always `Blocked`), so no live UI path today ever produces a saved character with a real non-zero value — a structural fact, not a testing gap, noted in the code rather than glossed over. One honest note left in-code at the time, initially unfixed: the "Recompute" action's own DTO didn't carry `damageReduction`, so it wouldn't have refreshed if a real value ever became reachable later — **closed the same day, see the `af2d8b9f` entry immediately below**, not left standing. Not part of the alpha bar's explicit calc list (item 4 doesn't name DR), but a real completeness improvement landed opportunistically, now genuinely end-to-end.

    **Recompute-DTO parity gap closed (backend, `af2d8b9f`).** Found the DTO under its real Rust name (`CharacterSnapshotDto` in `recomputeCharacter.rs`, backing `recompute_character`) and added `damage_reduction: Option<i16>` with the identical serde shape `PilotSnapshotDto` already uses, populated from the same source field (`PilotDefenseViewModel::damage_reduction`) at the one construction site. Since no live pipeline can exercise a real non-`None` value (Barbarian never reaches `Computed`, same structural fact as above), added the meaningful test that *is* available instead: a direct serialization test proving `Some(3)` emits the camelCase `"damageReduction"` key and `None` omits it entirely (not `null`), plus a golden-path assertion that the existing Fighter fixture correctly stays `None` — same wire-shape rigor as the earlier `corpusDerived` regression tests, without needing a real compute pass to get it. 2 new tests, full suite green, zero blast radius.

    **Independently re-verified, not just self-reported (QA, 2026-07-24).** Read the actual diff and code rather than trusting the commit message: (1) confirmed `damageReduction?: number` is genuinely on the shared `PilotSnapshotDto` interface every consumer imports, not used ad-hoc; (2) read `DefenseTab`'s JSX directly — `!== undefined` guards the rendered line, falling through to the stub text on absence, with `snapshot?.damageReduction` correctly propagating `undefined` all the way from a `Blocked`/`None` snapshot; (3) traced Barbarian's unreachability in the compute layer itself rather than trusting the claim — `table_class_id` (used by both the single-class dispatch and `is_supported_multiclass_mix`'s per-class check) has no Barbarian entry, so no single-class or multiclass Barbarian-containing build can produce anything but `class_chassis.unsupported` → `Blocked` → `snapshot: None`. All three claims hold completely. Clean, independently confirmed record.
7. **RESOLVED (2026-07-24) — starting wealth by class, operator-provided with citation.** Was: exhaustively searched and correctly left unresolved (QA/backend found no citable value in any local corpus source, correctly declined the file-shape-stub trap in `docs/release/SD-22/artifacts/corpus/{acg,apg}/class_*.lst.md`, correctly declined to implement from unverified memory) — a genuine content-provenance/licensing question, not an engineering call, deliberately kept out of the autonomous-operation decision set.

    **Operator (Todd Hintzmann) provided the full table directly, sourced from d20pfsrd.com's "Character Creation" page** (`https://www.d20pfsrd.com/basics-ability-scores/character-creation/`, Pathfinder SRD / OGL content):

    | Class | Starting Wealth | Average |
    |---|---|---|
    | Alchemist | 3d6 × 10 gp | 105 gp |
    | Barbarian | 3d6 × 10 gp | 105 gp |
    | Bard | 3d6 × 10 gp | 105 gp |
    | Cavalier | 5d6 × 10 gp | 175 gp |
    | Cleric | 4d6 × 10 gp | 140 gp |
    | Druid | 2d6 × 10 gp | 70 gp |
    | Fighter | 5d6 × 10 gp | 175 gp |
    | Gunslinger | 5d6 × 10 gp | 175 gp |
    | Inquisitor | 4d6 × 10 gp | 140 gp |
    | Magus | 4d6 × 10 gp | 140 gp |
    | Monk | 1d6 × 10 gp | 35 gp |
    | Ninja | 4d6 × 10 gp | 140 gp |
    | Oracle | 3d6 × 10 gp | 105 gp |
    | Paladin | 5d6 × 10 gp | 175 gp |
    | Ranger | 5d6 × 10 gp | 175 gp |
    | Rogue | 4d6 × 10 gp | 140 gp |
    | Samurai | 3d6 × 10 gp | 105 gp |
    | Sorcerer | 2d6 × 10 gp | 70 gp |
    | Summoner | 2d6 × 10 gp | 70 gp |
    | Witch | 3d6 × 10 gp | 105 gp |
    | Wizard | 2d6 × 10 gp | 70 gp |

    Plus the companion **Character Wealth by Level** table (for characters created above 1st level — PC level 2 through 20, 1,000 gp through 880,000 gp), same source. Worth noting: the Alchemist value (3d6×10) exactly matches what the earlier file-shape stub in `docs/release/SD-22/artifacts/corpus/apg/class_alchemist.lst.md` showed — that stub's *value* was apparently correct, it was the *provenance* (an unlicensed placeholder file, not a real source) that made it unusable at the time; this operator-cited value is independently sourced and now usable.

    **Implemented (backend, 2026-07-24).** `money::starting_wealth_gp` (`src/rules_core/money.rs`) returns the operator-cited average gp value for any of the 11 PF1 Core Rulebook classes `ClassId` models (all 11 rows populated, not just Fighter/Wizard/Rogue — mirrors `class_tables()`'s own "complete table, narrower dispatch" precedent, since a class outside the 3 that currently reach `Computed` never reaches the wealth-granting code path anyway). The 12 non-CRB classes in the operator's table (Alchemist, Cavalier, Gunslinger, Inquisitor, Magus, Ninja, Oracle, Samurai, Summoner, Witch) are deliberately omitted — no `class:<name>` id for any of them is recognized anywhere in this crate yet, so their rows would be inert data with nothing to key against. **Deterministic by design, not a rolled value**: PF1's own rule is to roll the die (e.g. Fighter 5d6×10), but this crate has no random-number generator anywhere and is deterministic throughout (every computed value is exactly reproducible from its inputs) — rolling would be a genuine architecture departure, not a drop-in fix. Uses the operator's own printed "Average" column directly (a citation, not a re-derived computation); a future "reroll" UI affordance, if ever wanted, is a separate frontend feature layered on top of this default. `create_character` (refactored into a testable `create_character_at_root` split, mirroring every other mutation command's own `_at_root` precedent) now initializes the new character's `money.json` balance via the existing `adjust_character_money_at_root` sidecar mechanism (item 9's own infrastructure, reused rather than duplicated) immediately after the build reaches `Computed` and saves — atomic with creation, no separate call for the caller to remember. A `Blocked` build is granted no wealth, fabricated or otherwise (verified by test). 3 new desktop-crate tests (Fighter/Wizard/Rogue's distinct correct balances, and a Blocked-build zero-grant proof) plus 5 new lib-crate tests on `starting_wealth_gp` itself (the 3 reachable classes, the other 8 CRB classes, an independent dice-count×35 arithmetic cross-check, and unrecognized-class `None` handling). Genuine RED→GREEN: the wealth-granting call was temporarily disabled, the two positive tests failed with the exact expected 0-vs-17500/7000 mismatch, then restored and confirmed green. 228/228 lib, 200/200 desktop.

    **OPERATOR DECISION (Todd Hintzmann, 2026-07-24) — go ahead on the 12 (currently: 10 named) non-CRB classes, d20pfsrd.com re-confirmed as an approved reference source.** Explicit go-ahead given directly, in response to a dashboard status question about blocked items. **Count discrepancy to reconcile before implementing**: this doc's own transcription above names exactly 10 non-CRB classes (Alchemist, Cavalier, Gunslinger, Inquisitor, Magus, Ninja, Oracle, Samurai, Summoner, Witch), but both this entry's own prose and `SWARM_TASKS.md` say "12" — backend should re-check the original d20pfsrd table (`https://www.d20pfsrd.com/basics-ability-scores/character-creation/`) directly to confirm the real count and catch any missed row before building, not assume either number is right. **Scope, once reconciled**: unlike the class-breadth item below, this does NOT require a full rules-engine chassis (BAB/saves/HP/skills/spells) per class — starting wealth is level-independent, flat data. Needs: (1) a recognized `class:<name>` id for each new class (extending whatever id space `ClassId`/`class_tables()` uses, even if the class has no other engine support yet — same "recognized but narrow" shape `class_tables()` already established), (2) a `starting_wealth_gp` table entry per class using the already-transcribed Average column, (3) confirm the wealth-granting path handles a recognized-but-otherwise-`Blocked` class correctly (a class with no working chassis will never reach `Computed` regardless, so no wealth grant risk — verify this explicitly rather than assume it). Bounded, real work; queued for backend after sub-task 5.

    **Count reconciled (backend, 2026-07-24) — the real count is 10, not 12; "12" was a prose typo, not a sign of missing rows.** Fetched the live d20pfsrd "Character Creation" page directly rather than trusting either number: its "Starting Character Wealth" table lists exactly 21 classes total. Cross-checked every single row (id + average gp) against this doc's own already-transcribed table programmatically — zero mismatches, zero missing classes. 21 = the 11 CRB classes already implemented + the 10 non-CRB classes already named here (Alchemist, Cavalier, Gunslinger, Inquisitor, Magus, Ninja, Oracle, Samurai, Summoner, Witch). No twelfth or thirteenth class exists on the real source.

None of the 10 has a `class_tables()` row or a `compute_class_chassis` dispatch arm — recognized only by this one function, the "recognized but narrow" shape, same as `class_tables()`'s own precedent of carrying rows a `Computed` build might never reach. **Verified explicitly, not assumed**: a new desktop-crate test creates a Human Alchemist level 1 and confirms it still reaches `Blocked` (no chassis dispatch exists for that class id) with zero wealth granted — proving the new recognition carries no risk of ever granting wealth to an unproven build. 2 new lib tests (all 10 real averages, including an independent dice-count arithmetic cross-check folded into the existing check) + 1 new desktop test. 276/276 lib, 212/212 desktop.

8. **CORRECTED SCOPE (operator directive, 2026-07-24, ~21:20 ET) — full class/race breadth across all four primary books, not just the 8 CRB classes, and NOT deferred, backlog, or future-epic framing.** Original entry (below, preserved for the record) scoped this to "8 of 11 CRB classes" and used "future-epic"/"not a bounded task" language that the operator correctly read as shoving real work off to a later cycle — that was not the intent (the intent was sequencing discipline, same as items 1/27's scoping-before-code pattern) but it's what the words said, and words are what get read. **Operator's actual directive**: every class from all four primary books this alpha bar already names (§1 item 2 of `release-swarm.md`: "any class or race from the four primary books — CRB, Bestiary 1, APG, ACG") gets wired all the way through — real computation, not a data-only stand-in. Of roughly 29 classes across all four books, only 3 (Fighter/Wizard/Rogue) have a working chassis today. **This folds in and supersedes the earlier CRB-only framing and the separate "non-CRB classes just need wealth data" framing from item 7** — non-CRB classes (Alchemist, Cavalier, Gunslinger, Inquisitor, Magus, Oracle, Summoner, Witch, Ninja, Samurai, and whatever ACG adds beyond those) need the same full BAB/save/HP/skill/spellcasting/feature computation as the 8 remaining CRB classes, not a wealth number standing in for real support. **This is the swarm's primary active work now, not backlog.** Scoping/sequencing discipline (which class(es) first, what's genuinely shared infrastructure vs. class-specific) still applies — a 26-class effort attempted with zero plan would be chaotic, not fast — but planning must lead directly into continuous real implementation, the same way items 1/27's scoping pass led immediately into 5 shipped sub-tasks in one session, not into a parked decision. Dispatched to backend now, not queued behind anything else.

    **QA's corpus/data survey (2026-07-24), read-only, per-category not per-class — one major finding reshapes the sequencing.** **BAB/save chassis may already be built, not missing**: `class_tables.rs`'s `CLASS_META` table already has complete, correct rows for all 8 target classes (Barbarian/Bard/Cleric/Druid/Monk/Paladin/Ranger/Sorcerer — Monk correctly capped at level 12, Druid at 15, matching real PF1), and `compute_generic_table_chassis` (`pilot_compute.rs`) already exists and is designed to read this table for "any class besides Fighter." **The actual blocker, verified directly by the lead**: `table_class_id` (`pilot_compute.rs:6747`) is a 3-arm hardcoded string match (Fighter/Wizard/Rogue only) gating every caller of `compute_generic_table_chassis` — confirmed via the 3 real call sites, all gated the same way. Widening this one function is plausibly a fast, high-confidence first slice for the BAB/save pillar across all 8 classes, not new table-authoring. **Class-skill lists**: zero corpus presence for any class (confirmed by grep) — Fighter/Rogue's own lists are hand-authored bounded arrays in `skill_allocation.rs`; all 8 need the same from-scratch, primary-source-verified authoring, no shortcut. **Class features**: also zero corpus presence — the largest new-authoring bucket, each class has unique non-formulaic mechanics (Rage, Wild Shape, Bardic Performance, Smite Evil, Favored Enemy, Ki pool, Channel Energy, Domains, Turn/Rebuke Undead), none reducible to a shared formula the BAB/save pillar's table-driven approach could reuse. **Spellcasting for the 6 casters (Cleric/Druid/Bard/Sorcerer/Paladin/Ranger)**: raw data is fully present (652 CRB spells ingested, and the `CLASSES:` per-class-level token is already parsed into `LstSpellRecord.classes`) but never projected into a per-class spell-level table — a "re-generate from already-parsed data" task, not a "go re-source it" task; spells-per-day-by-level ladders still need fresh authoring per class, same as Wizard's was. **Starting wealth**: a non-issue for these 8 — already covered by all 11 CRB rows in `starting_wealth_gp`. **Non-CRB wealth data note**: of the 10 non-CRB classes, only 6 (Alchemist/Cavalier/Inquisitor/Oracle/Summoner/Witch) are in a book this corpus has ingested locally (`advanced_players_guide`); Gunslinger/Magus/Ninja/Samurai aren't in any ingested book here (no Ultimate Combat/Ultimate Magic data present) — those 4 would need corpus ingestion before any chassis work could start, a real prerequisite QA flagged but did not attempt. Feeds directly into backend's scoping plan; not an implementation, an inventory.

    **QA's survey expanded to all 4 books (2026-07-24) — real class count corrected, and a real architectural split found between CRB and APG/ACG.** **Corrected count**: 27 total classes across the corpus (11 CRB + 6 APG: Alchemist/Cavalier/Inquisitor/Oracle/Summoner/Witch + 10 ACG: Arcanist/Bloodrager/Brawler/Hunter/Investigator/Shaman/Skald/Slayer/Swashbuckler/Warpriest), 24 without a chassis today — not "~29"/"~26" as earlier estimated. **Bestiary has no `class/` directory at all** — zero PC classes from that book, consistent with item 5's existing Pets/animal-companion ruling (monster/equipment data only). **Gunslinger/Magus/Ninja/Samurai** (4 of the original non-CRB wealth list) aren't in any locally-ingested book — no Ultimate Combat/Ultimate Magic data present, a real corpus-ingestion prerequisite regardless of approach. **Key architectural finding, lead independently confirmed**: `ClassId` (`crb/class_tables.rs:19`) is a closed 11-variant enum, and `class_tables.rs` is the only such file in the tree — no APG/ACG equivalent exists at all. So CRB's 8 missing classes (narrow `table_class_id` widening, per the entry above) and APG/ACG's 16 missing classes are NOT the same shape of fix: APG/ACG's raw chassis data is equally present (`data/corpus/{advanced_players_guide,advanced_class_guide}/class/*.json`, same `chassis_only` shape, confirmed by reading a sample Alchemist record directly) but there's no code path to consume it yet — needs a new or extended `ClassId`/`CLASS_META` equivalent, real new plumbing, not a dispatch-allowlist fix. **Skill lists and class features**: absent for all 27 classes, CRB included — this bucket doesn't shrink regardless of book. **Spell lists**: same present-raw-data/missing-per-class-projection pattern as CRB, for both other books (297 APG + 144 ACG spells fully ingested, both `"completeness": "full"`) — with one promising lead: `apg/spell_list.rs`'s own doc comment cites the exact PCGen checkout path its generation script used, which may still be directly re-runnable for real per-class spell levels rather than reconstructing them from the discarded parse state. **Wealth data gap for ACG**: the operator's original item-7 table (Paizo's "Character Creation" page) only ever covered CRB + APG classes — the 10 ACG classes have no operator-cited wealth value at all; would need a fresh citation if wanted, not something to guess. Straight inventory, no implementation design.

    **PCGen-checkout lead confirmed real, lead-verified directly (QA, 2026-07-24).** The `~/workspace/repos/pcgen` checkout `apg/spell_list.rs`'s doc comment cites is genuinely present (a real git repo). Same confirmed for `acg_spells.lst` (598 `CLASSES:`-bearing lines) and `cr_spells.lst` (CRB) present in the same checkout. **Lead independently re-confirmed** (`grep -c "CLASSES:"` = 947, exact Amplify Elixir line match).     **Backend's class-breadth scoping plan landed (`1e2ceeb7`, `class-multiclass-breadth-scoping.md`), then self-corrected the APG/ACG framing before finalizing — lead independently verified both passes.** Central finding, lead-confirmed directly in the code: all 8 CRB target classes already have real chassis logic (`explain_<class>_level1_...` functions, called unconditionally inside `compute_pilot_base_chassis` — spot-checked `explain_barbarian_level1_chassis` and `explain_ranger_level1_chassis_and_class_feature_separation` calls at lines ~4638-4715), architected as parallel "standalone" explanation surfaces never wired into the integrated pillars. `table_class_id` (`pilot_compute.rs:6747`) is the single 3-entry chokepoint, confirmed used by 3 real call sites including `durability.rs::compute_max_hp`. Corrected picture: all 3 books have equivalent resolver infrastructure already built (CRB's is one function+table, APG/ACG's are per-book modules) — the BAB/save pillar is plausibly a uniform, mostly-mechanical dispatch-wiring win across all 24 unwired classes, not CRB-only. One real asymmetry confirmed: APG/ACG's per-class files carry BAB/save only, no hit-die token (CRB's `class_tables.rs` has `hit_die_for`) — HP needs a small, real, corpus-sourced addition (the `HD:` token, not yet transcribed) alongside dispatch wiring for those 16.     **QA's Ranger test-coordination prep, ready ahead of need (2026-07-24), lead spot-verified.** Read all 30 Ranger-specific test files plus cross-referencing fixtures, split findings into "will definitely break" (integrated-field/`"standalone"` assertions across `tests/sd13_ranger_base_attack_and_saves.rs` and `tests/sd13_hybrid_level1_chassis_baseline.rs`, exact line numbers cited — lead spot-checked lines 96-106/160-168 directly, matches exactly) vs. "depends on backend's exact scope" (two `!has_explanation(..., "class_chassis.base_attack_bonus")` checks that may or may not survive depending on the new explanation id backend chooses — correctly flagged as uncertain rather than guessed) vs. "probably survives" (the overall-`Blocked` test, since Ranger's feature/spell posture blockers stay untouched in this slice) vs. "backend's judgment call" (3 support-matrix row-note assertions — does the note change to reflect new reality or stay historical record). All 4 classes have complete primary definitions in the same shape as every other class checked: Gunslinger (`uc_classes.lst:10`, HD:10, full BAB), Ninja (`uc_classes.lst:19`, HD:8, 3/4 BAB, `SERVESAS:CLASS|Rogue` — PCGen correctly models it as a Rogue-alternate class), Samurai (`uc_classes.lst:34`, HD:10, full BAB, `SERVESAS:CLASS|Cavalier` — same relationship to Cavalier), Magus (`um_classes.lst:8`, HD:8, 3/4 BAB, arcane caster with its own spell-progression variables).
