Read-only, no code touched.

    **ADVERSARIAL REVIEW FINDING (independent agent, 2026-07-24) — the plan's central "Ranger has no self-block, zero new diagnostic-authoring needed" claim is wrong, and shipping it as originally understood risked a real false-positive `Computed` claim. This is exactly why the review was run before greenlighting.** Confirmed by the lead as credible given the specificity (exact line numbers, exact test names, exact diagnostic ids); backend asked to verify and act before committing.

    - **Level 1 is not actually unblocked by the dispatch widening alone.** A separate, unconditionally-called function, `explain_hybrid_level1_chassis` (~line 7964-8058, called before the Ranger decomposition), pushes two `claim_blocking: true` diagnostics (`class_feature.hybrid.ranger.unsupported`, `class_spell.hybrid.ranger.unsupported`) for any single-class Ranger at level 1, entirely independent of `table_class_id` — `tests/sd13_ranger_level1_chassis_and_class_feature_separation.rs` already pins `Blocked` at level 1 because of exactly these. Since `HeadlessReceiptStatus` blocks globally on any claim-blocking diagnostic, widening the dispatch alone does not flip level-1 Ranger to `Computed`.
    - **More serious, levels 4-20**: a pre-existing code comment near the Ranger spell-gate arithmetic (~line 11180) states the ranger spell burden was deliberately left un-diagnosed, relying entirely on the generic `class_chassis.unsupported` diagnostic (from `table_class_id` not recognizing Ranger) to keep the receipt `Blocked`. Once the dispatch widens, that diagnostic disappears — and Ranger's own explain function structurally cannot push a replacement (no `diagnostics` parameter). Ranger's spell posture (spells known/day, save DCs, bonus slots) is only "gate arithmetic and the access ladder," narrower than the plan's "full partial-caster spell ladder... grounded through level 20" — meaning a level-4+ Ranger risked reaching `Computed` while spellcasting is genuinely not real, a direct no-stub-doctrine hit.
    - **Multiclass side-effect not in the original verification checklist**: widening `table_class_id` silently activates real fractional BAB/save stacking for any Ranger+Fighter/Wizard/Rogue combo via `is_supported_multiclass_mix`. The original step-3 verification only covered single-class Ranger.
    - **Minor correction**: `table_class_id` has 4 call sites, not 3 — `multiclass_good_saves` (~line 7038) is a 4th.
    - **Paladin coupling**: Paladin shares the same `explain_hybrid_level1_chassis` diagnostics as Ranger — the plan's "one class at a time, fully independent" framing doesn't hold for this specific pair; whatever Ranger's fix does to the shared hybrid diagnostics must not silently change Paladin's behavior.
    - **Everything else the review checked held up exactly**: the 3-entry allowlist, the ~60-assertion/~15-file regression history, the unconditional `explain_*` calls, Ranger's no-diagnostics-parameter signature, Ranger/Druid/Monk's exact file/LOC counts, `durability.rs::compute_max_hp`'s clean gating (no hidden per-class issue), and — critically — that Fighter/Wizard/Rogue cannot regress from this change (matched by earlier direct string comparison, or unconditional own branch).
    - **Required before landing**: (a) correct the plan's and the in-progress code comment's false claim; (b) add a real new diagnostic for Ranger's ungrounded spell posture (mirroring Paladin's `class_spell.paladin.partial_caster.unsupported` shape) so widening can't produce a false `Computed`; (c) explicit decision on the shared level-1 hybrid diagnostics without silently changing Paladin; (d) extend re-verification to Ranger multiclass combos. Relayed to backend in full before they committed.

Leaning Barbarian next (one cohesive feature vs. Monk's 7-feat spread), sizing the real `CharacterInput` schema needs before writing any code, same discipline as every prior class. This is 8 real class slices landed this swarm session (Ranger, Paladin, Sorcerer, Cleric, Druid, plus APG's 6-class and ACG's 10-class BAB/save/HP dispatch cycles) — 5 of 27 classes now genuinely reach `Computed` (Fighter, Wizard, Rogue, Ranger, Paladin), Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid have real spell-posture progress but stay `Blocked` on their own named permanent burden. **MILESTONE — BARBARIAN GENUINELY REACHES COMPUTED (uncommitted at verification time), the first class built on the new combat-time activation-state mechanism, lead independently verified end-to-end.** This is the payoff of the `combat-time-activation-state-scoping.md` plan and its one adversarial review (6 findings, all addressed and previously verified). Backend widened `table_class_id` to recognize Barbarian (**lead confirmed directly**, `pilot_compute.rs:6887`), hoisted the rage-execution validation to the top of `explain_barbarian_level1_chassis` before the single-class/Human gate (**lead confirmed**, same fix shape as every prior class), and built a real conditional engine replacing the old unconditional `rage_execution.unsupported` diagnostic: not-raging is a valid posture (grounds an honest record, no diagnostic), actively-raging-in-budget applies real Strength/Constitution/Will/AC bonuses, over-budget claim-blocks (never silently capped), and a non-blocking Fatigue-not-modeled diagnostic fires below level 17 while raging (per the review's own suggestion, accepted). **Lead independently verified the class-ownership validation gate directly** (`active_barbarian_rage_bonus`/`ground_or_block_barbarian_rage`, both keyed on `class_levels` containing Barbarian via `?`/early-return before reading any activation) — this is the exact fix for adversarial-review finding #3 (the spoofed-`ability_id` false-grounding risk), and **lead ran the specific test proving it**: `non_barbarian_characters_spoofed_rage_activation_is_ignored` gives a Fighter a spoofed active rage entry and asserts both `Computed` status and an unboosted Strength modifier. Also verified a subtle correctness detail directly in the diff: the rounds-per-day budget check uses `base_ability_modifiers` (pre-rage Constitution), not the post-rage boosted value, avoiding a circular-boost bug — real PF1 rage-duration math depends on normal Constitution, not raged Constitution. **Lead ran all verification independently**: `cargo test --lib` 364/364 (up from 357, matching backend's report exactly), the 7 new `barbarian_dispatch_widening_safety_tests` individually (not-raging/actively-raging-in-budget/over-budget/selected-inactive/non-Barbarian-spoofed/multiclass/Greater-Rage-tier, all pass), and the full desktop suite 212/212 clean. Backend's own self-caught regressions (Barbarian swapped for Monk as `durability.rs`'s "unrecognized class" example; `character_hub.rs`'s diagnostic-catalogue test updated for Barbarian's new zero-diagnostic golden path) followed the same established discipline as every prior class. **New QA-facing wave flagged, not yet started**: 19 `tests/sd13_barbarian_*`/`sd18_barbarian_*` files reference the retired unconditional diagnostic and need the same "no longer fires on a valid posture" fix — backend correctly held rather than drafting unprompted, awaiting QA's capacity signal after Cleric/Druid. This closes Barbarian as genuinely creatable (6 of 27 classes now reach `Computed`: Fighter, Wizard, Rogue, Ranger, Paladin, Barbarian), and validates that the adversarial-review-then-build pattern works for a materially riskier kind of change (mutating already-integrated pillar math) the same way it did for the simpler dispatch-widening slices.

**REAL PRODUCT-REACHABILITY GAP FOUND AND CONFIRMED (frontend, 2026-07-25, `833ea89c`) — Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid's `Computed` status is currently reachable only through headless test fixtures, not the shipped product.** While updating `characterHubModel.ts`'s stale support labels for the classes that closed this session (Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid/Bard), frontend found each of Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid's real `Computed` posture depends on a specific build-time choice (Sorcerer: an Arcane bloodline AND an Arcane Bond, both needed even at level 1-2, not just a level-3+ concern as first framed; Cleric: a Good-domain choice; Druid: an animal-companion nature-bond choice) that **no current UI surface can provide** — confirmed three independent ways, not just read once: (1) no picker component for any of the three choice ids exists in `CreateCharacterForm.tsx`; (2) `character_hub.rs`'s `compose_character_input` never auto-seeds a default for any of them; (3) the wire contract itself (`composeCreateCharacterRequest.ts`'s `CreateCharacterRequest`) has exactly 7 fields (displayLabel/raceId/classId/level/abilityScores/abilityBonusTarget/savedAt) — no choices field at all. **Lead independently corroborated finding (3) from the DTO shape before frontend's own live-verify finished.** Frontend then live-verified rather than stopping at the code read: fresh default-settings Human attempts at all three, through the real dev build, each returned "This build isn't ready yet" with exactly the predicted named diagnostic (bloodline-and-progression / domain-powers / animal-companion-execution), and none persisted to disk (confirmed via `~/.local/share/io.electricm0nk.codex/characters/`). Bard is unaffected — its known-spell posture treats an empty selection as honestly valid, the same "empty is valid" shape Ranger already established, no choice gate at all; live-verified fresh Human Bard 1 reaches `Computed`/`Saved` and levels cleanly to 2. Skald and Bloodrager (both ACG, landed the same day) are also unaffected — their Rage-shaped abilities are gated by `class_ability_activations` (an activation state, not a build-time choice), so no picker is needed for either.

Frontend added a new `ClassSupportLevel` value, `'headless-only'`, with copy naming the real cause honestly ("needs a choice picker not yet built," not "isn't computed"), and set Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid to it — deliberately did NOT attempt to build the picker/wire-field/backend-consumption fix itself, correctly treating that as new, separately-scopeable work rather than folding it into a label update. **Practical effect on this document's own "N of 27 classes reach Computed" framing (see the entry above and throughout this item's chain): that figure is a compute-engine claim, verified true against `build_pilot_headless_receipt` directly — it is NOT currently a product-reachable claim for 3 of those 10** (Sorcerer, Cleric, Druid). Fighter/Wizard/Rogue/Ranger/Paladin/Barbarian/Bard (7 of 10) are reachable through the real creation UI today; Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid are not, pending a picker. **Lead independently re-verified the commit**: read the diff directly (matches every claim above), ran the full frontend suite (78/78) and `npm run typecheck` (clean) against the actual committed HEAD, not just the pre-commit report. Not scoped or assigned as new work here — flagged for whenever the operator wants the picker/wire-contract gap prioritized.

**RESOLVED (2026-07-25, backend `9bafe303` + frontend `adf57cfb`) — the gap is fully closed, via a cheaper fix than the one originally scoped above.** Rather than building the full interactive picker this item's own framing implied (a new wire-contract field, new frontend UI, per-class option content authored honestly for 3 different shapes — genuinely multi-part, not a bounded slice), frontend's own follow-on scoping doc (`choice-picker-ui-gap-scoping.md`) found a cheaper option this codebase had already shipped once and accepted: **Path A**, mirroring the existing Wizard silent-canonical-default precedent (`compose_character_input` already seeds a fixed school-specialization choice for every Wizard, with zero picker anywhere in the UI, explicitly documented as "not a real in-game choice... out-of-scope future work"). **Lead independently confirmed the Wizard precedent** (`pf1_adapter.rs:304-328`, comment text verbatim) and `LevelUpCharacterRequest`'s existing `additional_choices` field (`character_hub.rs:862-871`, the proven shape Path B would have needed) before greenlighting. Backend implemented Path A in `compose_character_input`: Sorcerer gets Arcane bloodline + a familiar Arcane Bond, Cleric gets the Good domain, Druid gets the animal-companion nature bond (Wolf automatic) — zero wire-contract change, ~30-45 lines of Rust total. **Lead independently verified end-to-end**: read all 3 conditional blocks directly, ran the full desktop Rust suite (212/212) and frontend suite (78/78) against the real committed HEAD, and traced `create_character_at_root`'s own Blocked/Saved gating logic to confirm the updated tests are a genuine proof of the real command path reaching `Saved`, not a divorced internal check. Frontend's own follow-on (`adf57cfb`) then re-checked each class rather than assuming a uniform bucket — none needed `full-except-human-level-1` (confirmed directly none of the three appear in `hybrid_level1_class`'s match arms), but real per-class level ranges did differ and were live-verified through the real dev build including boundary crossings (level correctly stays at the last valid one with the real diagnostic shown, never silently advances): Sorcerer `full`/`[1,2]` (a real level-3 cutoff via `ARCANE_BLOODLINE_BONUS_LEVEL`), Cleric `full`/`[1,2,3]` (no cap found), Druid `full`/`[1]` (the code's own `druid_level == 1` condition). **Lead independently re-verified this commit too**: read the committed `CLASS_OPTIONS` entries directly, ran the frontend suite (78/78) and typecheck against the real committed HEAD — both clean. **All 10 classes that reach `Computed` via the compute engine are now genuinely reachable through the real product** — the "10 of 27 Computed" framing is, for the first time this session, simultaneously true of both the engine and the shipped product. Path B (a real interactive picker letting a player actually choose their own bloodline/domain/companion) remains deliberately deferred as separate future product-decision work, the same "out-of-scope for now" framing Wizard's own precedent already established — worth the operator's prioritization call whenever silent canonical defaults stop being good enough for launch, but not a blocker today.

**New instance of this same gap (2026-07-26): Arcanist.** Arcanist reached genuine `Computed` via the Metamagic Knowledge Exploit closure (`54d8048b`) — the first non-CRB class this segment to do so — but that closure required a `choice:arcanist_metamagic_knowledge` selection that, like Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid's own gap above, has no picker anywhere in the real character-creation UI and no auto-seeded default in `compose_character_input` yet. Named honestly by backend at build time and independently confirmed by the lead rather than assumed away: Arcanist's own `Computed` status is engine-only today, not yet product-reachable. The fix is the same proven Path A shape (a canonical-default seed naming `Empower Spell`, mirroring Wizard's own precedent and the exact pattern that closed this gap for Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid) — a small, well-understood follow-on, not a new unknown, but not yet built or scheduled as of this entry.

**PARTIALLY RESOLVED (2026-07-25/26, backend `772298bc`) — the backend half of Arcanist's own Path A gap closed the same day it was named; a second, separate half remains open, found by the lead.** `compose_character_input` (and `apply_level_up`'s own multiclass-dip branch, mirroring Wizard's dual-site precedent since Arcanist -- like Wizard, unlike Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid -- also needs a bootstrapped spell to avoid a save-time deadlock) now seeds BOTH the starter spell (`Light`, Known + Prepared) AND the canonical `choice:arcanist_metamagic_knowledge -> Empower Spell` selection for every Arcanist. New test `arcanist_level1_reaches_computed_from_compose_character_input_alone` mirrors `wizard_level1_reaches_computed_from_compose_character_input_alone` exactly and passes: a freshly composed Arcanist, with zero caller-added spells or choices, reaches genuine `Computed` through the backend command layer -- confirmed real by the lead, not just an engine-only claim in a hand-built test fixture. Desktop suite 214/214 (up from 212, two new tests, zero regressions), full lib/both SD-24 audits unaffected (this closure only touches the desktop crate).

**But the lead independently checked the actual React `CreateCharacterForm` before accepting "product-reachable" at face value, and found a second, still-open gap**: `characterHubModel.ts`'s `CLASS_OPTIONS` array — the sole source for the Create Character form's class `<select>` (a plain dropdown, no free-text/Other option) — has NO entry for Arcanist at all. This is different from Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid's own original gap: those three were already listed in `CLASS_OPTIONS` (as `headless-only`) before their Path A closure, so fixing the backend seed and flipping their `supportLevel` to `full` was the complete fix. Arcanist was never listed there in the first place, so backend's own Path A closure — while completely correct and verified for the backend command path — does not yet make Arcanist selectable in the real shipped UI. Closing this needs a `characterHubModel.ts` update adding an Arcanist `CLASS_OPTIONS` entry (mirroring `adf57cfb`'s own Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid update), not yet built or scheduled as of this entry. Arcanist remains one of the 11 of 27 classes reaching `Computed` via the compute engine (per the Metamagic Knowledge closure above), and is now reachable through the backend command layer specifically — but not yet through the real product. Path B (a real interactive picker for Arcanist's own Metamagic Knowledge, or any of the Exploits chooser-list) remains deliberately deferred, same framing as every other Path A precedent.

**REGRESSED — frontend's own attempt to close the `CLASS_OPTIONS` gap live-tested first (same rigor as the original Sorcerer/Cleric/Druid work) and found `772298bc` has a real, load-bearing bug, not just a missing UI entry.** A fresh Human Arcanist 1 through the real dev build hit a raw save-time error instead of a graceful `Blocked` diagnostic: `EMPOWER_SPELL_METAMAGIC_SELECTION = "Empower Spell"` has zero colons, but `local_store.rs`'s own save-layer validation (`validate_character_input`) requires every `selection_id` to have at least 2 colon-segments to round-trip through its fixture grammar — rejecting the save AFTER the compute engine already correctly reached `Computed`. The same gap category as item 23's earlier finding (a real feat key with no colon), recurring in a new, load-bearing spot this time. All four other Path A values (`bloodline:arcane`, `bond:familiar`, `domain:good`, `bond:animal_companion`) correctly follow the colon convention; `Empower Spell` — a real feat name, not a synthetic identity tag — breaks it. Backend's own milestone test (`arcanist_level1_reaches_computed_from_compose_character_input_alone`) only exercises the in-memory `CharacterInput` via `build_pilot_headless_receipt`, never the actual save/round-trip path, so it couldn't have caught this. **Lead independently confirmed the bug is real** (the colon-count check's exact position on the real save-write path, the exact colon counts on all five values) and **caught a real flaw in the lead's own first-pass fix suggestion** before backend acted on it: a naive rename to a colon-satisfying string would break `evaluate_metamagic_feat_prerequisites`'s real catalog lookup (which matches the selection value directly against the feat name), silently regressing Arcanist back to `Blocked` instead of fixing anything — corrected before backend started, the real fix needs a translation layer (a namespaced seed value, mapped back to the literal feat name before the catalog lookup). Frontend correctly reverted its own `CLASS_OPTIONS` addition rather than ship a class that errors on save — not yet re-attempted as of this entry.

**RESOLVED (backend, 2026-07-25) — the colon-convention regression is fixed via the translation layer the lead's own correction called for, not a rename.** `EMPOWER_SPELL_METAMAGIC_SELECTION` is now the namespaced value `"metamagic:empower_spell"` (satisfies the save layer's colon-segment requirement); a new `arcanist_metamagic_knowledge_feat_name` function in `pilot_compute.rs` strips the `metamagic:` prefix and reconstructs the literal Title Case feat name generically (every real CRB Metamagic feat is a simple "Word Spell" phrase, so this is a reversible slug transform, not a hardcoded single-feat lookup — recognizes ANY real Metamagic feat named this way, same design intent as the original closure). `ground_or_block_arcanist_metamagic_knowledge` now translates the selection before ever reaching `evaluate_metamagic_feat_prerequisites`/`resolve_metamagic_feat_effect`. `pf1_adapter.rs`'s own seed constant updated to match. **New real end-to-end regression test** (`create_character_at_root_saves_a_fresh_arcanist_without_a_colon_convention_error` in `character_hub.rs`) exercises the actual `create_character_at_root` save path the bug lived on — not just the in-memory compute path the original milestone test covered — and asserts `Saved`, not merely "doesn't panic." Also added a dedicated unit test (`single_class_arcanist_with_a_non_namespaced_metamagic_knowledge_selection_stays_blocked`) proving a bare, non-namespaced selection (the old broken shape) is correctly treated as unrecognized rather than silently mistranslated. Full verification: 15 Arcanist-specific lib tests green (including the new one), 215/215 desktop tests green (up from 214, the new save-path regression test), 474/474 full lib suite green. Frontend can now re-attempt the `CLASS_OPTIONS` addition. **Lead independently re-ran all of this against the real committed HEAD (`25c5bcae`)**: confirmed the translation function's slug transform against all 9 real CRB Metamagic feats' own corpus records (all genuinely "Word Spell" shaped, so the generic transform holds for the whole category, not just Empower Spell), ran the real save-path regression test directly and read its body to confirm it asserts `Saved` (not just no-panic), and reproduced 15/15 lib, 215/215 desktop, 474/474 full lib exactly.

8. **RESOLVED — Feats tab now shows a character's existing feat list.** `chosen.selected_feats` exposed through `load_saved_character`'s response (backend, `1509124`), wired into the Feats tab (frontend, `aa611ce`) — live-verified: Aldric's Feats tab correctly showed all 5 feats (3 fixed-loadout + Cleave + newly-added Toughness), revision bumped on the new add.

    **Independently verified, both commits together (QA, 2026-07-24).** `febf4d80` (Feat picker)'s own "can't show the complete list" caveat was accurate when written, and its real follow-up `aa611ce1` genuinely closes it — traced all 6 `toCharacterMutationRefresh` call sites in `CharacterSheet.tsx` individually, confirmed each correctly threads the unchanged feat list for non-feat mutations and the two feat-adding paths correctly append rather than fabricate, mirroring the real server-side behavior. Both relevant test files re-run, pass. Noted as a positive contrast worth calling out: unlike item 23's stale LevelUpDialog comment, this caveat was properly retired by a real follow-up commit rather than left to rot.
9. **RESOLVED — Money panel now coupled to equipment purchases.** Landed (`29e67515`): new atomic `purchase_equipment` command resolves real catalog cost headlessly (no corpus wall), pre-checks affordability, adds the equipment and deducts its cost together in one mutation, honestly `Blocked` (never free, nothing partially applied) if unaffordable or cost unknown. Closes the gap flagged against frontend's `59d5bc0` money panel — buying a weapon/armor now actually spends money.

    **Independently verified, static leg clean, live-UI leg inconclusive on an environment quirk (QA, 2026-07-24).** Confirmed `characterMoney.ts`'s `gpToCopper` (`Math.round(valueInGp * 100)`) mirrors `money.rs`'s `gp_to_copper` exactly (same `COPPER_PER_GOLD = 100` ratio, same rounding), and the boundary wiring (`loadCharacterMoney`/`adjustCharacterMoney`) correctly calls the matching Rust commands. Attempted the live-UI leg with `RUN_DESKTOP_AGENT=qa` — confirmed the driver.sh fix works as intended (frontend's concurrent session on `:96` completely unaffected by QA's on `:98`, no collision) — but hit a real, separate environment quirk: this session's window reports `1920x1200` via `xdotool getwindowgeometry` but genuinely renders/screenshots at `1280x900`, and the Load Character dialog's action-button row doesn't appear where expected in that smaller canvas, blocking the path to the Money panel. Not a code bug — stopped cleanly, no stray processes, frontend's session confirmed untouched throughout. 9a. **RESOLVED.** Backend field landed (`034d1b84`); frontend consumption landed separately (`d5db4321`, `perf(spells): use the cheaper addSpellSelection once a Wizard has 1+ spells`) — found stale during a routine idle-teammate check (2026-07-25), lead confirmed directly in `CharacterSheet.tsx`'s `handleAddSpell`: `existingSpells` is read from `props.detail?.spellsSelected`, fed through `resolveSpellRouting`, which picks the cheap `addSpellSelection` path once the character already has ≥1 spell and only uses the atomic bootstrap command for the genuine first-spell case. The code comment at the call site explicitly cites this item number. No longer a bounded frontend follow-up; corrected here rather than left stale or re-dispatched as new work.
10. **RESOLVED, LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-07-23) — Wizard is a genuinely playable second class now.** Frontend live-tested backend's Wizard fix (3484b5d) and found two further, independent gaps underneath it:
    - **RESOLVED — level-up path gap.** `apply_level_up` seeded zero choices for any class — only `compose_character_input` (character creation) was touched by 3484b5d, so multiclassing Wizard onto an existing character still hit the OLD diagnostic. Fixed in `1aabdf8`: seeds the same canonical choices in the new-class-entry branch only, guaranteed to fire exactly once per character (verified with a dedicated test — `wizard_has_canonical_specialization_selections` requires exactly 2 opposed-school entries, so a re-seed on a later level-up would break it, and doesn't).
    - **RESOLVED — bootstrap deadlock (the real bottleneck).** `create_character` only ever persists a proven-`Computed` build, so a fresh Wizard (empty spellbook) could never be saved in the first place. The Add Spell picker couldn't bootstrap around this either: `unmet_wizard_spellbook_conditions` requires one spell recorded as **both** `AcquisitionMode::Known` and `AcquisitionMode::Prepared`, but `add_spell_selection` only sets one mode per call, and `mutate_saved_character_at_root` discards (never persists) any call that doesn't independently reach `Computed` — so a Known-only call was discarded, a Prepared-only call was discarded, neither built toward the other. Backend's own earlier passing test only reached `Computed` by constructing `CharacterInput` directly in Rust, bypassing the command layer — real proof the compute logic was correct, not proof the UI could produce this state.

      **Root cause confirmed + fix landed for the second-call problem (backend, 2026-07-23).** Backend independently re-derived the same root cause before reading the framing above (verification, not agreement-seeking) and confirmed the scope precisely: purely a bootstrap/chicken-and-egg problem for the *first* spell — once a character has ≥1 Known and ≥1 Prepared entry, subsequent single-mode `add_spell_selection` calls already work fine, so `add_spell_selection` itself needed no redesign. Landed as `record_and_prepare_spell_selection` (`6e12437`) — a new Tauri command whose mutation closure pushes both a Known and a Prepared `SpellSelection` entry (same spell) in one `mutate_saved_character_at_root` call, atomically satisfying both conditions on the first call, same additive-command shape as every other `add_*_selection` command, no changes to `add_spell_selection` or the never-persist-unproven invariant. Scoped Wizard-only, shape generalizes without rework if another prepared caster gets a similar gate later. **This command is real and correct** — it just turned out not to be sufficient on its own; see the next sub-item.
    - **STILL OPEN, discovered after the above landed — a character holding a Wizard class level can never get saved in the first place.** Frontend live-tested `record_and_prepare_spell_selection` against a real build and found it still `Blocked`, on the new-and-correct "no wizard spells recorded/prepared" diagnostic (confirming 3484b5d's school-choice fix genuinely works) — never persisted, both via fresh creation and via leveling an existing character into Wizard as a third class. Read `record_and_prepare_spell_selection_at_root` directly: like every mutation command, it operates via load-existing-saved-character → mutate → recompute → save-if-`Computed`. It **requires a character that is already saved while holding a Wizard class level**. But `create_character` and `apply_level_up` both discard (never persist) a build that isn't independently `Computed`, and "Wizard class level + zero spells" is never `Computed` — so nothing in the current command surface can ever produce that starting state. This is a distinct, one-level-deeper chicken-and-egg from the one already fixed: class-acquisition and first-spell-acquisition are each independently gated, and neither can happen before the other. Backend's own earlier passing test proves the compute layer can represent this state (it saves a `SavedCharacterEnvelope` directly via `SavedCharacterStore::save`, bypassing the `Computed` gate — a test-only shortcut, not something the UI can do) — same "compute-correct vs. UI-reachable" distinction that's recurred at every layer of this investigation. Plausible direction floated to backend (not mandated): have class-acquisition itself (`compose_character_input` for creation, and `apply_level_up`'s new-class-entry branch) atomically seed one canonical starter spell as both Known and Prepared in the same mutation that adds the class level and school choices — mirrors the exact pattern already used for the school-specialization seeding. Backend investigating and proposing before any code, same diligence as every prior layer. **Net effect, unchanged from before this whole sub-thread**: bar item 3's "select spells at each spell-gaining level" remains untestable for Wizard. Frontend's UI routing change (Wizard spell picks always via `record_and_prepare_spell_selection`) is still correct, necessary work and was committed regardless — it's just not sufficient by itself.
    - **Minor, not urgent** — `apply_record_and_prepare_spell_selection` doesn't validate `source_class_id` against classes the character actually holds. Not a real discoverable tester workflow either way (flagged by frontend as a test-fixture curiosity only, explicitly not recommended as something to build UI around). Backend's call whether to fold into whatever it builds next or leave as backlog.
    - **RESOLVED, live-verified (backend `b2a5eb6`, frontend verification 2026-07-23).** `compose_character_input` and `apply_level_up`'s new-class-entry branch now both seed one canonical starter spell (`evocation.0.light`, Known+Prepared, reusing an already-proven-safe spell_id rather than a new literal) alongside the school-specialization choices — verified against `unmet_wizard_spellbook_conditions`'s own budget math (1 consumed vs. 3 available cantrips, comfortably under budget at every supported level 1–3) and confirmed not to touch the separate `WIZARD_SPELLBOOK_SUPPORTED_MAX_LEVEL=3` ceiling. Both existing deadlock-reproduction tests rewritten (not deleted) to explicitly clear `spells_selected` first, turned into standing regression guards for the mechanism; 3 new tests prove the actual fix. 187/187 desktop suite.

      **Live verification (frontend, full walkthrough with `b2a5eb6` pulled):** created "Elara Spellweaver" (Human Wizard) — creation form reported "computed and saved" for the first time in this investigation. Loaded the real sheet: Wizard 1, HP 8/8, a real Progression rail (Arcane bond/school/Cantrips/Scribe Scroll/Spells-per-day/Spellbook). Used the real Add Spell picker twice — Magic Missile (Evocation, rev.1→rev.2), then Alarm (Abjuration, rev.2→rev.3), both genuinely persisted. This closes the entire three-layer bootstrap investigation: class acquisition (`3484b5d`/`1aabdf8`), then first-spell bootstrap (`b2a5eb6`), all confirmed with real UI-driven persistence, not just compute-layer proof. **This is the first point in the swarm where a second class (Wizard, alongside Fighter) is genuinely creatable/loadable/level-up-able end-to-end through the shipped UI** — the "second class reaches Computed" claim struck twice earlier in this doc is now actually true for class acquisition and the first spell. **CORRECTION (see item 13): the "slot math summed to exactly 2/2, honestly enforced" claim originally recorded here was wrong** — it was a coincidence of the UI's own client-side arithmetic, not server-side enforcement; the actual budget check never runs against real spells at all, see below.

      **RESOLVED (2026-07-24) — render-staleness bug.** Right after a spell-add mutation, the Spells tab's corpus-derived reachability list briefly still showed "No corpus-reachable spells selected yet" even though the mutation succeeded — closing and reopening the character showed it correctly. Not Wizard-specific: same shared mutation pipeline every Add Weapon/Add Armor/Add Spell action uses. Root cause: `character_hub.rs`'s `CreateCharacterResponse::Saved` variant has `#[serde(tag = "kind")]` with no `rename_all`, so `corpus_derived` serialized literally as `"corpus_derived"` on the wire while the TS boundary's asserted-not-validated type declared `corpusDerived` — silently `undefined` at runtime. `summary`/`snapshot`/`diagnostics` all happen to have no underscores, which is exactly why every other post-mutation update looked correct and only corpus-derived data looked stale. **Fixed (backend, `498679d1`) with the correct targeted approach, avoiding the tag-renaming trap**: a per-field `#[serde(rename = "corpusDerived")]` on just the one field with an underscore, not an enum-wide attribute. Backend also caught and fixed an identical latent bug it had introduced earlier the same session in `PurchaseEquipmentResponse` (same pattern, before this bug class was known) before it became a second incident, and proactively swept the rest of the desktop crate's `#[serde(tag = ...)]` enums — none of the others needed a change (already `kebab-case` by deliberate design, or no underscored fields). **Genuine RED→GREEN**: 2 new tests serialize a real response with `serde_json` and assert on the actual JSON keys directly — the only test shape that can catch this bug class, since a test on Rust struct field values alone is blind to it. Backend reverted the fix, watched both tests fail with the exact snake_case symptom, then restored and confirmed green. 196/196 desktop suite.

      **Independently re-verified end to end, not just the diff (QA, completeness sweep, 2026-07-24).** Ran the 2 tests directly (pass). Reproduced RED themselves — removed the `#[serde(rename = "corpusDerived")]` attribute, reran, watched the exact reported symptom (literal snake_case `corpus_derived` while every other field stayed correctly camelCase, the `"Saved"` tag untouched) — restored, confirmed GREEN again. Confirmed the TS side genuinely expects `corpusDerived` (`loadCreateCharacter.ts:102`), closing the loop end to end. Independently checked the "swept the rest of the crate" claim rather than trusting it: grepped for every `#[serde(tag = ...)]` enum in the crate — exactly 4 exist, the 2 already fixed plus `Ge08BaselineArmorClass` (no underscored fields, genuinely unaffected) and `IssueUrlError` (uniform `kebab-case`, its own doc comment confirms the React consumer matches kebab-case throughout, not a camelCase boundary — also genuinely unaffected). Clean, fully independently confirmed.
11. **RESOLVED (2026-07-24) — a real, PCGen-loadable Wizard `.pcg` fixture now exists.** QA originally looked for one to mirror `sd26_pilot_case_verification.rs`'s Fighter pilot case and found none accessible (the one Wizard `.pcg` that exists, `characters/SpecialWizard.pcg`, is `GAMEMODE:3e`, wrong ruleset). Hand-authoring from scratch looked multi-cycle-shaped and was correctly not attempted speculatively; QA's later scoping pass found real syntax templates for every section needed and revised the estimate to one focused cycle. **Backend built it for real**: verified by actually running it through the real headless Gradle PCGen pipeline twice, not just plausible-looking syntax. Resolved the one flagged unknown (whether `PROHIBITED:` takes a comma-separated list in Pathfinder mode for PF1's 2 opposed schools) by confirming against a real bundled fixture. Caught and fixed a genuine authoring bug of its own along the way (pre-adjusted ability score double-counting with PCGen's bonus-pool mechanism) by diffing against the proven Fighter fixture rather than guessing. **Result: 8 of 9 core dimensions match Codex's compute engine exactly** — including real external validation of the class-skill-modifier fix from item 14 (Climb/Swim now correctly 3 against real PCGen output, not the old buggy 5, independent confirmation beyond QA's earlier corpus-citation check). The one remaining mismatch is the same pre-existing, already-documented, out-of-scope Weapon-Focus attack-bonus discrepancy the Fighter pilot case already has — not new. Fixture at `programs/codex/requirements/GE-05-oracle-validation-and-parity-harness/artifacts/pf1-crb-human-wizard-level1-v06-alpha-swarm.pcg`, handed to QA to build the actual comparator test.

    **Scoping pass, not a build (QA, 2026-07-23).** Revisited under the autonomous-operation directive to scope (not attempt) the task properly. Real finding, better than the original framing: `.pcg` is plain-text and line-oriented, not binary — genuinely readable/authorable. Found working templates for every section a Wizard fixture needs: the Fighter fixture for the class/skill/feat/equipment shape, `code/testsuite/PCGfiles/pf_Paladin.pcg` (Pathfinder-mode, prepared-caster) for the exact `SPELLBOOK`/`SPELLNAME` syntax, and `characters/SpecialWizard.pcg` for the `SUBCLASS`/`SPECIALTIES`/`PROHIBITED` arcane-school syntax. One real unknown flagged rather than assumed: that last template is `GAMEMODE:3e` where a specialist prohibits only 1 school, but PF1 requires 2 opposed schools — whether `PROHIBITED:` takes a comma-separated list in Pathfinder mode is unconfirmed, the first real step for whoever builds this. **Revised estimate: one focused cycle, not multiple**, now that the format is de-risked. Owner: backend or a dedicated future cycle — still not squeezed into this wave, but no longer assumed to be large.
12. **RESOLVED — Wizard has no spell save DC calculation.** While doing item 11's LST cross-check, QA found there was no `wizard_spell_save_dc` computation anywhere in `pilot_compute.rs` — Paladin/Ranger/Sorcerer/Bard all have the standard `10 + spell_level + ability_modifier` formula grounded (Cha/Wis/Cha/Cha respectively) with matching `tests/sd13_*_spell_save_dcs.rs` files; Wizard had neither. Confirmed via real PCGen data (`cr_classes.lst:281`, `SPELLSTAT:INT`) that the correct input is Intelligence, not Wisdom or Charisma like the other four. Not a bug — explicitly disclaimed as out-of-scope in `explain_wizard_level1_prepared_spell_baseline`'s original doc comment when that function was first written. Fixed (`3b39731`): `10 + spell_level + intelligence_modifier`, mirroring the exact existing pattern for the other 4 classes, 3 new tests, coordinated cleanly with QA on the one stale test it caused.
13. **RESOLVED, LIVE-VERIFIED (2026-07-23) — Wizard spell-slot budget enforcement never applied to any real spell.** Found by frontend while investigating item 10's cosmetic render-staleness finding, then verified live rather than left as a code-reading suspicion. Root cause in `pilot_compute.rs`'s `parse_wizard_spellbook_spell_id`: it expects the bespoke `<school>.<level>.<name>` format (matching the one seeded starter spell, `evocation.0.light`), but real corpus spell keys ("Magic Missile", "Alarm", "Grease") contain no dots at all — `spell_id.splitn(3, '.')` yields one segment, `level_token = parts.next()?` returns `None`, and the function returns `None` for every real spell. `unmet_wizard_spellbook_conditions`'s slot-consumption loop uses `.filter_map(parse_wizard_spellbook_spell_id)` to build the `consumed` sum, so every real-catalog spell is silently dropped before the over-budget check ever runs — the check can mathematically never fire against real spells, only the one bespoke-format seed spell is ever counted. **This means item 10's earlier "slot math summed to exactly 2/2, honestly enforced" observation was a coincidence of the UI's own client-side arithmetic (the picker showing "N of 2" locally), not a server-verified budget — corrected above.** Verified live: a Wizard at a 2-slot budget added two real spells (looked correct at "2/2" in the UI), then a third 1st-level spell (Grease) — expected `Blocked`, got silent acceptance and persistence (`rev.3`→`rev.4`, confirmed on disk). A Wizard can add unlimited real spells today with zero slot enforcement. Directly relevant to alpha bar item 4 ("spell slot allocation... matches PCGen") — this is a real gap, not cosmetic, and more significant than the render-staleness issue that led to finding it. Frontend correctly did not propose a fix (real ambiguity in the right repair shape — resolve real `spell_id`s against actual school/level data via the corpus, widen the parser, or something else backend would judge better — and it's backend's file).

    **Fix landed (backend, `365b3a1a`).** Confirmed this was NOT the same headless/corpus-aware architecture wall as AC/encumbrance — the real spell catalog (`SPELL_LIST`, 652 CRB records) is a plain `pub const` compiled directly into the binary, already headless-accessible with zero corpus threading, unlike equipment. `parse_wizard_spellbook_spell_id` now resolves real `SPELL_LIST` keys directly first, falling back to the old synthetic convention for zero blast radius on QA's 3 existing fixture files. `WIZARD_STARTER_SPELL_ID` upgraded from the synthetic placeholder to the real key `"Light"`, since real resolution now exists. **Genuine RED→GREEN verification, not just claimed**: backend temporarily disabled the fix, reran the new regression test, watched it fail with Grease silently accepted exactly as frontend reported, then restored the fix and confirmed it passes. New test reproduces frontend's exact scenario through the real command surface (not a parser-only unit test): Magic Missile + Alarm accepted at 2/2, Grease as a third prepared spell correctly `Blocked`, not persisted. 188/188 desktop, 197/197 lib.

    **Live re-verification (frontend, 2026-07-23).** Built a fresh Wizard ("Verify Slotbudget") rather than reuse the earlier "Elara Spellweaver" save, whose disk state still carries the pre-fix Grease artifact as stale data, not a live bug. Through the real Add Spell UI (full 652-spell catalog): Magic Missile accepted (rev.2, disk-confirmed) → Alarm accepted (rev.3, disk-confirmed, 2/2 real slots) → Grease as a third spell honestly `Blocked` with a real diagnostic ("spell level 1 over-prepared: 3 slots consumed... but only 2 available"), revision stayed at rev.3, no Grease entry on disk — mutation discarded, not silently persisted. Frontend verified both outcomes against disk rather than the screen, since the still-open render-staleness bug (below) makes accepted-vs-blocked look identical at a glance in the UI. Reproduces the exact original repro end-to-end, both sides disk-confirmed. This closes the entire Wizard investigation: class acquisition, first-spell bootstrap, and now slot-budget enforcement, all live-verified.

14. **RESOLVED (2026-07-24) — `compute_selected_skill_modifiers` applied the Climb/Intimidate/Swim class-skill bonus regardless of actual class.** Found by QA's systematic Fighter/Wizard/Rogue gap sweep (autonomous mode), verified concretely rather than reasoned abstractly. `CLASS_SKILL_BONUS` (+3) was written when this function only served Fighter (whose real PF1 class-skill list genuinely includes all three skills); the `Computed`/`Blocked` gate (`unmet_selected_skill_posture_conditions`) was later widened to any dispatch-supported class, but the +3 itself was never made class-aware. Rogue is coincidentally correct (its real class-skill list also includes all three). Wizard's real class-skill list includes none of them (Wizard: Appraise/Craft/Fly/Knowledge-all/Linguistics/Profession/Spellcraft) — a Wizard built with the deterministic Climb/Intimidate/Swim posture currently gets `climb = 5` (rank 1 + STR 3 + class-skill 3 − ACP 2); the real value should be `2` (no class-skill bonus). No claim-blocking diagnostic fires — this is a silently wrong number in the explanations list, not a caught/Blocked case, live right now for any Wizard (or Wizard-containing multiclass) that reaches this path. **Distinct from and more urgent than** the already-known, correctly-deferred "class-skill recognition is Fighter-only" finding in `skill_allocation.rs` (item 1's sizing discussion) — that one is medium-large and doesn't gate anything reaching `Computed` today; this one does, and is wrong today, not theoretical. QA correctly filed rather than fixed (production code, not their lane). Backend redirected here as top priority, ahead of the render-staleness investigation (item 10's render-staleness sub-item — reopened, see there, not actually eliminated).

    **Fix landed (backend, `93a0636d`), verified against the real PCGen corpus before writing code**, not memory: checked `cr_abilities_class.lst` directly and confirmed Fighter (`:2835`) and Rogue (`:2838`) both have Climb/Intimidate/Swim as real class skills, Wizard (`:2565`) has none of the three — resolvable entirely with data already available headlessly, no corpus wall this time. `compute_selected_skill_modifiers` now only applies the +3 when the character has at least one class (of the two, Fighter or Rogue, that actually grants it) that includes the skill; multiclass gets it correctly via PF1's real union rule (a Fighter/Wizard multiclass still gets the bonus via the Fighter side — explicitly tested, not assumed). 4 new inline tests. One real test break found and coordinated with QA before pushing: `tests/sd21_wizard_chassis_computes.rs`'s `wizard_level3_with_ge06_combat_posture_clears_the_selected_skill_diagnostic` asserted the old buggy `climb = 1`; correct value for that fixture's ability scores is `-2`.

    **Two smaller findings from the same sweep, logged, not actioned:** level-up hit points has an inconsistent exposure pattern (Fighter has a dedicated `class_chassis.fighter.level_1_hit_points` explanation + catalogue test; Wizard/Rogue don't have an equivalent per-class explanation, even though `durability.rs`'s `compute_max_hp` already computes the mathematically-identical, PCGen-cross-verified value for all three) — not a missing calculation, likely not worth dedicated work. Bonus spells from high ability score, including the Wizard Intelligence-based bonus-spells record, confirmed present and correctly wired — no gap.

    **Follow-up sweep (QA, same day): the adjacent narrow-posture-gate functions came back clean.** Checked `unmet_combat_posture_conditions`'s Fighter bonus-feat sub-check (correctly guarded by `supported_fighter_level(input).is_some()`, genuinely skipped for non-Fighter) and `compute_combat_baseline`'s Weapon/Armor Training bonuses (default to level=1 for non-Fighter via `.unwrap_or(1)`, correctly producing a 0 bonus — semantically right, not a bug despite the implicit fallback). Closes out the "does this bug pattern recur nearby" question with certainty rather than leaving it assumed.

15. **RESOLVED — class-dropdown honesty fix, plus a real positive surprise: Wizard and Rogue actually support any race, not just Human.** Frontend's class-dropdown audit (autonomous mode) found the create-character dropdown's "(Human only, partial)" label was actively false for 8 of 11 CRB classes (Paladin/Ranger/Sorcerer/Bard/Barbarian/Monk/Cleric/Druid): each has its own "does not compute a supported chassis" doc comment in `pilot_compute.rs`, and live-testing (fresh Human Barbarian) confirmed a real named `Blocked` diagnostic, never `Saved`, for **any** race including Human — the non-Human fallback text ("Pick Human to see what's computed") was false for these 8; Human only gets nicer-named diagnostics instead of 4 generic ones, never an actual computed build. Fixed with a new `ClassSupportLevel` variant (`human-diagnostics-only`, distinct from the genuinely-working `partial-human-only`) and honest dropdown/description/fallback text. Not a stub or silent failure — the `Blocked` path itself was always correctly detailed, only the labeling overclaimed. Pushed `34635157`, 62/62 suite green.

    **Bonus finding, opposite direction — real, free alpha-bar progress.** Wizard and Rogue, both labeled `partial-human-only`, turn out to be mislabeled too: confirmed via git archaeology (`3484b5d`, the Wizard fix's original commit, never had a Human condition) and live-verified (fresh Elf Wizard 1 and fresh Elf Rogue 1 both reached `Computed`/`Saved` through the real creation UI, disk-confirmed with `race_id=race:elf` and correct distinct stat blocks) that neither ever had a race restriction — `supported_wizard_level`/`supported_rogue_level` and every downstream gate check class_id and level only, never race. Reclassified both to `full`, same as Fighter. This means bar item 2 ("any class or race from the four primary books") is materially closer than believed for the three working classes — the "Human only" framing had been a stale, never-verified assumption carried forward since the original investigations, not a real constraint.

    **Confirmed through level-up too, not just creation (frontend, follow-up).** Leveled both the Elf Rogue and Elf Wizard from creation up one level via the real `LevelUpDialog`: Elf Rogue 1→2 (HP 10→17, BAB +0→+1, Reflex +4→+5) and Elf Wizard 1→2 (HP 8→14, BAB +0→+1, Will +3→+4), both disk-confirmed with correct progression and `race_id=race:elf` retained. The `full` race-support claim holds through the level-up path, not just a creation-time fluke.

    **Independently re-derived, all 11 rows, not just the two already touched (QA, 2026-07-24, completeness sweep).** Read every classification directly against `pilot_compute.rs` rather than trusting the commit: Fighter/Wizard/Rogue's `supported_X_level` functions check only `class_id` and level, confirmed no `race_id` reference in either — genuinely `full`. All 8 `human-diagnostics-only` classes (Paladin, Ranger, Sorcerer, Bard, Barbarian, Cleric, Druid, Monk) individually checked, not spot-checked — every one has the identical `supported_X_level` gate plus a `race_id != HUMAN_RACE_ID` early return, and none appear in `table_class_id`'s exact 3-class allowlist, which is what guarantees `Blocked` regardless of race for all 8, Human included. `describeClassSupportLevel`'s description strings don't overclaim anywhere. `characterHubModel.test.ts` re-ran clean. Clean, independently confirmed record for all 11 classes, not just the ones this doc had already touched.

16. **RESOLVED (2026-07-24) — 4 of 7 grounded races were missing their real third ability-score adjustment.** Elf fixed first (`9ec0e036`), then Dwarf/Gnome/Halfling + the size-explanation text fix landed together (`2f05dee4`) — backend independently re-verified all 3 remaining races against the real PCGen corpus rather than trusting QA's citations (confirmed Dwarf +2 Wis, Gnome +2 Cha, Halfling +2 Cha, all matching exactly), correctly declined to wire size into AC/attack (real future scope, not folded in), and applied the shared-checkout commit-serialization mitigation (checked `git diff --cached --stat` before committing) so no attribution race recurred. All 4 races now have accurate racial ability-adjustment recognition records; QA independently confirmed zero blast radius held (full integration suite clean) rather than trusting the claim. Catalogue adoption for Dwarf/Gnome/Halfling landed too (`fb01768d`) — QA independently re-verified all 3 PCGen citations before adopting rather than trusting the commit message, 34/34 new tests plus a full 474/474 suite run. Fully closed end to end: fix + independent re-verification + catalogue coverage for all 4 races. Following up on item 15's Elf-Wizard/Rogue confirmation, QA traced PCGen's actual race data all the way through the real reference chain (`cr_races.lst`'s per-race `.MOD` lines are only source-page citations; the real base race lives in a separate PCC pack per race → `<race>_abilities_race.lst`, each `BONUS:STAT` line explicitly tagged `TYPE:...Racial Default...`) for all 7 races Codex grounds (`explain_*_race_seam` in `pilot_compute.rs`), not just Elf. **Broken, same shape each time — a fixed physical +2/-2 pair is grounded, the race's real +2 mental-ability component is dropped:**
    - **Elf**: has DEX+2/CON-2, real is DEX+2/**INT+2**/CON-2 (`elf_abilities_race.lst:18`). Matters most since Elf Wizard is confirmed reachable and Intelligence is Wizard's casting stat.
    - **Dwarf**: has CON+2/CHA-2, real is CON+2/**WIS+2**/CHA-2 (`dwarf_abilities_race.lst:18`).
    - **Gnome**: has CON+2/STR-2, real is CON+2/**CHA+2**/STR-2 (`gnome_abilities_race.lst:18`).
    - **Halfling**: has DEX+2/STR-2, real is DEX+2/**CHA+2**/STR-2 (`halfling_abilities_race.lst:18`).

    **Confirmed correct, not affected**: Half-Elf and Half-Orc are both correctly implemented as a player-chosen floating +2 to any one ability score (QA caught and corrected its own initial misremembering that Half-Elf got nothing, by live-checking the actual code rather than assuming from the pattern — PCGen confirms Half-Elf gets the same floating +2 as Half-Orc). Human is already correct (live arithmetic on the player's chosen target, unrelated to this bug family). **Scope-checked, not assumed**: Dwarf's size/speed independently spot-verified against the same PCGen source (Medium, 20ft, both match) — the bug is isolated to the ability-modifier section specifically, each race's other trait records (size/speed/senses/immunities) look independently correct.

    Since each affected race's explanation is recognition-only (expects the fixture's chosen score to already reflect the adjustment, doesn't compute it live), any character of these 4 races built per Codex's own documented convention silently ends up 2 points off on the missing ability versus real PF1 — and for any calc keyed to that ability (saves, skills, spell DCs for casters), the error propagates. **QA's read, worth taking seriously**: this consistent a pattern across 4 races reads like a systemic authoring gap (built from a "2-stat template" that never accounted for these races' real 3-stat adjustment), not 4 independent oversights — recommends one bundled fix over 4 separate incremental ones, which the lead adopted. QA correctly filed all 4 rather than fixing them (production code, not their lane). Owner: backend, bundling all 4 into one fix, queued immediately after the render-staleness close.

    **Small, distinct addendum folded into the same fix (QA, same sweep):** while verifying Halfling's other named traits (Keen Senses, Sure-Footed, Fearless, Halfling Luck — all confirmed correct against real PCGen data), found Gnome's and Halfling's `race.<X>.trait_bundle.size` explanation text self-contradicts: it correctly sets `GNOME_SIZE_CATEGORY`/`HALFLING_SIZE_CATEGORY` to `"Small"` and correctly cites `SIZE:SMALL` from the corpus, but then claims Small "contributes no numeric effect to attack rolls, AC, skill checks... or any other computed value" — true for the other 5 grounded races (all Medium), false for real PF1 Small size in general (+1 AC, +1 attack, -1 CMB/CMD, +4 Stealth per SRD). Looks like the size-explanation template was copied across races without adjusting the claim for the two that are actually Small. **Correctly right-sized, not overstated**: this is a misleading claim in a recognition-only explanation, not a live wrong number — `compute_combat_baseline` has no size-modifier term for *any* race yet (confirmed during the earlier class-skill-bonus sweep), so nothing numeric is actually wrong today. Two independent pieces: (1) small/cheap — correct the misleading text, folded into backend's current race-bundle fix; (2) larger — actually wire a size modifier into AC/attack/CMB/Stealth, which overlaps the already-tracked, already-deferred AC/attack-bonus posture-narrowness architecture item (this doc's item 1) — not picked up now, noted as belonging alongside that existing tracking rather than as fresh scope.

17. **MAJOR ARCHITECTURE FINDING, now operator-directed to build — there is no feat-effects engine anywhere in the compute pipeline.** QA verified concretely, not reasoned abstractly: built a real `CharacterInput` from the GE-06 fixture (already `Computed`), added `feat:toughness` to `selected_feats` alongside the required trio, ran it through the real `build_pilot_headless_receipt` entry point, compared before/after. Status stayed `Computed` both times (the combat-posture gate only checks Dodge/Weapon Focus *presence*, no exact-feat-set restriction, so an extra feat doesn't risk breaking the build). Toughness genuinely present in `selected_feats` (checked directly). **HP was 12 both with and without Toughness** — checked via both `class_chassis.fighter.level_1_hit_points` and `durability::compute_max_hp` independently. Real PF1 Toughness is a flat +3 HP at level 1; the real value should be 15. Zero explanations anywhere mention "toughness." **Confirmed system-wide, not Toughness-specific**: grepped `pilot_compute.rs` for any `selected_feats` reference beyond the two hardcoded presence-checks (Dodge, Weapon Focus) in the posture gate — zero hits. There is no general feat-effects computation anywhere in this file; Power Attack, Dodge, and Weapon Focus are the only 3 feats with any mechanical consequence at all, and even those are hardcoded into one fixed deterministic posture, not a general mechanism. Every other selectable feat (of the 185-record CRB catalog the picker exposes) is purely recorded data with zero computed effect. **Correctly sized, not understated or overstated**: QA explicitly declined to presume the sizing call, but flagged this is architecturally the same shape as the already-deferred AC/attack-bonus/skill-posture item (this doc's item 1) — "the correct math doesn't exist yet, not just disconnected." **Not assigned to backend, not attempted this wave** — a general feat-effects engine is squarely future-epic scope, same bucket as the headless/corpus-aware architecture question. Flagged here for the operator's eventual review alongside item 1, since it materially affects how far v0.6 alpha actually is from bar item 3's "select feats" affordance being mechanically meaningful, not just UI-present.

    **Un-deferred and landed (backend, 2026-07-24) — operator directive: "easy enough, you need to create a feats engine," scoped to one real feat first.** New module `src/rules_core/feat_effects.rs`: `hp_bonus_from_feats(selected_feats: &[String]) -> i16` scans for grounded feat effects and sums them, scoped to exactly Toughness's real flat +3 HP for now (not a 185-feat resolver in one pass, per the operator's own scoping) — shaped to extend one feat/match-arm at a time later. **A real string-convention trap caught before it could ship silently wrong**: QA's original finding used `"feat:toughness"` (matching the three hardcoded fixed-loadout feats' bespoke `"feat:<name>"` id space), but traced the real live pipeline before writing the fix and found the actual value a live user's `selected_feats` entry contains is the raw catalog key, `"Toughness"` (verified through the full chain: `feat_data/general.rs`'s `FeatTableEntry.key`, `FeatCatalogEntryDto`'s verbatim passthrough with no transform, `CharacterSheet.tsx`'s `entry.key` sent directly as `featId`). Keying on `"feat:toughness"` (QA's test string, not a real production shape) would have compiled, passed a same-shaped unit test, and never fired for a single real user. `durability.rs`'s `compute_max_hp` signature is untouched (avoiding blast radius into the parity-comparator's own call site); `load_character_durability_at_root` (`character_hub.rs`) now adds the feat bonus on top of `compute_max_hp`'s result as a separate, explicit step. Genuine RED→GREEN: the wiring was temporarily disabled, the new test failed with the exact expected 12-vs-15 mismatch, then restored and confirmed green. 5 new lib tests (including one that explicitly proves the old `"feat:toughness"` string does NOT match, pinning the correct convention) + 1 new desktop test. 233/233 lib, 201/201 desktop. Widening to more feats is real future scope, not attempted here — the pattern is proven, not yet generalized.

    **Widened to 3 more feats (backend, 2026-07-24): Great Fortitude, Iron Will, Lightning Reflexes — each a real flat +2 to one named save.** Picked over Skill Focus (the other obvious candidate) after checking its catalog record directly: its `effect` qualifiers carry a `%LIST` placeholder, PCGen's own marker for a player-chosen target, so it needs to know *which* skill was picked — `selected_feats` has no slot to record that (the same shape of gap Weapon Focus's hardcoded compound id only works around for the one fixed-loadout case, not a general catalog pick). Left for a future slice rather than forced into this bounded pass. Unlike Toughness (wired into `durability.rs`'s separate, non-claim-gated command), these 3 extend `pilot_compute.rs`'s `compute_total_saves` directly — the claim-gated `total_saves` pillar itself. This does not reopen the item-1 architecture question: the non-claim-gated DTO-enrichment pattern (shape (c)) exists specifically for corpus-blocked math; feats are headless-accessible (`selected_feats` needs no corpus), so wiring straight into the claim-gated computation is the direct, correct fix — the same category as fixing items 14/20's silently-wrong values, not a workaround. `feat_effects::save_bonuses_from_feats` returns a small local `SaveBonusesFromFeats` struct (deliberately not reusing `pilot_compute::BaseSaves`, keeping `feat_effects.rs` a dependency-free leaf module). Genuine RED→GREEN: 7 new `feat_effects.rs` tests (each feat isolated, all three stacking, the real "Improved" variant feats correctly not matching) plus 2 new end-to-end `pilot_compute.rs` tests against the real Fighter fixture, wiring temporarily disabled to confirm the exact expected failure before restoring. 254/254 lib, 203/203 desktop.

    **Independently re-verified, clean, real ID chain re-traced end to end (QA, 2026-07-24).** Confirmed `"Toughness"` (not `"feat:toughness"`) is genuinely what a live pick sends by re-tracing the full chain themselves: `feat_data/general.rs`'s `key` field → `feat_catalog.rs`'s verbatim passthrough → `CharacterSheet.tsx`'s `entry.key` sent as `featId` in both `handleAddFeat` and `handleLevelUpFeatPick`. Confirmed additivity (exactly one call site for `hp_bonus_from_feats`, zero feat-related logic anywhere else in `compute_max_hp`/`explain_fighter_level1_hit_points` — no double-counting risk). Reproduced RED themselves (disabled the bonus term, watched the exact 12-vs-15 mismatch, restored).

    **3-feat widening (f38e9f33) independently re-verified, clean (QA, 2026-07-24).** Catalog keys re-confirmed directly against `feat_data/general.rs` rather than trusted from the commit message: `"Great Fortitude"`→`["SAVE","Fortitude","2"]`, `"Iron Will"`→`["SAVE","Will","2"]`, `"Lightning Reflexes"`→`["SAVE","Reflex","2"]`, all matching the code's constants and +2 magnitude exactly; the "Improved" variant feats used in the non-match test confirmed as real, distinct catalog entries (`effect: None`), not strawmen. Stacking confirmed non-double-counting: `save_bonuses_from_feats` has exactly one call site, applying each field to its own save only. No claim-gating risk: the bonus sits after `compute_total_saves`'s existing early-return, pure arithmetic on an already-decided path. RED→GREEN reproduced independently (swapped in `SaveBonusesFromFeats::default()`, got the exact expected `left: 4 / right: 6` failures on both new tests, restored). Ran clean: 7/7 new `feat_effects.rs` tests, 2/2 new `pilot_compute.rs` tests, 254/254 full lib suite. **Item 17 fully independently verified end to end, both slices, nothing outstanding.**

    **RESOLVED (frontend, `7360fe4a`) — the Defense tab doesn't refetch durability after a feat pick.** Was: `CharacterSheet.tsx`'s `durability` state only refetched on a `useEffect` keyed to `[props.row.characterId]`; neither `handleAddFeat` nor `handleLevelUpFeatPick` triggered a refetch. **Fixed**: a shared `refreshDurability()` helper now called after a successful feat grant on both paths, falling back to `null` on failure (same honest treatment as the initial per-character load). Live-verified both paths (HP 22/22→25/25 via the Feats-tab picker, then live update again via a level-up feat pick), no reselect needed. **Independently verified (QA, 2026-07-24)**: confirmed both call sites invoke `refreshDurability()`, correctly placed strictly after the `Blocked` early-return so it never fires on a rejected mutation; confirmed the failure-path fallback matches the initial-load pattern exactly (`catch { setDurability(null) }`, identical shape); confirmed no ordering/race risk (`await addFeatSelection` resolves, including its synchronous save, before the refetch fires). Live-UI leg attempted but inconclusive on an unrelated environment rendering issue (`libEGL`/DRI3 error, screenshots came back black despite a correctly-launched window) — correctly not chased further per standing practice, called clean on the strength of the code read, regression suite (69/69, typecheck clean), and the commit's own live-verified numbers.

    **OPERATOR DIRECTIVE (Todd Hintzmann, 2026-07-24) — build it, no longer deferred.** Explicit call: "easy enough, you need to create a feats engine." Un-deferred and added to the actionable backlog. Scope, per `future-epic-scoping.md`'s own sizing note (gap B): a per-feat effect resolver wired into HP/attack/skill/save totals for the CRB feat catalog — starting with a real, bounded slice (the feats with the most mechanical weight and highest chance of a real fixture to verify against, e.g. Toughness's flat +3 HP, since that's the exact gap QA's own verification already demonstrated) rather than all 185 records at once, following this swarm's established "prove one real case end-to-end before widening" discipline. **In progress (backend, 2026-07-24)** — dispatches resumed; backend landed item 7 first, now starting this one.

18. **RESOLVED, real precise gap identified (2026-07-24) — a documented Wizard spellbook ceiling (level 3) didn't fire at level 4, for non-Human Wizards specifically.** `pilot_compute.rs` documents `WIZARD_SPELLBOOK_SUPPORTED_MAX_LEVEL=3` with claim-blocking diagnostics expected past that level. Frontend live-drove an Elf Wizard through real level-ups 1→2→3→4 via the real `LevelUpDialog` — all four succeeded, disk-confirmed real progression each step (HP 8→14→20→26, BAB/Will climbing correctly). No `Blocked` wall appeared at level 4 as documented. Frontend sent backend the specifics with line numbers and an explicitly-unconfirmed guess (possibly the restrictive check only fires from a level-1-specific function, not the wider chassis-widening path that's since been built) — correctly didn't dig further into backend's file themselves. **Deliberately NOT logged as a bonus/positive finding yet, unlike the "any race" discoveries** — the ceiling may exist precisely because the compute formulas past level 3 were never validated as correct, in which case "unblocked" could mean level 4+ Wizards now silently produce wrong spell-slot/save-DC/whatever values instead of being honestly stopped, the same shape of problem as the class-skill-modifier bug (item 14) — just newly *exposed* rather than newly *introduced*. Backend asked to determine which it is (stale leftover check vs. a real validation gap) before this gets resolved either direction; if it's the latter, QA should spot-check the level 4 Wizard's actual computed values against real PF1/PCGen once backend clarifies what's reachable.

    **QA's static sweep (2026-07-24): the ceiling is correctly enforced in `pilot_compute.rs`, verified 3 ways, not assumed.** Systematically grepped all level-comparison gates, all `_MAX_LEVEL`/`SUPPORTED_MAX` constants, and all "only supported for"/"bounded to" comments — `WIZARD_SPELLBOOK_SUPPORTED_MAX_LEVEL` is the only sub-ceiling of its shape in the file, no other class has a hidden one like it. Confirmed the gate fires correctly three ways: (1) `unmet_wizard_spellbook_conditions` checks `level > WIZARD_SPELLBOOK_SUPPORTED_MAX_LEVEL` and pushes `claim_blocking: true` diagnostics; (2) `build_pilot_headless_receipt` derives overall `Blocked` status directly from `diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.claim_blocking)` — no parallel status logic that could diverge; (3) an existing test (`tests/sd13_wizard_level4_progression.rs`) already proves it fires at exactly level 4 through the real pipeline. Also checked the Tauri layer (`apply_level_up` has no bypassing path, defers entirely to the standard pipeline) and the reverse failure mode (all 11 classes' level gates use an identical, correct pattern; all `claim_blocking: false` diagnostics checked individually, none relate to a dropped threshold). **Conclusion: the engine-side enforcement is airtight** — if frontend's "didn't fire" symptom is real, it's very likely UI-side, matching the shape of the earlier render-staleness bug (server-correct, client looks wrong), not a second engine gap. QA and backend reconciling directly with frontend rather than duplicating investigation.

    **Root cause found (frontend, 2026-07-24): both QA's static analysis and the original live symptom were correct, from different angles.** The ceiling itself lives inside `explain_wizard_level1_prepared_spell_baseline` (`pilot_compute.rs:14950`), which has `if input.chosen.race_id != HUMAN_RACE_ID { return; }` immediately after its level guard — for any non-Human Wizard, the entire function, ceiling included, never runs at all. QA's sweep correctly found the enforcement airtight (true, for the Human path it traced); frontend correctly found no wall at level 4 for an Elf Wizard (true, because the whole function is skipped for non-Human). **Not a regression, and not "level 4+ silently computes wrong values" as originally feared**: BAB/saves/HP for a non-Human Wizard remain correct, live-verified, because those route through a separate chassis path that was already widened to be race-agnostic. **What's actually missing for non-Human Wizards**: the Wizard-specific spellbook validation (the ceiling itself never blocks them past level 3) and spell-save-DC grounding (lives in the same gated function) — both real, previously-invisible gaps that frontend's own "any race" discovery exposed, not something that discovery broke.

    **Empirically confirmed at every level, not just level 4 (backend, 2026-07-24).** Composed an Elf Wizard level 1 directly and called `build_pilot_headless_receipt` — status `Computed`, but ZERO explanations with a `class_chassis.wizard`/`class_spell.wizard` id prefix, zero wizard-spell diagnostics of any kind, structurally absent with no claim-blocking flag raised about the absence. Separately confirmed a Human Wizard leveled 1→4 correctly hits `Blocked` at level 4 exactly as documented — the ceiling works precisely as designed for the one race it's scoped to. So the original symptom wasn't level-4-specific at all: the entire spellbook-gate mechanism, ceiling included, never engages for a non-Human Wizard at *any* level, 1 through 20. **Correction to item 19's earlier framing**: the wizard-spell-save-DC fix from item 12 lives inside this same Human-gated function, so "Elf Wizard's spell save DC is shorted by 2" was itself imprecise — it isn't shorted, it's **absent entirely** for any non-Human Wizard (corrected here rather than left standing).

    **Decision (lead, 2026-07-24): documented as a deferred completeness gap, not fixed and not blocked.** Backend proposed two shapes — widen the function to all races (real future-epic effort, everything in it currently reasoned in Human-specific terms, would need per-race re-verification) or add a claim-blocking diagnostic for non-Human Wizards naming the absence (small, honest, but would **regress already-verified, disk-confirmed capability** — the swarm proved an Elf Wizard can add spells and reach `Computed` live, end to end). Neither is right: widening is out of scope for this wave, and blocking conflates a genuine **completeness gap** (wizard-specific spell math doesn't exist yet for non-Human, nothing is computed *wrong*) with a **correctness bug** (a value that's wrong, like the class-skill-modifier bug) — the same distinction item 17's feat-effects finding already established (a Fighter can select Toughness and reach `Computed` even though it does nothing; that's correctly not blocked either). Filed here alongside item 17 as the third tracked completeness/architecture gap (with the AC/attack-bonus item), not assigned to anyone this wave.

    **Lead correction (2026-07-24, ~14:40 ET) — briefly mis-dispatched to backend as active backlog after a context compaction; corrected before backend started.** This item's own decision two paragraphs up ("not fixed and not blocked... not assigned to anyone this wave") stands; SWARM_TASKS.md's row is corrected back to `blocked`/deferred, not `in progress`. No engineering work was actually done under the mistaken dispatch — caught by re-reading this doc before trusting the task-table summary alone. Backend independently caught the same contradiction from their own memory of this item's history before writing any code, and correctly stood down pending lead confirmation rather than picking a side themselves.

    **Scoping note, informational only, not a re-opening of scope (backend, 2026-07-24).** While getting oriented on the (mistaken) dispatch, backend read `explain_wizard_level1_prepared_spell_baseline` and its two callees before being told to stand down. Observation for whoever revisits this item's scope later: the widening effort may be smaller than the original decision estimated — the ~400-line function's only Human-specific content is the one `race_id != HUMAN_RACE_ID` race-gate line plus two cosmetic "Human Wizard" strings in explanation text; no formula in the body actually depends on race, since `ability_modifiers` already arrives race-adjusted via `apply_human_ability_bonus` upstream. Not verified beyond a read-through, not acted on, and does not by itself change this item's `blocked`/deferred status — logged here purely so the information isn't lost before an eventual real scope decision.

    **OPERATOR DECISION (Todd Hintzmann, 2026-07-24) — widen it now, given backend's smaller-than-feared scoping read.** Explicit call, made after the swarm-wide closure-readiness checkpoint (item 31): remove the `race_id != HUMAN_RACE_ID` gate in `explain_wizard_level1_prepared_spell_baseline` and re-verify the formulas for a non-Human Wizard end to end. Backend's scoping note (above) found only one race-gate line plus two cosmetic strings are actually Human-specific — no formula depends on race since `ability_modifiers` arrives pre-adjusted — but that was a read-through, not a full verification; backend should confirm this empirically (the same "prove it, don't reason about it" discipline this swarm has used throughout) before landing, including a real non-Human Wizard reaching `Computed` with correct spell-save-DC and the spellbook ceiling firing correctly past level 3. Dispatched to backend.

    **RESOLVED, landed and lead-verified (backend, `7c2d2ad2`, 2026-07-24).** Race gate removed from `explain_wizard_level1_prepared_spell_baseline`; the "Human Wizard" explanation strings generalized via a new `race_display_label` helper (derives the display name from `race_id`, same "derive, don't hardcode" pattern `class_summary_label` already used). Empirically verified exactly as required — 4 new tests through the real `build_pilot_headless_receipt` entry point, not just the narrower chassis function: an Elf Wizard reaches `Computed` with a correct racially-adjusted spell-save-DC at level 1, stays within the ceiling at level 3, and correctly hits `WIZARD_SPELLBOOK_SUPPORTED_MAX_LEVEL` at level 4 — all previously entirely absent for non-Human Wizards; Human Wizard behavior confirmed unchanged in the same pass. **Lead independently re-ran** the 4 new tests (pass), the full root-crate workspace suite (4275/4275, 0 failed, matches exactly — up from 4271 by the 4 new tests), and the desktop suite (204/204, unaffected — checked first since `character_hub.rs`/`pf1_adapter.rs` both reference this ceiling). Item 18 fully closed.

    **Independently re-verified, thorough full-function trace (QA, 2026-07-24).** Traced every formula in the function's full body (lines 15001–15412) by hand, not just the 4 new tests' coverage: confirmed BAB/saves/spell-slot-ladder/Scribe-Scroll/specialist-bonus/Intense-Spells are pure level-driven functions; confirmed `spell_save_dc` and `force_missile_uses_per_day` (the only two reading ability modifiers) receive an already race-adjusted `ability_modifiers.intelligence` (`apply_human_ability_bonus` passes non-Human scores through unchanged; the frontend bakes the real racial adjustment in before submission); confirmed `unmet_wizard_spellbook_conditions`/`ground_wizard_prepared_spellbook` have no race reference. Grepped the whole function for `race_id`/`HUMAN_RACE_ID`/`race:` — only hit is the new `race_display_label` call. Checked all 7 curated races through `race_display_label` (not just Elf), all correct PF1 naming, throwaway test reverted cleanly. Confirmed the "Human Wizard" string change is safe — grepped the tree for every `"Human Wizard"` hit, none are runtime string-matches against the live explanation text. **Reproduced RED themselves and surfaced a sharper finding than originally scoped**: restoring the old race gate didn't just lose the spell-save-DC — the Elf Wizard stayed `Computed` past the real spellbook ceiling at level 4 with zero ceiling diagnostic at all, meaning the pre-fix bug was a genuine correctness gap (enforcement silently never ran for non-Human), not merely a missing display value. Ran clean: 4/4 new tests, 258/258 full lib suite, 204/204 desktop — matches the commit's counts exactly.

    **Sweep for the same shape elsewhere (frontend, 2026-07-24): Wizard's gap does not repeat as a correctness issue, but one new question surfaced.** Checked all 11 `MAX_SUPPORTED_*_LEVEL` constants — `WIZARD_SPELLBOOK_SUPPORTED_MAX_LEVEL` is the only secondary/nested ceiling in the file, doesn't recur.
