Checked all 15 `if input.chosen.race_id != HUMAN_RACE_ID { return; }` occurrences: 2 are legitimately Human-only-named functions; 13 are per-class `explain_*` functions — 8 already match the known `human-diagnostics-only` pattern (expected, not new); of the 3 classes that reach `Computed` (Fighter/Wizard/Rogue), Rogue's `explain_rogue_level1_chassis` is race-gated the same way but only takes an `explanations` param, no `diagnostics` — structurally can't affect `Computed`/`Blocked`, so non-Human Rogues lose flavor-text grounding (Sneak Attack/Trapfinding/Evasion records) but nothing the frontend currently displays — cosmetic, not correctness. **One new genuine question**: Fighter's `validate_fighter_feat_choice_legality` (line 7867) is also race-gated *and* carries claim-blocking diagnostics for non-canonical feat choices — its own doc comment claims it's redundant with the separate, already-race-agnostic `unmet_combat_posture_conditions` gate ("any other posture is already claim-blocked upstream"). Frontend correctly did not assert this overlap themselves and flagged it for backend to trace — if the claim is wrong, a non-Human Fighter with an invalid feat choice could reach `Computed` when it shouldn't, the opposite failure direction from the Wizard gap (something that should Block but might not, vs. something absent). Backend investigating; frontend live-testing the same question from the UI side in parallel.

    **RESOLVED — the overlap claim was wrong; real, currently-reachable gap fixed (backend, 2026-07-24).** `unmet_combat_posture_conditions` only ever checked the level-1 Fighter bonus-feat slot; `CANONICAL_FIGHTER_FEAT_CHOICES` covers 17 slots total (levels 2–20 bonus feats plus 4 weapon-training groups), so the other 14+ slots had zero validation for any non-Human Fighter — not theoretical, already reachable today via `level_up_character`'s caller-supplied `additional_choices` with no other check catching it. **Confirmed empirically before fixing, not assumed**: built an Elf Fighter, gave it a wrong bonus-feat choice at level 2, got zero diagnostics — genuinely `Blocked`-worthy input silently passing. Fixed by removing the incorrect Human-only race gate from `validate_fighter_feat_choice_legality` — Fighter's own bonus-feat/weapon-training choices are Fighter class features, not Human-specific; the one genuinely race-coupled table entry is already self-limiting (absent for non-Human, existing "slot absent, skip" logic already handles it correctly). 2 new tests (the real gap now caught; canonical selection still passes for any race), full suite clean, zero blast radius. This is the opposite failure direction from item 18's Wizard gap — something that should have Blocked and now correctly does, rather than something absent that was reaching `Computed` unflagged.

    **Nuance, live-verified (frontend, 2026-07-24): the fix is correct but currently has no UI attack surface.** `compose_character_input` hardcodes the canonical values for both slots this check protects (`choice:level_1_character_feat → feat:power_attack`, `choice:fighter_bonus_feat → feat:weapon_focus:weapon:longsword`) unconditionally for every character — no create-form field or level-up affordance lets a player submit anything else for these two slots. Live-verified with a fresh Dwarf Fighter: `Computed`/`Saved` successfully, disk-confirmed identical canonical choice records to a Human Fighter, no fabricated third slot (the Human-only `choice:human_bonus_feat` entry correctly absent, not flagged as illegal — matches the validation function's own "absent slot is not fabricated" doc comment). So the divergent-choice scenario the check exists to catch can't currently be triggered by anyone, Human or non-Human, through the shipped UI — the gap backend closed is real and correct defense-in-depth at the command/API layer (`level_up_character`'s `additional_choices` parameter could still be invoked non-canonically by something other than today's UI), not an active user-visible bug today. Doesn't diminish the fix's value, just calibrates its actual current exposure for the record.

    **Second, independent gap in the same function, also confirmed and fixed (backend, `0eb9ea65`).** The gate used `supported_fighter_level` (single-class-only), so it skipped every Fighter+X multiclass mix entirely — regardless of race, even Human. Confirmed empirically before fixing: a Human Fighter1/Rogue3 with a wrong level-1 bonus-feat choice produced zero diagnostics. Fixed with a one-line swap to `fighter_level_in_mix`, the multiclass-aware equivalent already established elsewhere in the file (`wizard_level_in_mix`'s precedent) — no other change needed since the loop body never used the specific level value. 2 new tests, full suite clean. This closes both dimensions of the original wrong doc-comment claim: race and multiclass, same function, two independently-confirmed gaps. Very likely shares the same "no current UI attack surface" property as the race fix above (same hardcoded-canonical-values mechanism regardless of class-mix) — not separately re-verified, flagged for anyone who wants certainty later.

    **Systematic sweep, complete (backend, `68721ca0`): a third instance found and fixed, correctly risk-tiered as non-exploitable.** Checked all 4 `_legality`/`_conditions`/`validate_` gate functions that exist in `pilot_compute.rs` for the same brittleness. Found one more: `unmet_combat_posture_conditions`'s own Fighter bonus-feat sub-check had the identical blind spot (`supported_fighter_level` instead of `fighter_level_in_mix`) — confirmed empirically (the function's own `unmet` list came back empty for the multiclass scenario) before fixing. **Precisely risk-tiered, not overstated**: this instance was NOT actually exploitable at the system level, since `validate_fighter_feat_choice_legality` (already fixed for multiclass) independently catches the exact same slot — overall `Computed`/`Blocked` status was already correct either way. Fixed anyway as a real internal-consistency issue (two checks that shouldn't be able to silently diverge later), explicitly flagged as a different risk tier than the first two fixes. The other 2 candidate functions checked out clean: `unmet_wizard_spellbook_conditions` takes an already-resolved level as a parameter (caller already multiclass-aware); `unmet_selected_skill_posture_conditions` has no per-class level lookup at all. **Sweep complete — all 4 gate functions in the file now covered, no further instances found.**

19. **RESOLVED, MAJOR — CreateCharacterForm never actually submitted racial ability adjustments, for any of the 4 fixed-adjustment races, since the form was first built.** Frontend live-verified backend's Elf +2 Intelligence fix (`9ec0e036`) and found it didn't change the visible outcome: a fresh Elf Wizard's ABILITIES panel showed `INT 10 +0`, `DEX 14 +2`, `CON 14 +2` — none of Elf's three racial adjustments (+2 DEX, -2 CON, +2 INT) were actually applied. Root cause was entirely frontend-side, unrelated to any of backend's race-bundle explanation-text work: `CreateCharacterForm.tsx` computed `calculatedScore()` (raw + racial adjustment + allocation + age) for the on-screen "Calculated" preview column only, but submitted `rawScore()` — the unadjusted value — as the actual `abilityScores` in the create request. Confirmed against the engine's real contract via `pilot_compute.rs`'s own doc comment on `apply_human_ability_bonus`: every non-Human race's submitted score is expected to already bake in its racial adjustment (Human is the sole exception — pre-bonus base plus a server-side choice). The frontend never honored that contract, for any of the 4 races, independent of and predating this session's explanation-text fixes — those were correct about what a race's adjustment *should* be, just describing a number the frontend was never actually sending. **Fixed** (`f2c616ed`): new `applyRacialAbilityAdjustments()` in `composeCreateCharacterRequest.ts`, wired into the submission path, no-op for Human, 3 new regression tests. **Live re-verified end-to-end**: a fresh Elf Wizard now saves `dexterity:16, constitution:12, intelligence:12` (disk-confirmed) and the sheet shows the full correct cascade — DEX +3/CON +1/INT +1, Knowledge skills +1, DEX-based skills +3, AC/saves all shifted correctly. Since this is the same code path for all 4 races, QA re-verifying Dwarf/Gnome/Halfling now get correct submitted scores too, same rigor as the original race sweep — this closes the loop on the full 4-race gap (recognition-text fix + submission-bug fix + live verification), not just the explanation-text half.

    **Dwarf/Gnome/Halfling re-verified — real production-code execution, short of full live-UI proof due to an environment blocker (QA, 2026-07-24).** Confirmed `applyRacialAbilityAdjustments` is called unconditionally regardless of race (no race-specific branching at the call site) and that `RACE_OPTIONS`' catalog data has carried the correct full 3-stat adjustment for all three races since the file was first authored (checked via git history, independent of and predating backend's engine-side text fix). **Executed the real production code, not a reimplementation**: imported the actual function and catalog directly and confirmed Dwarf (CON+2/WIS+2/CHA-2), Gnome (CON+2/CHA+2/STR-2), and Halfling (DEX+2/CHA+2/STR-2) all compute correctly. **Found a real, separate gap while there**: `composeCreateCharacterRequest.test.ts`'s regression coverage is incomplete — the Dwarf test only asserts unrelated stats stay untouched, never checks the actual adjusted CON/WIS/CHA values, and Gnome/Halfling have zero coverage at all (only Elf got full treatment). Correctly filed to frontend rather than edited (frontend's file). **Could not complete the full live-UI-then-disk-confirm verification** matching frontend's methodology — the desktop app environment was consistently unstable (webview renders solid black, process/Xvfb pair dies within seconds, ~6 attempts, ruled out as a resource issue with 17GB free) — flagged honestly as an environment blocker rather than either fabricating a live-verified claim or silently giving up. **Accepted as sufficient** given the strength of the evidence (real code, real data, mechanically identical to the already-fully-verified Elf path) — not re-attempted further. Worth watching whether this environment instability is session-wide (frontend's capstone walkthrough uses the same GUI environment).

20. **RESOLVED — `skill_allocation.rs`'s class-skill recognition was Fighter-only, silently leaving Wizard's and Rogue's cross-class rank cap unenforced.** Dispatched as a bounded widening task following backend's own scoping finding (item 1) that class-skill-list recognition is a labor problem, not an architecture wall. Turned out to be more than pure widening: confirmed empirically before fixing that a level-1 Wizard could allocate 5 ranks to a cross-class skill (real PF1 cap is 1) with zero enforcement — because neither Wizard nor Rogue had ANY grounded class-skill posture in this module, `has_grounded_class_skill_posture` was false for both, so the cross-class half-cap logic never engaged at all, not just "no class-skill bonus." Same failure shape as item 14 (a silently wrong number, no claim-blocking diagnostic), but on rank enforcement rather than a modifier value. **Fixed (`21f815c1`)**, grounded against the real PCGen corpus: Rogue gets all 5 of this module's bounded skills (Climb/Intimidate/Swim/Diplomacy/Disable Device) as real class skills (`cr_abilities_class.lst:2838`, verified exact match); Wizard gets a genuinely empty grounded list (`cr_abilities_class.lst:2565`, checked zero overlap, not assumed) — but even the empty grounding matters, since it's what flips the cross-class cap from unenforced to correctly enforced for Wizard. Scoped to Wizard/Rogue only, same bounded-caution philosophy as the existing Fighter posture. 4 new tests, full suite clean, zero blast radius (QA's existing `skill_allocation` tests never touched Wizard/Rogue at all). QA to independently verify.

    **QA's independent catalogue coverage landed (`d35521ec`, lead stewardship during a quota outage).** QA wrote real fixture-driven tests (through the actual `load_character_input_fixture` parser, not hand-built structs) re-verifying both PCGen citations independently and cross-checking against the separate `compute_selected_skill_modifiers` fix's own fixture to prove the two modules now agree on Wizard's class-skill list. Finished but uncommitted when QA hit a session quota limit; the lead verified it (3/3 pass, full workspace suite clean) before landing it on QA's behalf rather than risk losing finished work during the outage.

21. **RESOLVED, SIGNAL FINDING — systematic sweep for other Fighter-only-grounded computations came back clean, one real near-miss traced and ruled out.** Following the pattern that found items 14 and 20 (widening Fighter-only recognition surfacing silent bugs for Wizard/Rogue), backend swept `pilot_compute.rs`, `skill_allocation.rs`, `durability.rs`, and `money.rs` for any remaining instance of the same shape. **One candidate investigated and ruled out**: `explain_rogue_level1_chassis` gates on the single-class-only `supported_rogue_level` (no `rogue_level_in_mix` counterpart), superficially the same shape as items 14/20's root cause — but tracing where its output goes shows its explanations (sneak-attack-die-count, trapfinding, Evasion, Trap Sense, Uncanny Dodge) are standalone, never wired into `compute_fighter_chassis`, `compute_total_saves`, or `compute_combat_baseline`, unlike Fighter's Bravery/bonus-feat progression which feeds real feat-choice validation. No downstream consumer exists, so for a multiclass build missing this record, nothing is silently wrong and no rule goes unenforced — same cosmetic-only shape already established for non-Human Rogue in item 18's sweep, not a new correctness bug. **Everything else checked out clean**: the other `FIGHTER_CLASS_ID` sites in `pilot_compute.rs` are deliberate, already-widened 3-class dispatch points with scope-decision doc comments; `unmet_selected_skill_posture_conditions` was already widened pre-swarm (SD-21 E6b.1); `supported_fighter_level`'s one remaining Fighter-only use (armor training) is correctly Fighter-exclusive, a real class feature, not a generalization gap; `durability.rs` is fully class-generic with an explicit Fighter/Wizard/Rogue-parity test; `money.rs` has no class branching at all, out of scope for this pattern. **Backend's own read, adopted by the lead**: this is a genuine signal, not an absence of looking — the swarm's remaining distance from the alpha bar is now confirmed architecture-level (items 1, 17, 18), not further silent-correctness bugs in the three working classes' shared computation paths.

22. **RESOLVED — `characterProgression.ts` now has real regression coverage.** Landed (`ca25bfef`): new `characterProgression.test.ts` covers `parseHeldClasses` (including the exact multiclass regression case `d03bc89d` fixed, plus an unknown-class-id fallback), `formatHeldClasses`, `totalCharacterLevel`, `casterLevel`, `classSkillPointsBase`, `classHitDie`, `classWeaponProficiency`, `maxHitPoints` (both single-class and a multiclass case verifying only the character's true first-ever level maximizes its hit die), `totalSkillPoints`, `buildLevelEntries`, `buildNextEntries`, and `previewLevelUp`. 63/63 suite green. Genuine RED caught along the way: frontend's own first-draft assumption about the title-case regex (that underscores would become spaces) was wrong — `_` counts as a `\w` word character, so the whole segment is one contiguous match and only the leading letter capitalizes — caught when the test failed and fixed to match real behavior, not adjusted to match the wrong assumption.

23. **RESOLVED — LevelUpDialog now collects a real feat pick at feat-gaining levels.** QA verified `e8e45976` (LevelUpDialog wiring) and found the mechanics clean, but flagged a real, understated-scope gap: the excusing comment about no feat catalog was stale, and Fighter 1→2 genuinely should offer a bonus-feat pick and didn't. **Fixed (`ddfc66bb`)**: new `levelGrantsFeat(features)` pure predicate in `characterProgression.ts` (covers the universal odd-level feat, Fighter's bonus combat feat, and Wizard's periodic bonus feat, all already represented as feature strings in the existing table). When a level grants a feat, `handleLevelUpAccept` defers and opens the exact same feat `ItemPickerModal` the Feats-tab "Add Feat" button uses (same catalog, no second picker built), retitled "Pick a feat — level N"; picking one persists the level-up first, refreshes so the sheet is never stale, then grants the feat via the real `addFeatSelection` command. Cancelling aborts the whole level-up rather than leaving a half-applied state. When a level grants no feat, behavior is byte-identical to before. **Real deviation from the original suggestion, found by testing, not assumed**: frontend tried also recording the pick via `additionalChoices` (mirroring the hit-die choice) and the backend rejected it outright — `local_store.rs`'s round-trip grammar requires at least two colon-segments in a `selection_id`, which `hp:average` happens to satisfy and a real feat catalog key like "Cleave" never will. Since `additionalChoices` is inert anyway (nothing gates on it) and the real grant lives entirely in `chosen.selected_feats` via `addFeatSelection`, the doomed provenance entry was correctly dropped rather than breaking every real feat pick to satisfy a decorative record. **Live-verified both branches end to end**: a Dwarf Fighter 1→2 got the picker, picked Cleave, confirmed on disk and in the Feats tab (HP recomputed to 22/22); a Human Wizard 1→2 (no feat at that level) leveled up with no interruption, exactly as before. New test (`verifiesLevelGrantsFeatDetectsEveryRealFeatSource`) covers all three real feat sources plus a true no-feat level. 63/63 suite green, typecheck clean.

24. **RESOLVED — PCGen parity comparator gained a new dimension, `combat.base_attack_bonus`.** Backend's self-directed scan (following up on the earlier DTO-consumption sweep) found the comparator only ever exposed `combat.baseline_melee_attack_bonus` (the Strength/feat-inclusive total), never the raw class-table BAB PCGen's own export template carries separately (`<attack><melee><bab>`). Verified empirically before implementing — ran the real PCGen pipeline against the Fighter fixture, confirmed `<bab>+1</bab>` matches Codex's own value. Landed across the full stack (`cda3bf1c` + QA's catalogue adoption `b8eff433`): `SelectedParityDimensions::from_receipt`/`from_pilot_receipt`, `pcgen-normalize-output.py` extraction (deliberately from `/character/attack/melee/bab`, not the confusingly-named sibling element — documented why), a new Python test file (RED→GREEN), 2 new Rust lib tests. **Real end-to-end confirmation**: both `sd26_pilot_case_verification.rs` and `v06_wizard_pilot_case_verification.rs` (which drive the actual PCGen pipeline) show genuine agreement — Fighter BAB 1==1, Wizard BAB 0==0 (correctly reflecting Wizard's poor BAB progression at level 1). Cross-surface protocol followed correctly: backend held the commit until QA landed the 4 mechanically-affected test files (`b8eff433`), then pushed. Small hygiene fix rode along: added a Python section to `.gitignore` (`__pycache__/`, `*.pyc`) since the new Python test file was the first thing to need it.

    **Second-pass scan, clean, real negative result (backend, 2026-07-24).** Went a level deeper on the same method (check `base-xml.ftl` directly for other exportable-but-unexposed fields) against everything Codex actually computes: touch/flat-footed AC and AC sub-modifiers (PCGen exports them, but Codex only ever computes one aggregate `baseline_armor_class` — new engineering, not wiring, same bucket as items 1/17/18); initiative (explicitly out of scope per `pilot_compute.rs`'s own doc comment, not computed anywhere); a skill-point pool total (zero hits anywhere in `rules_core` — only individual ranks/modifiers exist); encumbrance sub-fields like lift/push/drag capacity (`encumbrance.rs`'s `CarryingCapacityThresholds` only has light/medium/heavy, already fully exposed). DR was the one candidate Codex does compute but, unlike BAB, has no live PCGen fixture yet to verify against (no Barbarian pilot case exists) — not a same-day wiring win the way BAB was. Also swept every owned production file for TODO/FIXME/XXX/"unwired" markers — zero hits, consistent with the no-stub discipline held all session. Genuine clean result, not an absence of looking — this closes out the comparator-scan thread the same way item 21 closed the correctness-bug thread.

25. **RESOLVED — the frontend test-coverage backlog pattern is closed.** Was: multiple frontend logic modules shipped with sound reasoning but zero dedicated test coverage. QA verified `75200fcb` (SkillAllocationDialog wiring) and found the logic itself clean: `skillIdFor`'s "only 5 confirmed skill ids" claim matches `skill_key_ability_modifier` in `skill_allocation.rs` exactly; unrecognized skill ids are genuinely inert (a silent `continue`, no fabrication, no rejection); `handleSkillAllocationAccept` correctly checks `blocked` and returns before touching local state. But grepping every `.test.ts` in the tree for `skillIdFor`/`setSkillAllocations` turns up nothing — the 30-of-35 skill-id mappings beyond the 5 the engine actually recognizes have no regression test pinning them down.

    **Fourth instance confirmed (QA, `94a38657`, Bio editor wiring) — pattern is now systemic, no longer flagged individually.** Bio editor's own logic verified clean too: on-blur-not-per-keystroke persistence confirmed across all 8 fields, the character-switch `useEffect` has a real `cancelled`-guard against a stale in-flight load landing after a switch (not just claimed), and load/save failure handling is correctly non-load-bearing (bio is flavor text) vs. surfaced through the standard mutation-error banner. Same gap: zero test files reference `characterBio.ts`/`updateCharacterBio`/`loadCharacterBio`. That's 4 for 4 (`characterProgression.ts` — now resolved as item 22 —, `skillIdFor`/`setSkillAllocations`, the stale LevelUpDialog comment's underlying module, and now Bio) — QA's own judgment, adopted: this is a real, systemic gap across this wave of frontend persistence-wiring commits, not four independent coincidences, and not worth re-flagging one at a time going forward. **Owner: frontend, backlog** — a deliberate pass across `characterHub/` for modules with this shape (sound logic, zero dedicated test file) once the current queue (item 23 — since resolved — and any further items QA's sweep surfaces) is clear, rather than four-plus piecemeal fixes.

    **Self-audit complete, pattern fully closed (frontend, `0c602c72`, 2026-07-24).** Diffed exactly the files this swarm created/touched on `tranche/6` (`git diff $(git merge-base develop tranche/6) tranche/6 -- apps/desktop/src/`, matching this item's own "this wave of frontend persistence-wiring commits" framing) and checked each non-test file for a sibling `.test.ts`. Found and closed 8 in `boundary/` with real logic and zero coverage: the standard no-Tauri-runtime-throws case for `addFeatSelection.ts`/`listFeats.ts`/`loadCreateCharacter.ts`/`loadSavedCharacterDetail.ts`/`purchaseEquipment.ts`/`recordAndPrepareSpellSelection.ts` (same template as the existing `addSpellSelection.test.ts`); `characterDurability.ts` (both functions throw with no runtime, no "resolve to default" case unlike bio/money); `characterMoney.ts` (`gpToCopper`'s real conversion logic including the fractional-copper rounding case, plus its own asymmetric load/adjust behavior). Checked everything else this swarm touched (`characterHub/*.ts`, `testSupport/makeSurface.ts`) and found nothing further — already covered, a React component (no jsdom in this runner, an established non-goal), or fixture/test-support data rather than logic. **Lead-verified**: 77/77 test files passing (up from 69), typecheck clean, sampled `characterMoney.test.ts`/`characterDurability.test.ts` directly — real, specific assertions against real behavior, not scaffolding. **Item 25 fully closed, not partial** — no remaining candidates in frontend's ownership.

    **Independently sample-reviewed, clean (QA, 2026-07-24).** Read source-then-test for the 6 files the lead hadn't personally sampled (`addFeatSelection`/`listFeats`/`purchaseEquipment`/`recordAndPrepareSpellSelection`/`loadCreateCharacter`/`loadSavedCharacterDetail`). Confirmed each is a thin `invoke()` wrapper with no client-side logic beyond its `hasTauriRuntime()` guard (e.g. `listFeats`'s real filtering is server-side in Rust), so the no-runtime-throws test is genuinely the full available surface, not a shortcut — and confirmed it matches the pre-existing `addSpellSelection.test.ts`/`addEquipmentSelection.test.ts` convention byte-for-byte, not a new pattern invented for this batch. Noted `recordAndPrepareSpellSelection.test.ts` uses the real `WIZARD_STARTER_SPELL_ID` value (`'Light'`), not an arbitrary placeholder. Reproduced a real regression on one file (`purchaseEquipment.test.ts`): temporarily broke the source's error string, watched the test fail with the exact expected mismatch, restored byte-identical — confirms the assertions are load-bearing. All 6 pass individually; full suite 77/77 matches the lead's count. Nothing to flag.

    **Scoping pass (QA, 2026-07-24), not a build — turns "there's a pattern" into a concrete checklist frontend can execute directly.** One more targeted grep (every `characterHub/*.ts`/`boundary/*.ts` file without a sibling `.test.ts`) turned up two more real candidates beyond the three already named (`skillsModel.ts`/`setSkillAllocations.ts`, `characterBio.ts`, and the Wizard spell-routing logic): `abilityScoreMethods.ts` (real dice/point-buy logic, untested) and `portraitImageProcessing.ts` (Canvas/File-API logic, lower priority — needs a DOM shim this test runner doesn't have, different treatment than the rest). `previewData.ts` was checked and is data-only (a fixture, not logic) — not added to this list.

    - **`skillsModel.ts` / `boundary/setSkillAllocations.ts`** — the 30-of-35 unconfirmed mappings named above. Concrete cases: (1) `skillIdFor` on a parenthetical skill name (e.g. `"Knowledge (Arcana)"` → `"skill:knowledge_arcana"`, not a double-underscore or trailing-underscore artifact — the regex's edge case); (2) `skillIdFor` on a multi-word non-parenthetical name (`"Sense Motive"` → `"skill:sense_motive"`, `"Sleight of Hand"` → `"skill:sleight_of_hand"`); (3) `isClassSkill` for at least one class/skill pair per confirmed-vs-unconfirmed boundary (a class that has the skill, a class that doesn't, and — if feasible — a case exercising the multiclass union); (4) `boundary/setSkillAllocations.ts` itself only needs the standard no-Tauri-runtime-throws case every other boundary file already has (see `addSpellSelection.test.ts` for the exact template).
    - **`boundary/characterBio.ts`** — thin wrapper, same shape as every other tested boundary file: (1) `loadCharacterBio` outside a Tauri runtime resolves to `BLANK_CHARACTER_BIO` rather than throwing (the one behavior specific to this file vs. the others — bio's absent-case is a resolved default, not an error); (2) `updateCharacterBio` outside a Tauri runtime throws the exact descriptive message, matching the `addSpellSelection.test.ts` template.
    - **Wizard spell-routing logic** — currently inline in `CharacterSheet.tsx`'s `handleAddSpell`, not an extractable pure function today (unlike the other three, which already have clean module boundaries). Recommend extracting the two decisions it makes — (a) resolve `primaryClassId` preferring a held Wizard over `heldClasses[0]`, (b) choose `recordAndPrepareSpellSelection` vs. plain `addSpellSelection` based on that class id — into one small pure function (e.g. alongside `skillsModel.ts` or a new `spellRoutingModel.ts`), then test: a Fighter-only build routes to plain `addSpellSelection`; a Fighter/Wizard multiclass resolves to Wizard (not `heldClasses[0]`) and routes to the atomic path; a Wizard-only build routes to the atomic path. This is a slightly bigger lift than the other three (needs the extraction first) — flagging that distinction rather than treating all four as equal-sized tasks.
    - **`abilityScoreMethods.ts`** (found this pass, not previously named) — real, currently-untested logic: `pointBuyCost` at the table boundaries (7 and 18) and one in-range value; `generateAbilityScorePool` for each non-random `kind: 'pool'` method returns the right fixed values (`eliteArray` → `[15,14,13,12,10,8]` exactly) and the right *count* (6) for the randomized ones without asserting exact values; `rollStraightAbilityScores` maps its six rolls onto `ABILITY_KEYS` in the documented Str/Dex/Con/Int/Wis/Cha order, not any other order. The reroll-if-weak retry loop's 200-attempt cap is a defensive bound, not itself easily testable without mocking `Math.random` — reasonable to leave unverified rather than force a brittle test around it.
    - **`portraitImageProcessing.ts`** — lower priority, flagged for completeness rather than urged: the pure validation logic (`ACCEPTED_PORTRAIT_TYPES.includes`, the size-limit check) is testable today with a plain fake `File` object and no DOM, but the actual crop/resize path needs a `<canvas>`, which this `tsx`-based test runner doesn't provide — either accept partial coverage (just the two validation early-returns) or treat as out of scope for this backlog pass.

    **Not built this pass, deliberately** — this is a checklist, not new test files; QA's lane is `tests/**` (Rust), these are all frontend `.ts` modules. Owner: frontend, whenever the current queue is clear.

    **Checklist fully executed (frontend, `f905d656`).** All five modules covered per the exact cases named above: `skillsModel.ts` (parenthetical/multi-word normalization, class-skill boundary, multiclass union), `boundary/setSkillAllocations.ts` + `boundary/characterBio.ts` (standard no-runtime-throws template, Bio's absent-case-resolves-to-default behavior specifically verified), Wizard spell-routing (genuinely extracted into a new `spellRoutingModel.ts::resolveSpellRouting`, all 3 named cases plus 2 more — an already-bootstrapped Wizard routing to the plain path, zero-held-classes resolving to `null` rather than a fabricated id), `abilityScoreMethods.ts` (point-buy boundaries, `eliteArray`'s exact values, Str/Dex/Con/Int/Wis/Cha key order verified via insertion order), and `portraitImageProcessing.ts`'s two DOM-free validation cases as scoped. Since the spell-routing extraction touched real shipped code (`CharacterSheet.tsx`'s `handleAddSpell` now calls the new pure function instead of its old inline logic), frontend treated it as non-risk-free and live-verified it against the real dev build: adding a spell to an already-bootstrapped Wizard persisted correctly through the plain path, no stray Prepared entry, correct on-disk shape. 6 new test files, 1 new source file, 69/69 suite green, typecheck clean.

26. **RESOLVED — Load Character list now refreshes its cached row on every sheet mutation.** Found by frontend while live-verifying item 23. Root cause (`2b13a23c`): `CharacterHubPage.tsx`'s `onDetailRefreshed` already rebuilt the open sheet's own `row` from the mutation's fresh summary, but the separate list `surface` state never got the same treatment — only a full reload refreshed it. Fixed with `replaceRowInSurface(surface, updatedRow)` in `buildCharacterHubListSurface.ts` (in-place patch by `characterId`, no-ops on an unknown id rather than fabricating a row), called alongside the existing `sheet.row` patch in the same callback — one mutation, both pieces of state updated together, no extra IPC round-trip. 2 new unit tests (matching-row update, unknown-id no-op). Live-verified: leveling a Wizard from 1→2 and reopening the Load list immediately showed "Elf Wizard 2," no app restart needed. 69/69 suite green, typecheck clean.

27. **OPERATOR QUESTION (surfaced by backend's item-1 design pass, 2026-07-24) — should `Computed` ever accept an equipment loadout other than the one exact hardcoded posture?** Not an engineering call: `unmet_combat_posture_conditions`/`unmet_selected_skill_posture_conditions` gate on exact `item_id` matches (`"item:longsword"`, `"item:chain_shirt"`), completely independent of whether the compute engine could correctly handle other gear. This means bridging the headless/corpus-aware architecture wall (item 1's fix shapes a/b) buys **zero** currently-displayed value on its own — every build that reaches `Computed` today already uses only the one hardcoded posture, so widening what the math *could* compute changes nothing unless this gate is separately widened to accept other item ids. This is a claim-gating philosophy question (how much loadout variation is "supported enough" to honestly claim `Computed`, given the no-stub/never-persist-unproven-build discipline this whole swarm runs on) — distinct from and prerequisite to any value from shapes (a)/(b). Shape (c) (backend's recommendation, see item 1) sidesteps this entirely by surfacing corpus-derived AC/ACP as a non-claim-gated display section instead of trying to widen the gate. Not resolved here; genuinely the operator's call.

    **OPERATOR DECISION (Todd Hintzmann, 2026-07-24) — widen the gate to accept any equipment.** Explicit call, made after the swarm-wide closure-readiness checkpoint (item 31): `unmet_combat_posture_conditions`/`unmet_selected_skill_posture_conditions` should stop gating on exact hardcoded `item_id`s and instead validate whatever loadout is actually selected. This is real, substantial engineering scope — effectively item 1's original fix shapes (a)/(b) (thread corpus through the compute pipeline, or move the claim gate to the corpus-aware layer), not a quick flip, and every equipment/weapon combination that newly reaches `Computed` needs to be verified genuinely correct, not just unblocked. **Not dispatched as a single bounded task** — given the scale (backend's own item-1 design doc already sized shape (a) at ~347 call sites), backend should first produce a scoping/sequencing plan (same shape as `item-1-architecture-wall-design.md`) before implementation starts, so this doesn't turn into an unbounded, un-verifiable mega-change. This decision also directly informs item 1's multi-weapon question below (an equipment-attachment schema is needed either way).

    **SCOPE DISCOVERY, backend stopped before coding (2026-07-24) — the gate can't be swapped in isolation without regressing into fabricated values.** Before writing sub-task 4, backend traced `unmet_combat_posture_conditions`/`unmet_selected_skill_posture_conditions`'s own gated functions (`compute_combat_baseline`'s `baseline_armor_class`/`baseline_melee_attack_bonus`, `compute_selected_skill_modifiers`'s climb/intimidate/swim) and found they are hardcoded arithmetic on compile-time constants (`ARMOR_CLASS_BASE + CHAIN_SHIRT_ARMOR_BONUS + ... `), not real per-item corpus resolution — zero corpus access anywhere in either function, by construction (the headless layer has no corpus parameter). **Why this matters**: naively widening only the gate check while leaving the arithmetic untouched would let a character in, say, a Breastplate instead of the fixed Chain Shirt reach `Computed` and then get served the *exact same hardcoded* numbers regardless of what they actually have equipped — not a completeness gap but a fabricated-value regression, the exact failure mode the no-stub-mvp doctrine exists to catch. Backend correctly asked whether this now needs the operator's eyes since it reads as a correctness question, not just an engineering one.

    **Lead ruling: no operator escalation needed — this is a doctrine-mandated implementation detail of the already-made decision, not a new policy question.** The operator decided the *policy* (widen the gate, replace it outright); they never authorized shipping fabricated numbers to get there, and the no-stub-mvp doctrine (`scope: universal`, standing, not re-litigated per-decision) already forbids it regardless of who decides. There is only one correct way to implement the operator's decision without violating that doctrine: move the arithmetic to real corpus-resolved computation. **Approved sequencing** (backend's own proposal): (1) new corpus-aware `baseline_armor_class`/`baseline_melee_attack_bonus`, sourced from `compute_equipment_effects`'s already-real `armor_class_delta`/`armor_check_penalty_total`/`attack_bonus_delta`/`to_hit_bonus` (sub-tasks 1/2's own output) — verified byte-identical against today's hardcoded formula for the existing fixed posture, which doubles as both the compatibility check and the correctness proof the corpus-resolved path is right; (2) same treatment for the selected-skill pillar; (3) only then swap the command-layer gate to consult the new path. `base_attack_bonus`/`base_saves` are unaffected (already class/level-generic, not posture-gated). Sub-task 4 is now meaningfully bigger than originally scoped — flagged here plainly rather than quietly absorbed, per this doc's own recurring practice.

    **Further boundary drawn, first slice scoped to armor/shield only (backend, 2026-07-24, lead-approved).** A second hardcoded dependency surfaced: `fighter_weapon_training_attack_bonus` (Weapon Training's contribution to melee attack bonus) is gated on the "Heavy Blades" weapon-training group, hardcoded because the fixed posture's Longsword happens to be a Heavy Blade. Widening the *weapon* to any resolvable item would require mapping an arbitrary weapon to its real PF1 weapon-training group — a distinct, real data-mapping problem, not something already built. **This slice's boundary**: widen armor/shield only (any resolvable `arms_armor`-category item or genuine absence, real `armor_class_delta`/`max_dex_cap`/`armor_check_penalty_total`), leave the weapon requirement exactly as today (must still be the Longsword, keeping Weapon Training's group-matching valid) and feat requirements exactly as today (a separate, undecided question from the already-settled "never block on an unrecognized feat *effect*" policy). Still a real, meaningful widening — a Fighter could wear Breastplate/Full Plate or go shieldless and get genuinely correct AC. Weapon-loadout widening is a distinct, later increment. Same "prove the narrow case first" discipline as Toughness-before-widening and the single-weapon-before-per-weapon slice; lead agrees, no operator input needed.

    **Both pillars built and lead-verified, not yet committed (backend, 2026-07-24).** New `compute_combat_baseline_from_corpus`/`compute_selected_skill_modifiers_from_corpus` in `pilot_compute_corpus.rs`, real per-item resolution via `compute_equipment_effects`. Exposed ~29 previously-private `pilot_compute.rs` helpers/constants as `pub(crate)` to avoid duplicating the level-based Weapon/Armor Training math and posture constants — verified via `git diff` that each is a pure visibility-prefix addition, zero behavior change. **The correctness proof**: one test drives both the old hardcoded path and the new corpus-aware path through the identical fixed posture at every Fighter armor/weapon-training breakpoint (levels 1/3/5/7/9/11/15/20) plus Wizard and Rogue level 1, asserting byte-identical `melee_attack_bonus`/`armor_class`/`climb`/`intimidate`/`swim` — passes with no discrepancy. 7 more tests prove the real widening: a Breastplate computes its own correct AC; an equipped shield (previously required-absent) adds its real bonus; no armor computes a real unarmored AC; a real enhancement attached to the required Longsword (connecting sub-tasks 1/2/6 end to end) raises the attack bonus by its exact real value; a second weapon and an unresolvable item are both honestly `Blocked`, not guessed. **Lead independently ran** the new 8-test module (`corpus_aware_posture_widening_tests`): 8/8 pass, matches exactly. Not yet wired into any command (sub-task 5) or committed as of this check.

    **Landed (`6ef7f3ef`, 2026-07-24), lead independently re-confirmed with a fresh full-workspace run.** 4292/4292 root-crate tests (up from 4284 — 8 new), 211/211 desktop, both matching backend's own report exactly. Sub-task 4 fully closed; sub-task 5 (the actual command-layer gate swap) is next.

    **Sub-task 5 landed (`350e6566`, 2026-07-24) — the re-verification bar caught a real regression before it shipped, exactly as intended.** New chokepoint `resolve_unified_pilot_snapshot` (`pf1_adapter.rs`) replaces every command's own inline exact-posture-only check; `combat.baseline_unsupported`/`skill.selected_modifier.unsupported` are no longer independently claim-blocking (the sub-task 4 corpus-aware pillars decide those two now, for any resolvable armor/shield loadout), every other headless diagnostic still blocks exactly as before. Wired into all 7 real call sites sharing this pattern: `mutate_saved_character_at_root` (the shared chokepoint for level-up/add-equipment/add-spell/append), `create_character_at_root`, `clone_character`, `import_character`, both copies of `load_saved_character` (found a genuine pre-existing duplication between the `RuleSystemAdapter` trait impl and the direct Tauri command — not introduced by this sub-task, fixed while already there), and `recompute_character_at_root`. **The real discrepancy, caught and fixed, not shipped**: the first full desktop run came back with 9 failures. Root cause: sub-task 4's implementation required *every* `EquippedActive` selection to resolve against corpus with a known category — over-strict against the real desktop app, whose bundled demo corpus has only 2 records, and many existing tests equip a harmless second item (a Dagger, a Wand) the old exact-posture gate never cared about (it only checked its 3 hardcoded items). The new requirement turned a previously-harmless extra item into a hard block. **Fixed** by removing the requirement entirely — `compute_equipment_effects` already tolerates an unresolvable selection gracefully (skips it, contributes nothing), so the new pillars trust that same existing tolerance instead of re-blocking on top of it. The one real constraint that survives is genuine 2-real-weapon ambiguity, already naturally expressed via `attack_bonus_delta`'s existing `None` case. **Lead independently re-verified**: full workspace 4292/4292 (unchanged from sub-task 4, as expected — a wiring change, not new tests), desktop 211/211 (0 failures, confirming the fix), and specifically re-ran the 8-test `corpus_aware_posture_widening_tests` module directly — all 8 pass, including `matches_the_hardcoded_baseline_exactly_for_every_currently_computed_build` (the byte-identical proof) and a renamed test (`an_unresolvable_equipped_item_does_not_block_and_contributes_nothing`, updated from its sub-task-4 name to reflect the regression fix). **Sub-tasks 1 through 5 of the items-1+27 plan are now all fully closed.** Only sub-task 3 (operator-skipped) remains formally open on that plan, plus any further frontend attachment-UI polish.

    **Sub-task 6 landed both halves and lead-verified (backend `3a05539a` + frontend `a768ad85`, 2026-07-24).** Frontend wired the Gear tab: an "Attach Modifier" button per equipped item opens the existing `ItemPickerModal` scoped to Equipmods; new `attachEquipmentModifier.ts` mirrors `purchaseEquipment.ts`'s exact shape; `ResolvedEquipmentDto` gained `appliedModifiers` for nested display. Live-verified end to end against a real character: attached both an unknown-cost enhancement (free-attach path) and a known-$0-cost Masterwork modifier (a different code path) to two different items, both persisted correctly to disk targeting the right item, both survived a reload, both correctly surfaced via the unresolved-corpus indicator since the demo corpus has zero Equipmods records to resolve against. **Honest limitation noted by frontend**: the nested-nested-modifier-under-its-weapon display path is code-correct but not live-reachable today given the empty demo corpus — same "structurally correct but currently unreachable" shape as Barbarian DR and the Wizard-bootstrap branch noted elsewhere this session. **Lead independently verified**: full workspace 4284/4284 (unaffected — frontend's changes are TS-only), desktop 211/211, frontend suite 78/78 (up from 77), typecheck clean. Sub-task 6 fully closed.

    **Independently re-verified, two RED reproductions (QA, 2026-07-24).** Confirmed free-attach-on-unknown-cost against the real generated `EQUIPMODS_TABLE` directly, not just the doc comment's claim: `"Special Ability ~ +1 ~ Weapon"` genuinely resolves `cost_gp: None`, `"Special Quality ~ Masterwork ~ Weapon"` resolves `Some(0.0)` via first-match semantics — matching both the commit and frontend's live numbers. (Noted, not a defect: the generated table has pre-existing duplicate keys with differing `cost_gp`, resolved deterministically by first-match order — unrelated to this sub-task.) **RED #1**: disabled `local_store.rs`'s new `equipment_modifier=` line emission, reran the round-trip test — failed with `left: []` vs. two expected modifiers, exactly reproducing the silent-drop-on-save bug; restored, confirmed green. **RED #2**: changed the free-attach `None => 0` branch to `None => Blocked` — failed exactly as expected; restored, confirmed green. **RED #3**: swapped the modifier picker's routing in `buildItemPickerConfig.test.ts` for the plain equipment path — failed exactly as expected; restored, confirmed green. Confirmed frontend's `appliedModifiers` is a plain array guarded by `.length > 0`, not an `Option` — no risk of the earlier null-serialization bug class recurring here; `handleModifierPicked` refreshes from the real server response on success, zero state mutation on `Blocked`, same shape as other mutation handlers. 32. **RESOLVED — APG Oracle full-build closure (8th ACG/APG class-specific closure, backend, 2026-07-25), greenlit by the lead against the real corpus.** `is_supported_oracle_single_class` widens `has_supported_class_chassis`/`compute_apg_class_chassis`'s dispatch (mirroring Cavalier/Alchemist/Inquisitor's own exact-match gate shape). Real spontaneous known-spell posture (`oracle_spells_known_table`/`unmet_oracle_known_spell_conditions`/`ground_oracle_known_spells`) mirrors Sorcerer's own shape exactly, not the simpler prepared-spellbook shape Wizard/Arcanist/Warpriest use — a genuine structural difference, named honestly in the scoping doc before building rather than assumed to be "just a bigger table." Reuses `cleric_spell_list::cleric_spell_level` directly for the shared `SPELLLIST:2|Cleric|Oracle` portion; the Oracle-specific bonus-spell-list portion stays explicitly out of scope. Mystery (Life/Healing Hands, a flat +4 Heal bonus) and Curse (Clouded Vision, a flat 30-foot vision-range cap) both mirror Warpriest's own Blessing three-branch dispatch shape, each recognized-choice clearing its own claim-blocking diagnostic in favor of a non-blocking "other N types" note. Class-skill list confirmed clean (excludes Climb/Intimidate/Swim, same shape as Wizard/Arcanist) — no bug to fix here, unlike Warpriest/Slayer's own closures. **Unlike Arcanist, Oracle stays permanently `Blocked`** on its own new `other_features_deferred` diagnostic (no MVP narrowing was found for Cure Wounds/Inflict Wounds/Tongues this slice) — an honest outcome, proven by a dedicated test (`single_class_oracle_with_everything_recognized_still_stays_permanently_blocked`) rather than assumed. 9 new dispatch-widening tests, 13/13 `apg_class_chassis_dispatch_tests` (2 new: Oracle's own retired-diagnostic and positive-counterpart coverage), both SD-24 APG audit tests updated and green, `named_features_wired` set to 2 for Oracle (Mystery slot + Curse slot — known-spell posture/Orisons fold into the general spellcasting mechanism, not counted separately, mirroring Arcanist's Cantrips/Warpriest's Orisons). Full lib suite 474/474 green (up from 460). **Lead independently re-ran all 14 Oracle-related tests (including the permanently-Blocked proof) and the APG coverage audit against the real committed HEAD** — both clean, matching exactly.

33. **RESOLVED — ACG Swashbuckler full-build closure (9th ACG/APG class-specific closure, backend, 2026-07-25), correcting a real mis-scoping from the second comparative pass.** A third comparative pass across the 5 remaining classes found Swashbuckler had been mis-lumped in with Investigator/Shaman/Summoner/Witch's own real subsystem gaps (Familiar/Eidolon/new-spell-list costs) — checked directly against the real corpus and found it's a non-caster with zero spellcasting scope, the same cheapest structural shape Slayer already proved out. `is_supported_swashbuckler_single_class` widens the dispatch; Panache (a flat daily maximum, `max(1, Charisma modifier)`), Charmed Life (a real per-day budget, granted starting at 2nd level), and Nimble (a flat AC dodge bonus) are all genuinely grounded. **Two real findings worth naming honestly**: (1) Panache's own formula is sourced from the ability's DESC text, not a literal `BONUS:VAR` token — the base Swashbuckler's own corpus record defines no `Panache_Cap`/`PanachePoints` formula at all (only the Inspired Blade archetype and the Extra Panache feat set/adjust these variables); cross-validated against the archetype's own explicit `MAX(1,CHA)+MAX(1,INT)` formula, which confirms the base is `MAX(1,CHA)`, but this is a genuinely different evidentiary path than every other formula this segment has grounded. (2) Charmed Life's real grant level (2nd, not 1st) isn't encoded anywhere in the corpus record itself (no level-gate token) — confirmed via a real web search rather than assumed, which also resolved an open verification question from the scoping doc about the formula's negative-operand behavior at level 1 (moot, since the feature simply doesn't exist yet at level 1). Also fixed the FOURTH instance of the class-skill-bonus widening bug (Climb/Intimidate/Swim). Swashbuckler stays permanently `Blocked` on `other_features_deferred` (Deeds, Swashbuckler Finesse, and every other named feature remain deferred) — the same honest outcome as 8 of the 9 closures so far (only Arcanist reached `Computed`). 6 new dispatch-widening tests, 24 `acg_class_chassis_dispatch_tests` (2 new: retired-diagnostic and positive-counterpart coverage, renamed carve-out to "the other two"), both SD-24 ACG audit tests updated and green, `named_features_wired` set to 3 (Panache, Charmed Life, Nimble — structurally independent, no shared table). See `docs/release/v0.6/third-full-class-build-comparative-scoping.md` for the full scoping record.

34. **RESOLVED — ACG Investigator full-build closure (10th ACG/APG class-specific closure, backend, 2026-07-26), a real no-spellcasting MVP with a genuine class-skill design decision, scoped by a new "scout" agent and independently re-verified by the lead.** `is_supported_investigator_single_class` widens the dispatch (chassis already built since SD-22 Epic 4, no new chassis work needed). Trapfinding (`max(level/2,1)`), Trap Sense (`level/3`, no floor), and Inspiration's flat pool-size fact (`max(1,level/2+INT)`) are all genuinely grounded. **A real "swapped floors" hazard, flagged in scoping and verified during build**: Investigator's Trapfinding has the `max(...,1)` floor and its Trap Sense does not — genuinely backwards from Slayer's own two features (Trap Sense floored, Trapfinding not), even though both classes' records share the same `BONUS:VAR` name `TrapSenseBonus`. Verified each formula against its own class's own corpus record directly rather than reusing Slayer's functions as a template. **Investigator is the first class on the whole roster with a genuine PARTIAL class-skill match** (Climb/Intimidate present, Swim absent) — forcing a real, lead-greenlit design decision: `selected_skill_class_skill_bonus_applies`'s single scalar could not represent "true for Climb/Intimidate, false for Swim" for the same character, so it split into three independent per-skill functions (`selected_skill_climb_is_class_skill`/`..._intimidate_.../..._swim_...`), touching both `pilot_compute.rs` and its corpus-aware mirror in `pilot_compute_corpus.rs`. Confirmed behavior-preserving for all 6 prior classes (each already cleanly all-three or none-of-three) via the full existing test suite, and caught + fixed one real regression this split caused in an existing QA-owned test (`v06_selected_skill_class_skill_bonus.rs`'s own explanation-text assertion expected the old shared-scalar phrasing "not class skills," updated to the new per-skill phrasing "not a class skill"). **Prepared extract spellcasting deliberately deferred to its own follow-on slice**: Investigator's `SPELLLIST:1|Alchemist` reuses the Alchemist formula list, and no Alchemist spell-list mapping exists anywhere in this codebase yet (confirmed by grep/find) — a genuinely new ~104-record data-ingestion cost none of the 9 prior closures needed, mirroring the Skald spellcasting split precedent. Investigator stays permanently `Blocked` on `other_features_deferred`. 4 new dispatch-widening tests plus a dedicated class-skill partial-match test, 24 `acg_class_chassis_dispatch_tests` (2 new, carve-out renamed to "the other one"), both SD-24 ACG audit tests updated and green, `named_features_wired` set to 3 (Trapfinding, Trap Sense, Inspiration pool-size). See `docs/release/v0.6/investigator-acg-full-build-scoping.md` for the full scoping record, including the two decisions (per-skill widening; smaller MVP first) the lead explicitly greenlit before this build started.

35. **RESOLVED — real bug fixed in Warpriest's Sacred Weapon dice count, found by scout's formula-audit pass, independently confirmed by the lead and re-verified by backend before fixing.** `warpriest_sacred_weapon_base_dice`'s own `dice_count` term used `level/20` — the `PREBASESIZELT:M` (smaller-than-Medium) branch's own denominator from `acg_abilities_class.lst` — transcribed by mistake into this function's applicable `PREBASESIZEEQ:M` (Medium) branch, a sibling-branch mix-up. The function's own doc comment also cited `/20` as if corpus-verified, so the error was internally self-consistent and would not have been caught by a doc-vs-code check alone — only a direct corpus re-read surfaced it. Corrected to the real EQ:M formula, `1+min(1,level/15)`. Real effect: a level 15-19 Warpriest previously got `dice_count=1` (wrong; `15/20` through `19/20` all floor to 0) instead of the correct `2` (`15/15=1`, `min(1,1)=1`, `+1=2`) — this closure's own bounded 1-3 level scope never diverged between the two denominators, so the bug was silently wrong only at level 15+, and only ever surfaced in the explanation's own prose text (never a separate `.value` field any existing test could have caught). New dedicated test `warpriest_sacred_weapon_dice_count_matches_the_real_eq_m_corpus_branch_at_every_level` covers the full level range including the previously-affected band, proven RED-then-GREEN against the real bug (reverted to `/20`, confirmed the new test fails, restored the fix, confirmed green) before landing. Full lib suite 489/489.

36. **RESOLVED — APG Witch full-build closure (11th ACG/APG class-specific closure, backend, 2026-07-26), correcting the standing "blocked on the unbuilt Familiar" verdict for the last untouched classes.** A third comparative pass (scout-scoped, lead-verified) found Witch's own Ward hex is genuinely familiar-independent — a flat deflection-AC/saving-throw-resistance bonus (`BONUS:VAR|WitchWardBonus|2` base, `+1` at level 8, `+1` at level 16). `is_supported_witch_single_class` widens the dispatch; `ground_or_block_witch_class_features` recognizes `choice:witch_hex -> hex:ward` and grounds the flat bonus, mirroring Oracle's/Warpriest's own choice-recognition three-branch shape. **Independently re-verified the scoping doc's own claim before building** (per this segment's own "verify, don't assume" discipline, the same one that caught the Shaman/Healer's-Touch level-gate) and confirmed Ward's own gate (`PREVARGTEQ:WitchHexAbilityLVL,1`, where `WitchHexAbilityLVL` resolves to `WitchLVL` unconditionally, and `WitchMinorHexQualify` is set to `1` unconditionally too) is genuinely immediate at level 1, unlike Shaman's proposed MVP. No further scoping or building work is queued on Summoner until this is decided.

41. **RESOLVED — Inquisitor Judgment deepened (backend, 2026-07-26, task #3), a genuine scoping correction found by re-verifying rather than trusting the original closure's own exclusion list.** Directed to widen Inquisitor beyond its single Justice judgment (item 8's third APG closure): checked the original scoping doc's "opponent/effect-dependent" exclusion of the other 7 judgment types directly against the raw corpus (`apg_abilities_class.lst`) rather than assuming the prior summary was right, and found it wrong for three of them. **Protection** (`1+InqJudgeProtectionLVL/5` sacred/profane bonus to Armor Class) and **Purity** (`1+InqJudgePurityLVL/5` sacred/profane bonus on all saving throws) are both flat, unconditional self-buffs with real live consumers already computed in this codebase (`baseline_armor_class`, `total_saves`) — identical shape to Justice's own attack-roll bonus, not opponent-dependent at all; the original doc had simply mis-grouped them. **Smiting** (`BONUS:VAR|InqJudgeSmitingLVL|InquisitorLVL`, no scaling) is a flat, level-independent boolean fact ("weapons count as magic for bypassing damage reduction"), grounded as a standalone explanation record since no numeric total exists to layer it onto — the same shape Brawler's Strike DR-bypass fact (item 39) already established. All three now ground alongside Justice via a widened `ground_or_block_inquisitor_judgment` (an if/else chain over all 4 recognized selections, each with its own `active_inquisitor_<type>_judgment_bonus`/formula function) — `choice:inquisitor_judgment` now recognizes 4 of the 8 real judgment types; the remaining 5 (Destruction, Healing, Piercing, Resiliency, Resistance) genuinely still need engine state that doesn't exist (a damage-roll total, round-tick HP state, a concentration/CL-check total, a DR-received facet distinct from Smiting's own DR-bypass-on-attack, and a chosen-energy-type mechanism plus energy-resistance state, respectively). Also grounded **Stern Gaze** (`max(1,level/2)` morale bonus, real unconditional level-1 feature, no choice/activation gate unlike Judgment) into `compute_selected_skill_modifiers`'s Intimidate total — only the Intimidate half of its real two-skill DESC lands, Sense Motive is not a skill this codebase computes anywhere, the same "ground only the computed half" discipline item 40's own Persuasive/Diplomacy split already established. `named_features_wired` rises from 1 to 2 for Inquisitor (Judgment slot + Stern Gaze slot — Judgment's own 4 sub-types stay one slot, per this repo's established "counts slots, not sub-options" convention already applied to Witch's own ~20-hex Ward count). 8 new/updated dispatch-widening tests (Protection/Purity/Smiting each applying their real bonus with no cross-leak into Justice's own attack-bonus consumer, an unbuilt-type-naming negative case distinct from the "no choice at all" case, and Stern Gaze's unconditional stacking), both SD-24 APG audit tests updated and green. Full lib suite 524/524 green (up from 519).
