New `standalone_feat_skill_facts_consumer_wiring_tests` module (6 tests) proves both functions ground for real through the actual `compute_pilot_base_chassis` pipeline, not just `feat_effects.rs`'s own unit tests: a bare-fixture no-leak baseline, Acrobatic's two facts, Persuasive's Diplomacy-only fact with no double-counted Intimidate, Skill Focus grounding nothing without an explicit target choice (no fabricated canonical skill), grounding the real chosen skill when a target choice is present, and one fact per target when taken more than once. Full lib suite 575/575 green (up from 569), both SD-24 audit files unaffected and green, desktop 215/215.

48. **RESOLVED — Alchemist deepened (backend, 2026-07-26, task #4), flipping from "too big" to a genuinely tractable slice once Investigator's own shared spell-list leverage removed the original blocker.** Scout's fresh `alchemist-remaining-features-scoping.md` corrected the task note's own claim that Alchemist's casting is "formula-based, a different mechanic" from Investigator's — verified against the corpus and found byte-identical (`SPELLSTAT:INT MEMORIZE:YES SPELLBOOK:YES` for both), confirmed independently by the lead before greenlighting. **Bomb** (`KEY:Alchemist ~ Bomb`) is now fully wired — damage dice, save DC, and uses-per-day, all standalone facts since Bomb's own damage is a weapon-like magnitude against any target, not opponent-dependent (the same "groundable side of the Studied-Combat line" distinction Investigator's own Studied Combat/Strike stayed on the wrong side of). **The lead specifically flagged a sibling-branch hazard to verify carefully, the same shape the Sacred Weapon dice-count bug hid in** — and it was real: the corpus's own `AlchemistBombAdditionalDice` formula carries 10 conditional `-1` override branches at odd levels 3-19, each gated behind an `Alchemist_CF_BombDamage<N>` flag. Traced the flag's own definition and found it `DEFINE`s to `0` for the base Alchemist class and is ONLY ever set to `1` by two specific Ultimate Magic archetype feature grants (Psychonaut, Reanimator) — entirely out-of-scope splatbook/archetype content this codebase's own single-book, base-class-only build never reaches, so the 10 branches are provably vacuous for this closure's scope, not a hidden bug. A second `AlchemistBombTimes` contribution (`BonusBombCount/2`) was checked the same way and found equally vacuous (`BonusBombCount` only increments via a Gnome-specific Favored Class Bonus choice this engine models nowhere). **Poison Resistance** was re-derived independently (not copied from Investigator's own identical-shaped feature) and confirmed to share the exact same tiers, grounded as Alchemist's own separate copy per the established parallel-copy discipline. **Prepared extract spellcasting** now genuinely validated too, reusing the shared `alchemist_spell_list` module directly (Alchemist is the list's own namesake class) plus Alchemist's own extract-count table, re-verified against d20pfsrd.com's own separate Alchemist page and confirmed byte-identical to Investigator's. The old `class_feature.apg.alchemist.spellcasting_deferred.unsupported` diagnostic is retired in favor of a real `class_spell.apg.alchemist.prepared_extracts.unsupported` (spellcasting-specific) plus a renamed, narrower `other_features_deferred` (Discovery/Swift Alchemy/Swift Poisoning only). `named_features_wired` rises from 1 to 3 for Alchemist (Mutagen + Bomb + Poison Resistance — Bomb's own three numeric facets count as one slot, the same "one record, several parameters" shape Cleric's Channel Energy already established, not three separate slots). 19 new/updated dispatch-widening tests, both SD-24 APG audit tests updated and green. Full lib suite 582/582 green (up from 575), desktop 215/215.

49. **RESOLVED (backend, 2026-07-27, `73c70055`, lead-verified) — Bloodrager's shipped spellcasting diagnostic asserted something false and user-visible about the corpus (scout's `bloodrager-spellcasting-scoping.md`, 2026-07-27, task #1).** `push_bloodrager_spellcasting_deferred_diagnostic`'s message claims Bloodrager "has no class-skill list," but `acg_abilities_class.lst:331` grants one (`ABILITY:Class Skill|AUTOMATIC|Acrobatics|Climb|Craft|Handle Animal|Intimidate|Knowledge (Arcana)|Perception|Ride|Spellcraft|Survival|Swim`, 11 skills including all three of Climb/Intimidate/Swim — the 7th instance of the recurring class-skill-bonus widening bug). Worth recording the encoding trap that produced it: ACG classes carry zero `CSKILL:` tokens anywhere in `acg_classes.lst` — class skills live on a separate `<Class> ~ Class Skills` internal ability instead, so a `CSKILL:`-shaped search finds nothing and wrongly concludes the list is absent (scout's own first pass made this mistake before checking how Slayer's list was encoded). A second, related diagnostic defect in the same function: it claim-blocks unconditionally at every level, but Bloodrager has no `CAST:`/`KNOWN:` rows below level 4 at all (`BONUS:CASTERLEVEL|Bloodrager|Caster_Level_Bloodrager|PRECLASS:1,Bloodrager=4`, lead-verified directly against the corpus) — so a level 1-3 Bloodrager is claim-blocked today for a feature RAW says it doesn't have yet. Both landed in the first, standalone half of task #1: the diagnostic is now level-aware (`spellcasting_absent_by_level_gate`, non-claim-blocking, below level 4), and the false class-skill claim is corrected with the real 11-skill list grounded. Lead independently verified: 670/670 lib, 13/13 Bloodrager tests, ACG audit 3/3, clippy unchanged. `named_features_wired` rises 1→2 (Bloodrage + Spells) — not the originally-suggested 3, since `KEY:Bloodrager ~ Class Skills` is `CATEGORY:Internal`, the same as Warpriest's identical record which Warpriest's own count of 5 already excludes; confirmed against the corpus that no class in this segment has ever counted the class-skill-bonus widening fix as its own slot. The spell-list build (110-of-183) remains open, not part of this fix.

50. **OPERATOR-EQUIVALENT DECISION (lead, 2026-07-27) — genuine PCGen corpus authoring bug in Swashbuckler Deeds, ruled fixable via level-source substitution (scout's `swashbuckler-finesse-and-deeds-scoping.md`, task #14).** `SwashbucklerDeedQualifyLVL` (the variable gating all six deed tiers, `PREVARGTEQ:SwashbucklerDeedQualifyLVL,{1,3,7,11,15,19}`) is `DEFINE`d to 0 and set by exactly one token in the entire ingested PCGen tree: `BONUS:VAR|SwashbucklerDeedQualifyLVL|MagusLVL|TYPE=Base`. It is never set from `SwashbucklerLVL`, confirmed absent from Swashbuckler's own two `CLASS:Swashbuckler` chassis lines too — lead independently re-verified this directly, not just trusted the doc. Taken literally, a pure single-class Swashbuckler qualifies for zero deeds at any level; only a Magus (via the deed-granting archetype) ever satisfies the gate. This is a genuine data-authoring gap in PCGen's own ACG dataset, not a misreading — the sibling variable `SwashbucklerDeedsLVL`, which the deed *magnitude* formulas actually consume, is correctly set from `SwashbucklerLVL` in the same file, and the gate thresholds (1/3/7 spot-checked) match RAW's own published deed tiers exactly. **Ruled: substituting `SwashbucklerLVL` as the gate's level source is a transcription fix, not fabrication** — the tier constants come straight from the corpus's own (correctly-set-elsewhere) gates, and the alternative (implementing the gate faithfully) would ship a core class feature that provably never functions for the class it belongs to. First time this segment a build knowingly deviates from what the corpus literally encodes; the deviation must carry an explicit code comment recording the corpus defect and this ruling, so it reads as a deliberate, documented fix rather than an unexplained departure from the established "ground exactly what the corpus says" discipline.

51. **OPERATOR DECISION (2026-07-27) — REVERSES the 2026-07-26 "tag expensive blockers low-priority" policy. Blockers are the highest priority regardless of cost; swarm on one as soon as it's found, not after it's cheap.** The earlier prioritization pass (recorded in `SWARM_STATUS.md`, 2026-07-26) explicitly deprioritized Oracle/Witch/Shaman/Slayer/Swashbuckler/Monk/Warpriest and initially Bloodrager/Alchemist/Cavalier as "not cheap" or "needing a brand-new architecture pillar/mechanism this codebase doesn't have at all." **That reasoning is no longer the standing policy.** Cost/cheapness is not a reason to leave a blocker deprioritized — a real blocker gets swarmed on immediately, including the ones that need genuine new subsystem work (Monk's Deflect Arrows opponent-interaction engine, Slayer's Studied Target opponent-tracking pillar, Witch's/Shaman's Familiar subsystem), not just the ones a bounded slice can close cheaply. This directly followed a status check where the operator noted Monk's and Cavalier's blockers still showed as open on the dashboard despite a "clear the blockers" priority set 2026-07-26 — Cavalier's case turned out to be a stale cost estimate corrected same-day (task #6, closed); Monk's Deflect Arrows genuinely has not been touched, and under the old policy it would have stayed that way indefinitely. Going forward: no remaining class blocker (full list currently: Witch/Shaman spellcasting+Familiar, Slayer's opponent-tracking pillar, Swashbuckler — now closed — Monk's Deflect Arrows engine, Summoner's evolution-spending economy) should be left unscoped or unscheduled solely because it needs new architecture rather than a grounding pass.

53. **OPEN — the #23/#24 undercount bug is repo-wide, not Alchemist-only, and the true scale is far larger than task #24 or #25 as filed describe. Operator input needed before dispatching a fix.** Backend's #24 close flagged `alchemist_spell_list` as short 26 ACG-book records (121→147) and filed #25 as a single yes/no book-scope question ("should Alchemist also pull from ACG"). The lead independently re-derived every currently-shipped spell list against the real ingested corpus (`core_rulebook/cr_spells.lst` + `advanced_players_guide/apg_spells.lst` + `advanced_class_guide/acg_spells.lst`, correct token parse: split on `|`, then each group on `,`, membership-test) and found **all six CRB caster lists are short, several by more than half**:

    | Class | Shipped (home-book only) | True (CRB+APG+ACG union) | Missing |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Cleric | 236 | 298 | 62 (26%) |
    | Sorcerer | 394 | 576 | 182 (46%) |
    | Bard | 164 | 264 | 100 (61%) |
    | Druid | 169 | 271 | 102 (60%) |
    | Paladin | 45 | 95 | 50 (111%) |
    | Ranger | 51 | 114 | 63 (124%) |
    | Alchemist | 121 (post-#24) | 147 | 26 (21%, this is exactly #25) |

    Witch (324) and Bloodrager (200) are already correct because #23/#24's fix already unions APG+ACG for those two. **The underlying rule is unambiguous, not a preference**: PF1 RAW defines a class's spell list as every spell whose own `CLASSES:` entry names that class, regardless of which sourcebook printed the spell — a Cleric can prepare an APG- or ACG-published cleric spell exactly as freely as a CRB one. The CRB-list modules' own doc comments explicitly scope to `cr_spells.lst` alone ("this table re-parses ... `core_rulebook/cr_spells.lst`"), which was a **methodology gap inherited from before this session's `CLASSES:` bug was even found**, not a deliberate design choice — nothing in the codebase argues for single-book scoping on purpose. **Ruling: #25's technical answer is "all ingested books," full stop; that part is not in question.** What IS an open, operator-level decision: whether to dispatch a corrective wave across all 6 CRB lists (plus Alchemist's remaining 26) now, mid-swarm, given (a) it reopens six already-"closed" spell tables with no report of defect until now, (b) two of the six lists are undercounting by more than 100%, meaning "already correct" claims made earlier this segment about CRB casters were wrong by a wide margin, and (c) the standing 2026-07-26 directive already pauses new class work pending a full reconciliation review — this discovery surfaced *during* that pause, and the question is whether a same-day corrective wave counts as reconciliation (in scope now) or as new work (waits for the review). Not dispatched to backend pending this call.

52. **METHODOLOGY CORRECTION (lead, 2026-07-27) — the standalone-grounding bar's "opponent-dependent" exclusion was over-broad; corrected to distinguish an unmodelled CIRCUMSTANCE from an unmodelled QUANTITY (scout's `opponent-interaction-architecture-design.md`, tasks #13/#15).** Explicitly reverses the lead's own earlier ruling that Studied-Target-shaped features (a bonus that only applies against a specific opponent) need a real opponent-tracking pillar and stay deferred. Scout found the inconsistency by re-deriving every relevant formula from the corpus: Slayer's Studied Target (`SlayerLVL/5+1`), Investigator's Studied Combat (`InvestigatorLVL/2`) and Studied Strike (`min(9,(InvestigatorLVL-2)/2)`d6), and Cavalier's Challenge damage (`+CavalierLVL`) read ONLY the character's own level — none touches any property of the opponent (AC, HD, type, state). The opponent is a *scope condition* on when the bonus applies, not an *input* to computing its magnitude. **The precedent that settles it was already shipped, inside Slayer's own class**: `class_feature.acg.slayer.sneak_attack_dice` is already grounded as a standalone record despite Sneak Attack being conditional on flanking or a denied-Dexterity target — an opponent relationship this engine models nowhere — and its own detail text says so explicitly ("grounded as a standalone record only"). Lead independently re-verified this directly in the code before ruling. **Corrected line, to replace the old blanket "opponent-dependent defers": a magnitude conditional on an unmodelled CIRCUMSTANCE grounds as a standalone, explicitly-scoped fact (Sneak Attack, Trap Sense, Studied Target, Studied Combat/Strike, Challenge damage); a magnitude that needs an unmodelled QUANTITY belonging to the opponent (their AC, HD, save, or other property as a term in the formula) still defers.** Ruled Tier 0 (ground as scoped standalone facts, no total-folding, no new architecture) for all four affected features across three classes — Slayer (new), Investigator (deepening of already-closed task #8), Cavalier (deepening of already-closed task #6). Tier 1 (folding into computed totals via the existing `ClassAbilityActivation` primitive Rage already uses) deferred as a follow-on, not required now. **This correction does NOT reach Monk's Deflect Arrows**, which fails a different test entirely — zero `BONUS` token exists anywhere in the corpus for it, so there is no magnitude to ground under either the old or corrected bar; closing it for real would need a full attack-resolution pipeline (a product decision on real combat resolution, out of scope for one feat, tracked separately from this correction).

54. **RESOLVED — item 53's fix had a second, independent bug, found by backend holding #27 instead of shipping a number that happened to match. Ruling: PREVAREQ/optional-rule-gated spells belong on the list (extends item 53's reasoning).** The `CLASSES:` token parse item 53 fixed handles `Name1,Name2=N` correctly but not a trailing bracketed prereq — `CLASSES:Sorcerer,Witch,Wizard=3[PREVAREQ:Heroic,1]` — where `int(level)` throws and a swallowed `except ValueError: continue` silently drops the whole record, the same invisible-omission shape as the substring bug with a different root cause. Backend found it because Paladin's own re-derivation landed at 94 against the already-queued 95 and it checked the gap instead of concluding the queued number was stale (task #26's own lesson, applied). **The lead independently re-derived all nine affected classes with a bracket-stripping fix, and separately caught a bug in its own first re-check** (a quick script that didn't strip `.MOD` suffixes counted `"Resounding Blow"` and `"Resounding Blow.MOD"` as two different spells, inflating Paladin to a wrong 96) — corrected before reporting back, not shipped. Confirmed figures, all independently reproduced: Witch 324→**326** (Malediction, Unravel Destiny — reopens task #23, filed as task #33), Sorcerer 576→**578** (same two spells, corrected on tasks #30/#31 before backend builds them), Cleric 298→**301** (Heroic Fortune (Mass), Severed Fate, Unravel Destiny — plain "Heroic Fortune" was already correctly captured, its Cleric-facing group is unbracketed), Paladin queued number (95) unaffected in total but the correct *set* includes Heroic Fortune, not a phantom `.MOD`-suffix duplicate. Ranger/Bard/Druid/Alchemist/Bloodrager confirmed unaffected by either bug. **Ruling, extending item 53's "reachability over sourcebook" logic**: `[PREVAREQ:Heroic,1]`-gated spells (the APG Hero Points optional rule — a real, ingested, corpus-reachable spell, unlike Bloodrager's 73 genuinely unreachable entries) belong on the list. The gate is a condition on *casting* (does this table run the optional rule), not on *list membership* (can this class ever draw on this spell) — this engine enforces no spell-selection prerequisites today (list membership + level ceiling + slot budget only), so grounding the spell as list-present and leaving the gate unenforced is the same honest-standalone idiom already established for Studied Combat's own unenforced duration (item 46) and every other "real, reachable, but conditionally gated" fact this segment has chosen to represent rather than hide.

55. **OPERATOR DECISION (2026-07-27) — new class work resumes with Shaman (#12); the spell-list undercount wave (#23-33) counts as the reconciliation pass the 2026-07-26 pause was waiting on.** With #23-33 fully closed (nine spell lists, two independent parsing bugs, every number lead-verified against the raw corpus), the lead surfaced the question rather than assume the standing pause had lifted on its own — #23-33 were corrections to already-claimed-done work, in scope under the pause by the same logic that let #24/#25 proceed without a fresh go-ahead, but #12 is genuinely new build work, which the pause's own text reserves for explicit operator approval. Operator ruled: dispatch Shaman now. Reasoning recorded for future reference — the spell-list wave, by accident of what it turned up, already served the reconciliation review's real purpose (surfacing the largest latent defect in the swarm's closed-work claims and fixing it end to end), so a second, separate reconciliation pass before resuming build work is not required. Shaman itself is well de-risked for this restart: its Familiar subsystem is already shared/built (task #11), and its own spellcasting build can reuse the corrected `CLASSES:`-parsing discipline from the start rather than risk a third recurrence of either bug found this segment.

56. **RULING (lead, 2026-07-27) — Shaman's level-11 spells-per-day row stays as the corpus states it, `4,4,4,3,3,2,1`; do NOT substitute Cleric/Druid/Wizard/Witch's shared `4,4,4,4,3,2,1`.** Backend (task #12, stage 2) flagged a genuine single-cell divergence: Shaman's own `CAST:` rows in `acg_classes.lst` are byte-identical to all four peer full 9th-level casters at levels 10 and 12, but diverge at 11 — Shaman gains its 4th 3rd-level spell slot one level later than every peer. Lead independently re-checked all three rows directly against the raw corpus (`acg_classes.lst` lines under `###Block: Shaman` vs `cr_classes.lst` under Cleric) and confirmed byte-for-byte: this is real, not a transcription artifact of backend's own reading. **The deciding factor is the absence of an internal contradiction.** Swashbuckler's Deed gate (item 50) was ruled a genuine corpus defect because a *sibling* variable (`SwashbucklerDeedsLVL`) was correctly set from `SwashbucklerLVL` in the same file while the gate variable wasn't — proof the corpus's own data was internally inconsistent with itself. No such proof exists here: Shaman's own level-11 row is internally monotonic (3rd-level slots go 3→3→4 across levels 10-11-12, never dipping), and nothing else in the class block contradicts it. A single class differing from its peers on one cell is not itself evidence of an authoring error — PF1 classes routinely have small, deliberate table differences from their nearest peers as part of their own design. Per the standing "ground exactly what the corpus says, deviate only on proven internal contradiction" discipline (established across items 50 and the whole spell-list wave, items 53-54), the corpus value stands. Backend already pinned this with a test (`shaman_level_11_follows_the_corpus_not_the_peer_row`) asserting both the value used AND the peer value NOT used, with a comment naming exactly what to do if this ruling is ever reversed on real evidence (a published errata, a PCGen upstream fix) — that test needs no further change under this ruling. Also confirms two design choices from the same stage as correct: deriving the access ceiling from the slot table itself (`shaman_spell_level_access` calls `rposition` on `shaman_base_spells_per_day_table` rather than duplicating threshold constants, preventing the two tables from drifting apart) and proving the spellcasting wiring live end-to-end through `build_pilot_headless_receipt` rather than only unit-testing the pure functions.

57. **OPEN — Witch's real spellcasting shape is a third caster posture this codebase hasn't built yet, and building it as a Shaman-shaped prepared-from-list caster would be quietly wrong. Needs a scoping pass (dispatched to scout) before #11's remaining piece is built.** Backend stopped before building rather than mirror Shaman's own stage 2, and the lead independently re-verified the finding directly against the corpus before dispatching. `CLASS:Witch`'s own posture token is `SPELLSTAT:INT KNOWNSPELLS:LEVEL=0` — automatic knowledge of cantrips only, with no full `KNOWNSPELLS:LEVEL=0|1|...|9` ladder the way Cleric/Druid/Shaman all carry, and no `SPELLBOOK:YES` the way Wizard carries. This matches RAW exactly: a witch's spells above cantrip level are stored in and prepared from her familiar, not innately known and not spellbook-gated — a genuinely third shape, not a variant of either shape this codebase already builds. **A real factual error already sitting in shipped code, found in the process**: `src/pcgen_import/lst_parser/spellcasting_class.rs:22-26`'s own comment claims Witch carries "the same absent-signals prepared-casting shape as Cleric/Druid" — the lead independently confirmed this is wrong (the absent-signals half is right, the "same as" half isn't; Cleric/Druid's own KNOWNSPELLS ladder is present and full, Witch's is present but cantrip-only). Dispatched to backend as a one-line factual correction, not a design call. **Why this needed stopping rather than building**: mirroring Shaman's own stage 2 would ship a Witch who prepares any of her 326 spells directly with zero familiar involvement — mechanically a Cleric with an INT stat, would pass every test written against it, and would be wrong about the class's own defining mechanic. Exactly the no-stub-doctrine failure mode this segment has been naming plainly rather than shipping quietly. **What's already usable, independently confirmed by the lead**: the 326-spell list (task #33) is built and verified; Witch's `CAST:` table is byte-identical to Cleric's through all 20 levels including L11 (found by backend while investigating Shaman's own L11 divergence, a different question landing on the same table). **The open design question**: how to model "prepares from the familiar" when the familiar's own stat block doesn't exist yet (the Familiar subsystem's deferred Tier 2, task #11's original scoping). Two honest paths named, not yet chosen between: a familiar-as-spell-store mechanism (retargeting Wizard's own spellbook shape at the familiar instead of the character), or an explicitly-bounded MVP that grounds slots/DCs and leaves preparation itself blocked on the missing store. Worth noting this may be the first real product reason to build Familiar Tier 2 at all, which should factor into how that deferral gets sized going forward rather than treated as indefinitely low-priority.

**RULED (lead, 2026-07-27) — scout's `witch-spellcasting-shape-design.md` landed same day, correcting item 57's own open question: no Familiar Tier 2 is needed after all.** Scout's central finding, independently re-verified by the lead directly against the corpus before ruling: `KEY:Witch ~ Familiar`'s own DESC states plainly "The familiar stores all the spells that you know" — but this repo's Wizard spellbook mechanism (`unmet_wizard_spellbook_conditions`/`ground_wizard_prepared_spellbook`, lead-confirmed directly in the code) was never a modeled container object in the first place — it's a semantic read of `input.chosen.spells_selected` filtered by `AcquisitionMode`, nothing more. A spell store is a set of spell identities; the familiar's own stat block (HD, saves, INT, skills) is irrelevant to storing them, the same "creature is not an input to the mechanic" shape the Studied-Target and Familiar-master-benefit passes already established. Witch is genuinely **Cleric-shaped at level 0, Wizard-shaped at levels 1-9** — a hybrid of two already-built shapes, not a third mechanism needing new architecture. **Three rulings, all confirming scout's own recommendation after independent verification**:
1. **Treat `spells_selected`/`AcquisitionMode::Known` as the familiar's store — yes.** It's the only container this engine has, matches RAW's intent, reuses shipped code. Per scout's own honesty framing (matching Sacred Weapon's evidentiary-note precedent): this is a deliberate modeling choice, not a corpus-stated mechanical fact, and the explanation text must say so plainly rather than imply the corpus encodes a familiar-storage check it doesn't.
2. **Patron spells (12 `SPELLKNOWN:CLASS|Witch=<level>|<spell>` records, gated `PREVARGTEQ:WitchPatronLVL,{2,4,6,...}`, lead-confirmed count and gate directly against `apg_abilities_class.lst`) are OUT of this slice**, deferred to a follow-on that needs the patron chooser recognized first — keeps the caster-shape build bounded to one design question at a time, same discipline as Investigator's own MVP-first sequencing.
3. **The ingest parser needs no third posture value — comment-only fix.** `spellcasting_class.rs`'s posture derivation reads `MEMORIZE`/`SPELLBOOK` correctly and never reads `KNOWNSPELLS` for any class; only the editorial "same as Cleric/Druid" comparison was wrong. The hybrid distinction belongs in the compute layer (where this design puts it), not the ingest layer.

Recommended build scope for #11's remaining piece: cantrips auto-known (Cleric-shaped); levels 1-9 `Known`=familiar-recorded/`Prepared`=drawn-from-it (Wizard-shaped, Arcanist's simpler no-opposed-school variant); slots reuse Cleric's byte-identical `CAST` table; save DC `10 + spell level + INT`; patron spells deferred but named honestly in the diagnostic either way. No Tier 2, no new architecture.

58. **RESOLVED — the Witch/Shaman familiar-count asymmetry (66b8aae9) is a population of one, not a class of bugs. Backend swept the codebase and found a sharp, checkable criterion for when this shape can even occur.** Enumerated all 62 `ground_*` functions, generalized to every locally-defined, non-class-named helper called from two or more distinct class contexts (9 helpers), and triaged each. Most are pure arithmetic (`ability_bonus_spells`, `ability_modifier_for`) or feat-effects layered onto existing totals, not class features with their own count. Two findings worth recording as standing methodology, both lead-independently spot-checked: (1) `effective_combat_feat_intelligence_score` is shared by Brawler's Cunning and Swashbuckler's Finesse but implements **two distinct features** that happen to need the same `max(a,b)` arithmetic — confirmed exactly two production call sites (`pilot_compute.rs:16307`/`:13600`), each independently crediting its own count (9/12) in separate match arms; a shared *formula* used by two features is not the same hazard as a shared *feature* credited by two classes, since only the latter has a single fact two rows must agree about. (2) `ground_wolf_companion_stat_block` is shared by Druid/Hunter but **cannot produce this asymmetry at all** — `named_features_wired` coverage audits exist only for APG and ACG (confirmed: no `sd24_crb_class_coverage_audit.rs` exists anywhere in `tests/`), so Druid has no coverage row for Hunter's to disagree with. **The resulting criterion, sharper than "watch for shared mechanisms"**: a counting asymmetry requires one feature-grounding helper (not arithmetic) shared by two classes that both have coverage rows (both APG or both ACG). Exactly one helper in the codebase meets both conditions — `ground_familiar_master_benefit` — and it's already fixed. Backend is adding a guard test asserting both familiar-sharing classes credit it, so a future third consumer of `Standard Familiar List` can't silently repeat this. Worth reusing this criterion directly when scoping any future class that reuses an existing class's mechanism, rather than re-deriving it.

59. **RESOLVED — #16-adjacent scoping (task #34, backend, investigation only): the two-skill General-feat gap is genuinely empty; the real remaining gaps are Master Craftsman and a mis-scoped "Focus-feat chooser" framing.** Enumerated `feat_data/general.rs` exhaustively rather than eyeballing it — lead independently re-verified: **50 total General feats, 12 carry any `SKILL` qualifier, exactly 10 are two-skill.** All 10 are correctly triaged already: 8 fully standalone in `STANDALONE_TWO_SKILL_FACTS` (Acrobatic/Alertness/Animal Affinity/Deceitful/Deft Hands/Magical Aptitude/Self-Sufficient/Stealthy), Persuasive correctly split (Diplomacy standalone, Intimidate integrated), Athletic correctly absent (both Climb and Swim are computed skills, no standalone remainder needed). The premise that makes the split/absence correct rather than lucky — `compute_selected_skill_modifiers` computes exactly Climb/Intimidate/Swim, confirmed directly in its own diagnostic message — was verified, not assumed. **One real gap found**: `Master Craftsman` (`BONUS:SKILL|LIST|2` + a ranks-tracking var, a single chosen Craft/Profession skill) has zero handling anywhere — not two-skill, not Skill Focus-shaped, fell through both existing categories. Confirmed zero references anywhere in `src/rules_core`. **On the Focus-feat question, the framing in #16's own description was wrong, and backend corrected it rather than answer the literal question asked**: the chooser mechanism is not the gap — `skill_focus_facts_from_choices`/`spell_focus_facts_from_choices`/`weapon_focus_facts_from_choices` already exist, all three sharing one proven `choice:<feat>_target` shape, lead-confirmed all three functions exist. **The actual blocker is the destination**: every spell-save-DC id in the engine is keyed by spell *level*, never by school (lead confirmed: every `*save_dc*` id found is `spell_level_{n}`/`extract_level_{n}`-shaped, zero school-keyed), so Spell/Greater Spell Focus can only ever ground standalone — the same "no total exists to integrate into" reasoning that correctly kept Spell Focus deferred on #22. Weapon Focus is worse: zero `attack.*` explanation ids exist anywhere in the engine (lead confirmed directly), so there is no per-weapon attack total at all, not even a narrow one. Weapon Specialization, Greater Weapon Specialization, and Improved Critical share the identical no-destination story (lead confirmed: only named as a prerequisite string or comment reference, no grounding producer for any of the three). **Recommendation, for whoever picks up #16 next (featmate)**: Master Craftsman is the one cheap remaining two-skill-shaped widening (existing standalone-fact idiom, no new architecture); the Focus-feat threads should be re-scoped as a genuine product decision — "does this engine want a school-keyed spell-save-DC pillar / a per-weapon attack total" — rather than treated as a feat-effects widening task, since the chooser half was never the blocker.

60. **RESOLVED — the roster survey's `class_feature.*`-only methodology (item 59's own near-miss, recurring) undercounted CRB classes systematically; corrected pick is Summoner, not Monk/Paladin.** Scout's fast survey ranked Monk/Paladin as the biggest gaps by comparing corpus records against grounded `class_feature.*` ids alone. Lead checked both directly and found CRB classes ground most chassis-level features under a second prefix, `class_chassis.<class>.*`, entirely unsearched: Paladin already has ~21-24 grounded ids (Divine Grace, Lay on Hands, Smite Evil, Channel Positive Energy, Mercy, three Auras, Holy Champion, partial spellcasting), Monk ~18-20 (AC Bonus, Ki Pool, Slow Fall, Diamond Body, Purity of Body, Flurry of Blows, Unarmed Strike damage die, Evasion family). Neither was close to the near-zero/skeletal state first reported — dispatching either would have meant rebuilding working code. Scout re-derived across all three id-bearing prefixes known at the time (`class_feature` 649, `class_chassis` 256, `class_spell` 115, whole-tree count — **a fourth, `class.<name>.*`, was found later, task #40, and is folded into the addendum below; treat "three" in this sentence as a historical snapshot, not the current count**) and corrected the pick to **Summoner**: 6-7 remaining features (Bond Senses, Maker's Call, Merge Forms, Twin Eidolon, Summon Monster, Aspect/Greater Aspect), only reachable now that the Eidolon MVP (task #17) landed — the pool sizes are self-scoped facts needing nothing from the eidolon's own stat block, the fourth instance this segment of "the creature is not an input to the mechanic" (after Studied Target, the familiar's master benefit, the spell store). **Lead independently re-verified every cited formula directly against `apg_abilities_class.lst` before greenlighting** (own first two grep attempts gave false negatives from imprecise syntax — worth naming, the same trap as always: a query that looks precise and misses can produce high-confidence wrong answers): `BONUS:VAR|BondSensesRounds|classlevel("Summoner")`, `MakersCallTimes|((classlevel("Summoner")-2)/4)`, `MergeFormsRounds|classlevel("Summoner")`, `TwinEidolonMinutes|classlevel("Summoner")`, `SummonMonsterDuration|classlevel`, `SummonMonsterTimes|CHA+3`, `SummonMonsterLVL|min(9,(classlevel+1)/2)` — all confirmed exact. **One real correction to scout's own description**: Aspect/Greater Aspect are not a flat `ABILITYPOOL of 1` — `BONUS:VAR|EidolonAspect|{1,1,1,2,2,2,3,3}` sets real corpus-encoded point costs, and the ability grants a "divert N points from the eidolon's evolution pool, add N evolutions to yourself" chooser tied directly into the shared, still-deferred evolution pool. The point costs are flat and corpus-real (not subsystem-blocked the way the full evolution economy is), but this is a canonical-narrowing candidate (Oracle's-Mysteries shape), not an independent flat fact like the other four. Scout cleared to write the full Summoner scoping doc with this correction folded in. **Second methodology-gap catch of the same session in the same lane (item 59, then this)** — worth treating "which id prefixes exist" as a checked fact before any future roster survey, not assumed from one file's convention. **Addendum (scout, 2026-07-27): the durable form of this rule is "enumerate the prefixes, don't name them."** Naming "two" or "three" prefixes in a doc (as the lead did, informally, in a later chat message) just moves the staleness rather than removing it — a fourth prefix appearing later would silently repeat this exact bug. One-line check that can't go stale: `grep -oh '"[a-z_]*\.[a-z0-9_.]*"' | cut -d. -f1 | sort | uniq -c`. Lead independently re-ran an equivalent enumeration directly (`grep -rohE` across `src/rules_core/`) and confirmed `class_spell` is a real, substantially-sized third prefix (100+ ids, not noise) — every future roster survey should run this enumeration fresh rather than assume the prefix list from any prior doc, including this one.

62. **RESOLVED — scout's `stale-deferral-sweep.md` found a genuine self-inconsistency in the codebase's own DR-grounding reasoning (item 52 follow-on): 3 flat `DR:N/-` records grounded standalone, 2 identical-shape records deferred on the exact reasoning item 52 already overturned. Monk's Perfect Self (`DR:10/Chaotic`, level 20) has zero code hits anywhere, not even a deferred record. **Lead independently re-verified every corpus token directly against `cr_abilities_class.lst`** (`DR:10/Chaotic` for Perfect Self, `DR:5/Evil` for Aura of Righteousness, both exact) and confirmed Paladin's own shipped comment cites the consumer-absence reasoning verbatim (lines 387/449/456), and that Monk's Fast Movement/Maneuver Training/High Jump/Wholeness of Body/Perfect Self all still show zero `pilot_compute.rs` hits as of the sweep. **Scout's own ranking, adopted as-is**: cross-checked scout's proposed split against the standing "bounded, one-clear-thing-per-task" discipline (Investigator's pre-slice/main-body split, Alchemist's deepening) rather than the alternative (one bundled "make DR consistent" closure) — bundling reads cleaner in the doc but has no owner advantage here, since the three features are already naturally split across different lanes. **Ruling**: (1) Paladin's Aura of Righteousness DR — the only one not already attached to in-flight or future work — filed as its own standalone task (#38), dispatched to whoever frees up next; three in-codebase precedents, one flat value, no new design. (2) Monk's Perfect Self DR folded directly into the already-in-progress task #36 (same file, same session, same flat-DR pattern as Fast Movement/Maneuver Training/High Jump/Wholeness of Body — no separate dispatch needed); its own level-20 gate sits above `MAX_SUPPORTED_MONK_LEVEL = 12`, so task #36 must also settle whether the cap widens for this one feature or Perfect Self grounds as a scoped-but-currently-unreachable fact (Master Craftsman's 5-rank-prereq precedent). Scout's "confirmed correctly deferred" list (Ranger Camouflage, Bard Soothing Performance, Ranger Evasion/Monk Improved Evasion, Ranger Master Hunter, Druid Wild Shape/A Thousand Faces, Barbarian Indomitable Will, Fighter Armor Mastery, Skald's DR ally-extension) taken as-is — each cites a real absent-magnitude or already-grounded reason, not consumer-absence.

**Featmate's independent Monk audit, taken deliberately over Paladin** (a second read on the untouched class is worth more than re-confirming an already-checked one) **confirms the corrected prefix methodology and finds Monk is genuinely not the big remaining gap.** Corpus baseline: 24 real class features, 14 with numeric BONUS tokens, 10 grant-only. **The headline finding**: `MAX_SUPPORTED_MONK_LEVEL = 12` (lead-confirmed, `pilot_compute.rs:4427`), so most of what looked "missing" (Abundant Step/Diamond Soul/Quivering Palm/Tongue of the Sun and Moon/Timeless Body/Empty Body/Perfect Self, all gated ≥12) is unreachable by this engine's own level ceiling, not un-built — chasing it would be wasted effort. **Four genuinely missing and in-range**, all lead-independently re-verified against `cr_abilities_class.lst` directly: Fast Movement (gate 3, `10*floor(MonkLVL/3)` ft), Maneuver Training (gate 3, `CMB_BAB = ManeuverTrainingLVL - ManeuverTrainingBAB = MonkLVL - floor(MonkLVL*3/4)`, confirmed the full three-token derivation, not just the summary formula), High Jump (gate 5, `+MonkLVL` on Acrobatics-to-jump), Wholeness of Body (gate 7, heals `MonkLVL` hp). Two have direct in-repo precedents, both lead-confirmed real: `class_chassis.barbarian.fast_movement` and `class_feature.acg.brawler.maneuver_training_count`/`.bull_rush_bonus`. **The meta-finding is the more valuable one**: High Jump and Wholeness of Body were deferred under the *old* standalone-grounding bar, with shipped doc comments (lead-confirmed at several lines) explicitly calling them "named-but-unproven" because they were once believed to need an Acrobatics-check total or a healing engine — the corrected bar (item 52) no longer requires either, the same stale-deferral shape already fixed once on Stunning Fist's DC. **Worth treating as a standing question for every other class's own old "not flat" deferrals**, not just Monk's — filed as task #36 (backend's lane) for the four Monk features; the broader stale-deferral sweep across other classes is not yet dispatched, worth a future pass.

65. **RESOLVED — task #43 (Hunter spellcasting scoping) landed and is fully lead-verified, including an independent script re-derivation of every headline number; ruling made on the one genuine open question (spell-level collision rule).** `hunter-spellcasting-scoping.md` answers all four brief questions with corpus-exact evidence: `SPELLLIST:2|Druid|Ranger` confirmed as a union against two shipped precedents (Skald's single-list `SPELLLIST:1|Bard`, Oracle's two-list `SPELLLIST:2|Cleric|Oracle`, both tokens lead-confirmed byte-exact in `acg_classes.lst`/`apg_classes.lst`); the full 20-level CAST/KNOWN table lead-independently transcribed directly from `acg_classes.lst`'s own `###Block: Level progression` and matched the doc exactly at every level, including the corpus's own comment explaining the KNOWN-column "+1" as the automatic Summon Nature's Ally knowns (validating the doc's leading-`0`-sentinel reasoning from the source itself, not just inferred); spontaneous/Oracle-shaped posture confirmed via `MEMORIZE:NO`; the six auto-known Summon Nature's Ally spells and their bounded I-VI range confirmed. **Lead independently re-derived the headline union/overlap numbers with a standalone script reading the two already-shipped spell-list modules directly** (not re-checking the doc's arithmetic, re-computing it fresh): union 300, overlap 85, different-level overlap 27, castable (≤6) 255, out-of-range 45 — all matched exactly, including the three spot-checked individual examples (Detect Poison Druid 0/Ranger 1, Protection from Energy Ranger 2/Druid 3, Glide Druid 2/Ranger 1).

**Ruling on the doc's own flagged open question**: no in-repo precedent resolves a same-spell-different-level conflict (Skald's single list can't collide; Oracle's second list was deferred rather than reconciled), and this is PF1's real published Hunter spell list being approximated by PCGen's list-union mechanism rather than transcribed directly, so no corpus token settles it either. **Adopt take-the-lower as the modeling choice** — it already matches the direction of most of the 27 conflicts, is the simplest defensible synthesis rule for a hybrid class inheriting access from two sources, and errs toward broader spell availability rather than narrower. This is a deliberate modeling choice, not a corpus-stated fact, and must be recorded as such in the implementation's own comment (the same honesty framing already established for Witch's familiar-store choice, item 57) — if an authoritative Hunter-specific spell list is ever sourced from the ACG book directly, this rule should be revisited against it rather than assumed permanently correct. Cleared to build.

66. **RESOLVED — INCIDENT: `e076c8ad` ("ground Paladin's DR 5/evil") also contains backend's complete, independent task #39 (Bloodrager's own DR), swept in by a same-file `git add` while both agents had uncommitted work in the shared checkout. Corrects item 64's diagnosis: the wrong test-count wasn't a self-report error, it was a real provenance mix.** Featmate staged `pilot_compute.rs` by explicit path for their own Paladin work; the file also contained backend's finished-but-uncommitted Bloodrager DR (`bloodrager_damage_reduction_amount`, `ground_bloodrager_damage_reduction`, both gate branches, the negative-value clamp, 5 dedicated tests) at that moment, and it rode along under the Paladin commit message. **Lead independently confirmed directly**: `e076c8ad`'s `pilot_compute.rs` diff is 166 added lines, of which 39 reference Bloodrager and only 1 references Paladin (backend's own doc-comment comparing the two DR shapes) — the commit's actual code content is almost entirely Bloodrager's, with Paladin's real grounding (`class_chassis.paladin.damage_reduction`) having landed in an earlier, separate part of the diff. Re-verified in an isolated worktree: 844/844 lib, both SD-24 audits 3/3 — this is item 64's same number, now correctly attributed: the discrepancy was never a remembered-number error, it was the tree genuinely containing 5 more tests than the commit message described because of whose work was actually staged. **Both agents independently found and reported this without prompting** — featmate by re-diagnosing their own "why 839 twice" question against saved raw test-run logs rather than accepting a surface explanation, backend by comparing the shipped diff against what they'd actually built. Two further self-corrections worth recording: featmate had been treating "staged only my file, explicit path" as sufficient isolation all session — it isn't, since an explicit path still stages a co-resident agent's uncommitted edits in the same file; the actual discipline is `git diff <file>` review or `git add -p` before staging, and worktree isolation (already used for Summoner Slice A) sidesteps the hazard entirely, which is why that commit contained only its own author's work. Featmate also found a second-order consequence: staging happened after their own `cargo test` run but after backend added ~5 more tests to the same file, so the commit shipped code whose test run predated part of what was actually committed — the totals not reconciling (839+3≠842) was the tell that should have triggered a re-check sooner. **Ruling: do not rewrite history.** The branch is shared and pushed, the code is correct and fully verified either way, and this repo's own git safety protocol treats history rewrites on shared branches as a last resort. Fixed via tracking instead: task #39 marked completed with a provenance note pointing at `e076c8ad`; task #42 (Bloodrager's remaining features) corrected from 7 items to 6, explicitly told not to rebuild DR. **Standing lesson for the rest of this swarm**: on a shared single checkout, `git status`/`git diff <file>` before every `git add`, not just before every `git commit` — the window where a same-file collision can occur is between "I finished my own change" and "I looked at what's actually staged," and an explicit path alone does not close it.

67. **RESOLVED — task #45 (Inquisitor scoping) landed and mostly checks out; the brief's own framing ("thin, chooser-shaped") was wrong, and scout caught a real methodology gap in item 60/63's own id-count survey along the way.** `inquisitor-remaining-features-scoping.md`: the dominant remaining item is spellcasting (219 spells per scout, fully ingested, spontaneous, own list — not a chooser, not thin), with Bane and a Sacred/Profane Judgment widening as the genuinely small remainder. **Real methodology finding, independently confirmed**: Stern Gaze is already grounded (`inquisitor_stern_gaze_intimidate_bonus`/`active_inquisitor_stern_gaze_bonus`, lead-confirmed exact at `pilot_compute.rs:10159`/`:10170`) but produces no explanation record because it feeds the computed Intimidate total directly rather than standing alone — so item 60/63's id-count roster method systematically undercounts any class whose wins landed as total-integrations, biasing against exactly the highest-value kind of work. **Widened by item 69's Skald finding to cover the inverse direction too**: a bare name-grep across classes that share a feature name (Bard's own Lore Master/Versatile Performance, Barbarian's own Rage Powers) makes an *ungrounded* Skald feature look already-covered, the opposite failure mode from Stern Gaze's undercounting. Same root cause both ways — matching on a name or id without checking what it's actually scoped to — so the durable rule isn't "id counts are a floor" specifically, it's "verify scope, not just presence," in either direction. Worth carrying forward whenever a future roster survey runs. **CORRECTION — the 219-vs-222 gap was the lead's own script bug, not scout's count. 219 is confirmed correct.** Scout re-audited independently rather than deferring to the lead's number, verified their own 219 four ways (only two files carry an Inquisitor `CLASSES:` entry at all; 220 raw occurrences dedupe to 219 unique names via exactly one base/`.MOD` pair, `Resounding Blow`, both level 5; zero names appear at more than one level; zero substring-only false matches), and could account for exactly one of the lead's three extra spells (the level-5 cell) but not the other two — reported that honestly rather than claiming outright victory. **Lead re-ran the script with per-spell output rather than just totals and found the real bug**: three `#`-prefixed lines (`#Corruption Resistance`, `#Defile Armor`, `#Commune (Greater).MOD`) were being counted as active spell records. In this corpus's own convention a leading `#` marks a disabled/commented-out duplicate — confirmed directly: the *real* active `Corruption Resistance` record (no `#`) carries `CLASSES:Antipaladin,Paladin=2`, no Inquisitor at all, while the disabled `#`-prefixed duplicate carries `CLASSES:Antipaladin=2|Inquisitor=2|Paladin=2`. Excluding `#`-prefixed lines, the lead's script produces **219, with the identical per-level breakdown as scout's** (15/38/43/44/35/24/20) — exact match. Scout's number was right; the lead's original script had a real, previously-unflagged parsing bug (not yet known to affect any of the other nine already-shipped spell lists — worth a quick sweep for `#`-prefixed false positives across those before assuming they're clean, since this is a new bug class, not a repeat of the comma-group/bracket bugs items 53/54 already fixed). Task #47 updated to build from 219 directly rather than re-deriving from scratch. Confirmed independently: Inquisitor's class line carries no `SPELLLIST` token (own list, no reuse possible, unlike Hunter), the Bane formula (`BONUS:VAR|InquisitorBanePool|InquisitorLVL`) is exact, and Sacred/Profane Judgment's four extra types route through the already-built Judgment execution path.

68. **RULING REVERSED (featmate, lead-ratified after independent verification) — item 63's option (b) for task #41 is not actually available: it would ship provably dead code, not a "scoped but unreachable" fact. Deferring #41 for real, with a properly scoped follow-on filed for the cap question.** Item 63 posed the choice as (a) widen `MAX_SUPPORTED_MONK_LEVEL` or (b) ground Perfect Self as scoped-but-currently-unreachable, citing Master Craftsman's 5-rank-prereq precedent. Featmate traced the actual dispatch path before building and found the precedent doesn't hold: every Monk feature (all four of #36's, and any Perfect Self branch) lives inside `explain_monk_level1_chassis`, which starts with `let Some(level) = supported_monk_level(input) else { return; }` — and `supported_monk_level` only returns `Some` for `(1..=MAX_SUPPORTED_MONK_LEVEL).contains(&level)`, `MAX_SUPPORTED_MONK_LEVEL = 12`. An `if level >= 20` branch placed there **can never execute under any input the dispatch admits** — not "no legal character reaches it," but the code path itself is dead, unreachable even by a test exercising the real pipeline. Master Craftsman's producer genuinely runs (tests call it and it returns the fact); what's unreachable there is a *character* satisfying its prereq, not the *code*. Categorically different, and the no-stub doctrine rules out the dead-code shape directly. **Lead independently re-verified the trace**: confirmed the early-return guard, confirmed `supported_monk_level`'s exact range check, and confirmed via the next top-level `fn` boundary (`supported_rogue_level` at line 25119) that `explain_monk_level1_chassis` spans lines 23757–25118, comfortably containing all four #36 features and where a Perfect Self branch would have to live.

**Ratified: option (b) as originally framed, defer #41 honestly** (the DR magnitude and gate are correctly verified and recorded, `DR:10/Chaotic` at level 20, plus a separate no-magnitude Outsider-type clause that stays deferred regardless — same clean-split shape as Paladin's aura) — closed as correctly-deferred-not-built rather than left open indefinitely, per this session's own standing convention that an honest "cannot be built as scoped" is a legitimate closure. **Filed the cap question as its own properly bounded task** rather than as a side effect of one DR fix, matching the Summoner Slice A/B precedent: widening `MAX_SUPPORTED_MONK_LEVEL` to 20 admits Diamond Soul (13), Quivering Palm (15), Timeless Body (17, partially present), Tongue of the Sun and Moon (17), Empty Body (19), and Perfect Self (20) — six genuinely new, currently entirely-ungrounded features — plus it makes every already-shipped Monk formula (Fast Movement's own comment scopes itself explicitly to "+40 at 12") start emitting at levels 13–20 where none has ever been verified, so the real cost is a verified pass across eight new levels, not a one-line constant change. **One correction to featmate's own count**: Abundant Step (level 12) is already inside the current `1..=12` range and is a separate pre-existing gap, not newly admitted by widening — cheap to bundle into the same pass since it's adjacent, but not part of what the cap change itself unlocks.

**Follow-up sweep, same session**: checked whether the same `#`-prefixed-disabled-record bug touched any of the nine already-shipped spell lists. Only one hit across all nine: Paladin's `Corruption Resistance` also appears in the same disabled `#Corruption Resistance` duplicate. Confirmed benign — the record's own *active* (non-`#`) version independently carries `CLASSES:Antipaladin,Paladin=2`, so Paladin's already-shipped inclusion at level 2 (`paladin_spell_list.rs:74`) is correct regardless of whether the disabled duplicate was ever consulted. No fix needed on any of the nine shipped lists. The bug only produced a wrong result for Inquisitor specifically because Inquisitor appears *exclusively* in the disabled duplicate, with no corresponding grant on the active record.

**Generalized technique (scout, recorded for reuse), from the Raging Song resolution (item 69): when a corpus token and shipped code disagree and no primary source is trustworthy in the moment, find a third feature whose corpus token and shipped code already agree and are structurally related to the disputed pair — the authoring convention that reconciles the agreeing pair usually adjudicates the disagreeing one, using only in-repo evidence.** This is what solved Skald's Raging Song (Bard's Bardic Performance, named as a sibling by Skald's own DESC text, revealed the corpus's `-2`-offset authoring convention and settled which side had the defect) without touching RAW memory at all. Caveat carried over from item 69: the disagreement can be three-way (both corpus and code wrong relative to a real primary source), so resolve every candidate against the same one reference rather than pairwise. **Also worth stating as one rule rather than two**: the fifth id prefix found in item 71 (`ability_modifier.<class>.*`) and the Stern Gaze finding (item 67) are the same phenomenon — a consumer-integration record — surfacing twice under different names. The durable form: any feature whose value feeds directly into another computed total, rather than standing as its own explanation record, is invisible to id-count and prefix-enumeration surveys alike, regardless of how many prefixes get enumerated.

69. **RESOLVED — task #48 (Skald scoping) landed with a real "shipped bug" flag, which fully resolves in the shipped code's favor via a proven in-corpus internal contradiction (same precedent class as item 50's Swashbuckler deed gate). No code fix needed; the corpus's own raw token has the defect.** Scout found Skald's shipped `skald_inspired_rage_rounds_per_day` (`3 + CHA + 2*(level-1)`, i.e. `1 + CHA + 2*level`) disagrees with the corpus's raw `BONUS:VAR|SkaldRagingSongRoundsPerDay|3+CHA+(2*SkaldLVL)` token by a uniform `-2` at every level, and deliberately declined to rule which side was right, flagging real recollection-based RAW answers as unreliable today and recommending resolution against one primary source rather than a pairwise guess.

**Lead resolved it without any RAW recollection, using a cross-check already sitting in the repo.** Skald's own corpus DESC text says explicitly "counts as the bard's bardic performance special ability for any effect that affects bardic performances" — so Bard's own Bardic Performance rounds-per-day is a structurally identical sibling record, already shipped and already lead-verified this session. Bard's corpus token is `BONUS:VAR|BardicPerformanceDuration|2+CHA+(2*BardicPerformanceLVL)` and its shipped formula is `4 + CHA + 2*(level-1)` — algebraically identical (`4+2*(L-1) ≡ 2+2*L` for all L, confirmed by direct computation at L=1,2,3,20). **The corpus's own authoring convention for this exact shape is: token-constant = DESC-prose-base minus 2, because the token multiplies by `level` directly while the prose's "+2 per level after 1st" implies `level-1`.** Applying that same convention to Skald: DESC-prose-base is 3 ("3 + his Charisma modifier" at 1st level, explicit in the DESC text), so a correctly-authored token would read `1+CHA+(2*SkaldLVL)` — not the `3+CHA+(2*SkaldLVL)` actually shipped in the corpus. **The shipped Rust code already matches the DESC prose and the sibling Bard's correct authoring pattern exactly; it is the corpus's raw `BONUS:VAR` token for Skald specifically that has the transcription defect** (missing the `-2` offset applied correctly everywhere else in this same family). Confirmed directly: shipped/DESC values at levels 1/2/3/20 are 3/5/7/41; the raw corpus token gives 5/7/9/43 (a consistent `+2`); the correctly-offset-like-Bard value would be 3/5/7/41 — matching the shipped code exactly. **Ruling: no code change. `SKALD_RAGING_SONG_BASE_ROUNDS_PER_DAY` and its formula are already correct and stay as-is** — but per item 50's own standard, this deviation-from-the-literal-token must carry an explicit code comment recording the corpus defect and this ruling (currently the comment only says "verified against the PCGen corpus DESC text," with no mention of the token disagreement or why the token was correctly not followed) — filed as the first item in task #50.

**Rest of #48 confirmed real**: five flat magnitudes remain — Well-Versed (`SkaldWellVersedBonus = 4`, exact precedent match to Bard's own identical `BARD_WELL_VERSED_BONUS = 4`), Spell Kenning (`(1+SkaldLVL)/6`), Lore Master (`min((SkaldLVL-1)/6,3)`, a proper two-argument `min`), Versatile Performance (`BONUS:ABILITYPOOL|Skald Versatile Performance|min((SkaldLVL+3)/5)` — genuinely single-argument, confirmed directly; the `(SkaldLVL+3)/5` term alone already matches the DESC's own grant schedule exactly (1 at level 2-6, 2 at 7-11, 3 at 12-16), so ground the formula as its own value rather than inventing a second cap operand, per scout's own caution), Rage Powers (count, Barbarian cross-class leverage). **Name-collision methodology finding, independently confirmed**: `grep -i skald.*lore_master\|versatile_performance\|rage_power\|well_versed` returns zero hits in `pilot_compute.rs` — none of the four are currently grounded for Skald despite Bard/Barbarian owning same-named features, confirming scout's flag that a bare name-grep would have wrongly shown Skald as already covered. **This is the inverse of item 67's Stern Gaze finding** (there, an id-count made a grounded feature invisible; here, a name-match makes ungrounded features look grounded) — both are instances of the same underlying rule, "check what a name or id is actually scoped to, not just whether it exists somewhere in the codebase." Item 67 widened to name both directions explicitly rather than just the undercounting one. Dispatched the five-feature build (plus the Raging Song comment fix) as task #50.

70. **GREENLIT — task #49's pre-build investigation found three existing Monk formulas that would ship wrong values the moment MAX_SUPPORTED_MONK_LEVEL widens, all three independently verified, plus a decoy the naive corpus reading would have walked into.** Featmate verified all 7 newly-admitted/pre-existing features fresh against `cr_abilities_class.lst` (4 with magnitudes: Abundant Step `AbundantStepCasterLVL=MonkLVL`, Diamond Soul `10+MonkLVL` SR, Quivering Palm DC `10+MonkLVL/2+WIS`/duration `MonkLVL`, Perfect Self `DR:10/Chaotic`; 3 grant-only: Timeless Body, Tongue of the Sun and Moon, Empty Body — no numeric tokens on any of the three, correctly to stay ungrounded beyond naming).

**The three real corrections, all lead-independently re-verified directly against the raw corpus:**
1. **Slow Fall — a self-caught error in featmate's own first read.** The record carries nine separate `.MOD` lines, each `BONUS:VAR|SlowFallLVL|-2|PREVARGTEQ:MonkLVL,<N>|PREVAREQ:Monk_CF_SlowFall<M>,1`. Taken literally these would zero out or invert a low-level monk's slow fall — featmate caught this because the arithmetic didn't reconcile with the known 20 ft floor value, not because the shape looked suspicious on its own. **Lead independently confirmed all nine `Monk_CF_SlowFall<N>` flags `DEFINE` to 0 in `cr_abilities_globalvar.lst` and are declared only as archetype-suppression `FACTDEF:ABILITY` toggles this repo's archetype records never set** — provably vacuous, the exact shape already established for `Bloodrager_CF_DamageReduction`. Base reduces cleanly to `floor(MonkLVL/2)*10`. **Level 20 is not the naive 100 ft extension** — a separate DESC record gated `PREVAREQ:SlowFallLVL,20` reads "fall any distance without harm," lead-confirmed verbatim. Current code is right through 13, wrong from 14 without this fix, and needs a distinct unlimited case at 20.
2. **Flurry attack count — a decoy, same class as the Weapon-Focus/Spell-Focus stacking-shape trap.** The corpus's `BONUS:VAR|FlurryAttacks|2+(FlurryLVL>=6)+(FlurryLVL>=8)+(FlurryLVL>=11)+(FlurryLVL>=15)+(FlurryLVL>=16)` looks like the obvious source to substitute in, but **lead independently confirmed it evaluates to 7 at level 20** — it's counting total attack-routine entries including BAB iteratives, not flurry-granted attacks specifically, a different quantity from what the existing RAW-sourced record (2/3/4 at 1st/8th/15th) represents. Using it would have silently shipped a wrong and much larger number. Existing semantic extended instead: 2/3/4 at 1st/8th/15th (current code wrong from 15 without the 4th-attack extension).
3. **Unarmed strike damage die** — the shipped code's own comment already admits levels 16+ (2d8 and beyond) aren't grounded; `min(5, MonkLVL/4)` caps the band index, confirmed to correctly stop the progression at 2d10 rather than continuing indefinitely. Bands 4-5 (2d6 at 12-15, 2d8 at 16-19) are the missing pieces.
4. **AC Bonus — confirmed pre-existing and already honestly documented, not newly broken.** Omits the "+1 per 4 levels past 4th" progression (understates by +4 at level 20); featmate correctly declined to silently widen a different feature's scope inside this slice and is naming it in the record instead.

**Sequencing, greenlit as proposed**: two commits, formula fixes first (provably inert at the current cap, since none of the three is reachable above level 12/15 respectively until the cap moves — a genuinely safe standalone commit), then the cap widening plus all 7 features together (never exposes a wrong value in between). Featmate also self-corrected an own earlier accounting slip (Abundant Step at 12 is inside the *current* range, not newly admitted by widening) before it calcified — already reflected in #49's own description.

**Incidental find, not in scope for this slice**: `class_chassis.monk.bonus_feat_choice`'s own explanation text still claims "this codebase does not ground those automatic grants either" about Improved Unarmed Strike and Stunning Fist, while `feat.standalone.stunning_fist.save_dc` sits in the same receipt, grounded since task #20 — stale text contradicted by its own output. One-line fix, left for whoever's nearest rather than folded into #49.

71. **RESOLVED — task #51 (round-2 roster survey) landed, single-class scoping is essentially exhausted, and the real remaining gap is a shared chooser family: Rage Powers.** Scout applied all four corrected-methodology checks from this session (enumerate prefixes fresh, check for consumer-integrated invisibility per item 67, check for name-collision overcounting per item 69, exclude `#`-disabled records per item 67's follow-up) and found a genuine fifth class-bearing prefix, `ability_modifier.<class>.*` — lead-confirmed real (`ability_modifier.skald.inspired_rage_bonus_applied` exists exactly as cited) — itself a consumer-integration category, proving the enumerate-fresh discipline keeps paying off. Every class at the thin end of the roster is already scoped or building (Inquisitor #47, Hunter #44, Skald #50, Bloodrager closed via #42). **Rage Powers wins on every axis**: 60 active records (lead-confirmed no `#`-disabled inflation), zero magnitudes grounded anywhere (only choice-recognition records exist), shared by Barbarian (CRB, `RagePowersLVL/2`) and Skald (ACG, `RagePowersLVL/3`), both grant-rate tokens lead-confirmed present — one build credits two classes and closes a marquee CRB class's (Barbarian, 17 grounded features) largest remaining hole. Dispatched as task #53, canonical-narrowing shape (one representative power grounded, 59 named and deferred), sequenced alongside #50 since Skald's own rage-power pool count is already in that task's scope.

72. **RESOLVED — task #53 (Rage Powers canonical-narrowing scoping) landed, every claim lead-verified exactly, including a subtle caution about class credit that turned out precisely right.** `rage-powers-canonical-narrowing-scoping.md` recommends Raging Climber (`BONUS:VAR|RagingClimberBonus|RagePowersLVL`, `BONUS:SKILL|Climb|RagingClimberBonus|PREVAREQ:Raging,1`) — lead-confirmed byte-exact. Wins on every criterion this session has used: simplest formula on the roster (no arithmetic, no divisor/offset transcription risk — the exact failure shape items 69's Raging Song and Sacred Weapon's own dice-count bug both had), purely passive, and lands on a computed total (Climb is one of the three tracked selected-skill modifiers). **A real selection constraint the brief didn't anticipate, lead-confirmed verbatim in Skald's own DESC**: "This cannot be a rage power that requires the creature to spend a standard action or rounds of rage to activate it. For example, the skald cannot choose terrifying howl... but can choose knockback." A pick that isn't Skald-legal would have collapsed the two-class leverage that motivated the whole task — Terrifying Howl (otherwise an attractive flat-DC candidate) is corpus-confirmed excluded by name, as are Renewed Vigor/Powerful Blow/Surprise Accuracy/Strength Surge (once-per-rage or standard-action shaped). **One correction to scout's own earlier survey, caught and precisely hedged rather than overclaimed**: `RagePowersLVL` is set by three sources, not two — Barbarian, Skald, and `Bloodrager Archetype ~ Primalist`. Lead independently confirmed the third is **archetype-only**, not base Bloodrager, matching this session's standing convention that archetype records aren't ingested/counted (the same shape as the `Monk_CF_*`/`Bloodrager_CF_*` suppression-flag family) — so the two-class credit (Barbarian `/2`, Skald `/3`, both confirmed) stands as scout scoped it, not three.

**Ruling on the doc's own open question — ground both Raging Climber and Raging Swimmer, not just one.** Raging Swimmer is confirmed an exact structural twin (`BONUS:VAR|RagingSwimmerBonus|RagePowersLVL`, `BONUS:SKILL|Swim|RagingSwimmerBonus|PREVAREQ:Raging,1`, byte-for-byte the same shape targeting Swim instead of Climb — also one of the three tracked computed skills). This deliberately deviates from the strict one-canonical-pick-per-chooser precedent (Oracle's Mystery, Hunter's Animal Focus, Cavalier's Order) — recorded explicitly rather than silently: that precedent exists to bound a chooser's *design* complexity, not to cap at exactly one entry when a second is a genuine zero-risk twin (same formula shape, same gate, same computed-total integration, differing only in which of the three tracked skills it targets). Doubles the computed-total integration value scout flagged for nearly free. Superstition (`2+RagePowersLVL/4` on saves, Skald-legality confirmed by explicit DESC naming) stays the documented fallback, not built — carries arithmetic and a narrower scope, no reason to prefer it over two zero-arithmetic twins. Dispatched as task #54, sequenced alongside/after #50 per scout's own note (Skald's own rage-power pool count is already in #50's scope).

73. **RESOLVED — task #55 (Arcanist Exploits scoping) landed, every claim lead-verified exactly, and the class-specific constraint pattern from item 72 repeats a third time.** `arcanist-exploits-canonical-narrowing-scoping.md` confirms 46 active Exploit records (lead-confirmed exact count) with a real class-specific legality/cost constraint the brief explicitly asked scout to check for rather than assume absent: **36 of 46 reference the Arcane Reservoir** (lead-confirmed via case-insensitive DESC search, exact match) — most Exploits cost a spendable resource this engine doesn't model, so "purely passive" is the exception here, not the default, same shape as Skald's rage-power activation restriction (item 72) and a third instance of "verify the class-specific constraint, don't rank on formula quality alone" this session. Exactly four Exploits carry neither a Reservoir cost nor a `PRE` gate: Metamagic Knowledge (already grounded), Familiar, Item Crafting, and Bloodline Development (deferred as a chooser-in-chooser).

**Ruling: Familiar is the canonical pick, not Item Crafting.** Lead independently confirmed `BONUS:VAR|FamiliarMasterLVL|ArcanistLVL` byte-exact and confirmed `ground_familiar_master_benefit` (`pilot_compute.rs:11793`) already exists, emitting `class_feature.familiar.master_hit_point_bonus` — genuinely namespaced `familiar.`, not `witch.`/`shaman.`, confirming it was built as shared class-agnostic machinery from the start. This is the decisive fact scout's recommendation turns on: Familiar reuses an already-shipped mechanism and lands on `durability.rs`'s computed max-HP total (the Toad canonical's own `+3 max HP`), extending the familiar thread already serving Witch and Shaman to a third class — cross-class *reuse*, not new design. Item Crafting is structurally near-identical to the already-grounded Metamagic Knowledge (same `ABILITYPOOL|...Feat|1` shape) and genuinely lower-risk to build, but grounds a bare count with no computed consumer — correctly ranked second by scout, and not built alongside Familiar the way Rage Powers' exact twins were (item 72): unlike Raging Climber/Swimmer, Familiar and Item Crafting are not twins, and this task's own honest framing (single-class, no status change, lower value than Rage Powers) doesn't justify building two Exploits when one already captures the real win. Dispatched as task #56.

74. **RESOLVED — backend's pre-build verification pass on task #54 found three real additional hazards before any code was written, then responsibly released the task back to the queue rather than half-build it on a shared checkout while low on context.** All lead-independently re-confirmed: (1) Rage Power records are keyed `KEY:Rage Power ~ <Name>` (mechanism-namespaced), not a bare `KEY:<Name>` — a naive grep would return zero hits and look like the records don't exist, the same trap the Witch 324-vs-326 spell-count saga hit; backend caught it on their own by re-checking plain text before reporting a false negative. (2) `RagePowersLVL` has a fourth setter beyond the three already known (Barbarian, Skald, Bloodrager's archetype-only Primalist): `KEY:Viking ~ Viking Rage Power`, a **Fighter archetype** — lead-confirmed the `FighterLVL` setter directly. Same exclusion reasoning applies (archetype-only, not base Fighter), so the two-class credit (Barbarian, Skald) from item 72 stands unchanged, but the full setter list is now on record so a future search doesn't rediscover it and wonder. (3) An `Unchained Rage Power ~` duplicate exists in `pathfinder_unchained`, lead-confirmed **not ingested anywhere in this repo's corpus** — a looser search pattern than the exact `KEY:Rage Power ~` prefix would match both and double-count. All three findings written directly into task #54's own description rather than left in chat, so the next agent gets a head start with no re-derivation needed. **Task #54 released back to pending/unassigned** — backend judged a shared-checkout integration build (Climb/Swim computed-total wiring, activation-gated) wasn't safe to start with limited remaining context, correctly citing the earlier #39-inside-#38 incident (item 66) as the failure shape to avoid repeating. Right call: an unstarted, fully-verified task beats a half-finished one in a shared tree.

75. **RESOLVED — task #57 (Rogue Talents scoping) landed, confirmed the class-legality-constraint pattern a fourth time, and self-corrected a real earlier undercount.** `rogue-talents-canonical-narrowing-scoping.md`: 67 active `Rogue Talent ~` records (CRB 25, APG 42, lead-confirmed exact), only 10 carry a numeric token — the thinnest of the three shared families scoped this session. **Investigator does not inherit all 67** — it carries its own explicit 40-record whitelist (`KEY:Investigator ~ Rogue Talent ~ *`), lead-confirmed exact count. **Scout caught and corrected its own earlier #45 claim of "14" on the spot** rather than let a stale figure propagate into this task — the real number is 40. Recommends Resiliency (`= RogueTalentLVL` for base Rogue), confirmed present in Investigator's whitelist, no `PRE` gate, lands on `durability.rs`'s computed temp-HP total. **One detail for the eventual builder, lead-confirmed while verifying**: Investigator's own copy of Resiliency uses `BONUS:VAR|ResiliencyHitPoints|InvestigatorLVL`, not `RogueTalentLVL` — each class credits its own level variable via its own separate record, the same pattern already established for Rage Powers (Barbarian `/2` vs Skald `/3`). Not a discrepancy, just something to get right when grounding both classes' copies.

**Scout's own honest assessment, adopted as-is: this family is genuinely lower-value than its two predecessors and not worth urgent dispatch.** Comparison table in the doc: Rage Powers (60 records/18 tokened/2 classes/lands on ×2 computed skills), Arcanist Exploits (46/21/1/computed max HP), Rogue Talents (67/10/2/computed temp HP) — most records, fewest magnitudes. Scout explicitly recommended letting #54 and #56 land first before committing to this one, rather than presenting three equally-weighted options. **Ruling: agreed — filed as task #58, left unassigned/low-priority rather than immediately dispatched**, consistent with not forcing low-marginal-value work ahead of higher-value queued items.
